Original word and original interpolation rows\n\nFix an integer m>=8. Put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_m=1-1/m,\n c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m*c_m^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nLet N=2^m, n=N-1, and h=H_m=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Every child keeps\nthese enclosing-m parameters. The first three PHYSICAL steps are\n\n h_0=A=1+a_m, h_1=B=1+beta_m=2-1/m, h_2=A.            (1)\n\nWe will use the exact rational bounds\n\n 13/10 < A < 3/2,     15/8 <= B < 2.                  (2)\n\nIndeed a_m=(m-1)/(lambda(m+1))>=7/(9lambda)>14/45>3/10,\nbecause lambda<5/2. (3)\n\nAdd the optimum sample x_star=g_star=0, f_star=0. Necessity in\nfact1252b8823bb5a290 requires Q_ij<=0 for EVERY ordered pair of\ndistinct indices in{star,0,...,n}. This requirement holds\nfor any actual smooth convex function, independently of any assertion\nabout its closest minimizer. No query or incoming terminal row is\ndeleted in this draft.\n\n## 2. Explicit global tree directions and the selected recurrence\n\nUse a Euclidean space with the following orthonormal basis:\n\n e_const, e_root, and e_(ell,k),\n 0<=ell<m, 0<=k<N/2^(ell+1).\n\nThere are N+1 basis vectors. The coordinate e_(ell,k) belongs to the\ndyadic interval[k*2^(ell+1),(k+1)*2^(ell+1)) of leaves. For a leaf\n0<=i<N define\n\n k_ell(i)=floor(i/2^(ell+1)),\n sigma_ell(i)=+1 if i mod2^(ell+1)<2^ell, and -1 otherwise.\n\nTake r>0 and nonnegative weights w_ell with\n\n w_0=3/4,    r^2+sum_(ell=1)^(m-1)w_ell=1/4.          (4)\n\nThe definite selected law uses\n\n r^2=1/(4m), w_0=3/4, w_ell=1/(4m), 1<=ell<m.        (5)\n\nAll tree levels and the common root coordinate then have positive\nweight. The more general weights in(4) will be covered at the same\ntime. Define the ACTUAL unit vectors\n\n u_i=r*e_root+\n       sum_(ell=0)^(m-1)sqrt(w_ell)*sigma_ell(i)*e_(ell,k_ell(i)). (6)\n\nEvery norm is1 by(4). These are finite coordinate vectors, not an\nunknown Gram matrix. If two leaves first split at level ell, their\ninner product is\n\n r^2+sum_(j>ell)w_j-w_ell.                            (7)\n\nThus signed correlations are allowed. For(5), siblings have inner\nproduct-1/2, while leaves splitting above them can have positive or\nzero inner product. In particular\n\n <u_0,u_1>=-1/2,\n <u_0,u_2>=<u_1,u_2>=t=1/4-2w_1,\n |t|<=1/4.                                          (8)\n\nThe directions depend on the entire enclosing tree; the weights and\nthe following dampings may be chosen with knowledge of the entire\npredetermined word. There is no online restriction.\n\nChoose ANY theta_i in[99/100,1], independently for each physical\nstep0<=i<n, and define\n\n g_0=e_const+e_root,\n alpha_i=<u_i,g_i>,\n g_(i+1)=g_i-theta_i*h_i*alpha_i*u_i.                 (9)\n\nThis is a constructive full-vector recurrence. Equivalently g_i is\nthe chronological product of the explicit matrices\nI-theta_j*h_j*u_j*u_j^T applied to e_const+e_root. No scalar covariance\nclosure or future-gradient-only rule replaces that state. In particular\nthe old coordinates remain available at every subsequent step.\n\nFor an arbitrary x_0, define EVERY x_i by(3). For any real f_0 define\nthe explicit adjacent-midpoint values\n\n f_(i+1)=f_i-h_i*||g_i||^2+\n                     (theta_i*h_i^2/2)*alpha_i^2.    (10)\n\nAll vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) have explicit coordinates. Their full\nGram matrix is V^T V, where V has precisely those vectors as columns:\nfor every coefficient vector z, z^T V^T V z=||Vz||^2>=0. This is the\nonly PSD claim, and its factor is specified. No formal gradient is\nidentified with another or removed.\n\nThe constant component satisfies <g_i,e_const>=1 for EVERY i. It\nwill allow all optimum rows and a noncore residual to be paid explicitly;\nit does not affect the cycle that rejects the law.\n\n## 3. Every adjacent row, optimum row, and noncore state can be supplied\n\nLet Delta_i=g_(i+1)-g_i=-theta_i*h_i*alpha_i*u_i. Substitution of\n(10) into the two original adjacent rows gives, separately,\n\n Q_(i,i+1)=Q_(i+1,i)\n       =-theta_i*(1-theta_i)*h_i^2*alpha_i^2/2 <=0.    (11)\n\nFor example Q_(i,i+1) equals\n-theta_i*h_i^2*alpha_i^2/2+||Delta_i||^2/2, which is(11).\nFor the reverse row use <g_(i+1),g_i>=||g_i||^2-theta_i*h_i*alpha_i^2.\nWhen theta_i<1 and alpha_i!=0 both adjacent inequalities are strict.\nConsequently failure below is not an artifact of imposing all adjacent\nequalities or of using zero optimum heights.\n\nHere is one fully explicit choice paying EVERY optimum row on the\nENTIRE original word, despite its large bridges. Define\n\n D_0=1, D_i=product_(j<i)(1+h_j),\n M=7*D_n^2, x_0=M*e_const, f_0=2*D_n^2.              (12)\n\nThese quantities are finite analytic products, never machine-evaluated\nin this round. Since ||g_0||=sqrt(2) and the operator in(9) has norm\nat most1+h_i,\n\n ||g_i||<=sqrt(2)*D_i,\n ||x_i-x_0||<=sqrt(2)*sum_(j<i)h_j*D_j\n                      =sqrt(2)*(D_i-1).             (13)\n\nThe last equality is the telescope D_(j+1)-D_j=h_j*D_j.\nAlso (10), theta_i<=1 and |alpha_i|<=||g_i|| give\n\n |f_(i+1)-f_i| <= (h_i+h_i^2/2)*||g_i||^2\n              <= (2h_i+h_i^2)*D_i^2\n               = D_(i+1)^2-D_i^2.\n\nTherefore\n\n 2D_n^2-D_i^2+1 <= f_i <=2D_n^2+D_i^2-1.             (14)\n\nThe lower optimum rows obey\n\n Q_(star,i)=||g_i||^2/2-f_i <=-1.                   (15)\n\nSince <x_0,g_i>=M and(13) holds,\n\n <x_i,g_i> >=7D_n^2-2D_i(D_i-1) >=5D_n^2.\n\nCombining this with f_i<=3D_n^2-1 and ||g_i||^2/2<=D_n^2 yields\n\n Q_(i,star)=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2\n                                                <=-D_n^2-1.    (16)\n\nIn particular every conjugate excess\n\n c_excess,i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2\n\nis at least D_n^2+1. The separate primal excess f_i-||g_i||^2/2 is\nat least1. No optimum row is saturated, and nonzero heights are\nexplicitly included in the attempted construction.\n\nLet s_i=sum_(j<i)h_j. Its noncore residual y_i=x_i-g_i satisfies\n\n <y_i,e_const>=7D_n^2-s_i-1\n             >=7D_n^2-D_n>=6.                       (17)\n\nHere 1+s_i<=D_i<=D_n follows by induction from the nonnegative\nsteps, and D_n>=1. Thus no physical query, block boundary, or return\nis a core reset y_i=0. All children retain this state. The large\nchoice(12) is only a transparent way to supply these rows; the\nobstruction below holds for EVERY x_0 and EVERY value array.\n\nFinally alpha_0=r and\n\n alpha_1=r*(1+theta_0*A/2)>0.                        (18)\n\nHence g_1!=g_0 and g_2!=g_1 inside the original physical H2. This\ndoes not reuse the old constant-H2 construction. We make no claim\nthat all later alpha_i are nonzero; the recurrence retains those\nqueries even when an increment happens to vanish.\n\n## 4. An exact value-independent three-query cycle identity\n\nWe derive the needed identity for general local data. Suppose three\nconsecutive gradients are v, v'=v+d, v''=v'+e, and the two steps\nare a,b. Their positions differ by -a*v and -b*v'. Put\n\n d=-p*a*alpha*u,  e=-q*b*beta*v_dir,\n alpha=<u,v>, beta=<v_dir,v'>,\n ||u||=||v_dir||=1, c=<u,v_dir>.\n\nHere p,q are the local dampings and v_dir denotes a direction,\ndistinct from the starting gradient v. The original cycle using\nthe first-to-third row and the two reverse adjacent rows is\n\n C=Q_(first,third)+Q_(third,second)+Q_(second,first).\n\nAll function values cancel. Its linear terms are\n\n a<d,v>+b<d+e,v'>,\n\nand its half-squared-norm terms sum to\n\n ||d||^2+||e||^2+<d,e>.\n\nRegrouping gives\n\n C=[a<d,v>+||d||^2]+[b<e,v'>+||e||^2]\n                                      +b<d,v'>+<d,e>\n  =p*a*b*alpha*[(p*a-1)*alpha+q*c*beta]\n       -p*(1-p)*a^2*alpha^2-q*(1-q)*b^2*beta^2.      (19)\n\nThis is a literal sum of original Q rows. It assumes neither the\nmidpoint values nor any optimum. It is unchanged by translating all\npositions or by their initial constant component. In particular it\ndoes not assign a fictitious smooth convex function to the recurrence.\n\n## 5. The first physical H2 gives a positive cycle\n\nRetain the general weights(4). From(8),(9), let\n\n k=1+theta_0*A/2,\n E=theta_0*A+theta_1*B*k,\n l=1-t*E.\n\nThe three relevant projections are exactly\n\n alpha_0=r, alpha_1=r*k, alpha_2=r*l.                 (20)\n\nFor alpha_2, take the u_2 inner product of\ng_2=g_0-theta_0*A*r*u_0-theta_1*B*r*k*u_1 and use(8).\n\nApply(19) to the original queries1,2,3 with a=B,b=A,p=theta_1,\nq=theta_2,c=t. Their cycle C_1 has the exact expression\n\n C_1/r^2\n  =theta_1*A*B*k*[(theta_1*B-1)*k+theta_2*t*l]\n   -theta_1*(1-theta_1)*B^2*k^2\n   -theta_2*(1-theta_2)*A^2*l^2.                    (21)\n\nThe following rational estimates hold simultaneously for every\nm>=8 and every allowed theta_0,theta_1,theta_2:\n\n 8/5 < k < 7/4,      0<E<5,      |l|<=9/4.          (22)\n\nIndeed the lower bound for k follows from\n1+(99/100)*(13/20)=3287/2000>8/5. The upper bound follows\nfrom A<3/2; then E<3/2+2*(7/4)=5. The last bound uses |t|<=1/4.\n\nWrite the bracket in(21) as\n\n J=(theta_1*B-1)*k+theta_2*t-theta_2*t^2*E.\n\nSince theta_1*B-1>=137/160 and(22) holds,\n\n J >137/100-1/4-5/16=323/400>4/5.                   (23)\n\nThus the positive term of(21) is greater than\n\n (99/100)*(13/10)*(15/8)*(8/5)*(4/5)=7722/2500.\n\nThe sum of the two terms subtracted in(21) is nonnegative and less\nthan\n\n (1/100)*[4*(7/4)^2+(3/2)^2*(9/4)^2]\n             =1513/6400 <1/4.                       (24)\n\nConsequently\n\n C_1/r^2 >7722/2500-1/4=7097/2500>14/5.             (25)\n\nFor the concrete weights(5), r^2=1/(4m), proving(A).\nFor EVERY weight allocation(4), the more general statement is\nC_1>(14/5)r^2>0. This covers independent damping choices, including\nthe strictly damped theta_i=99/100 choice at every physical step.\n\nBecause (21) is independent of ALL values, no f_i can make every\noriginal row nonpositive. At least one of rows(1,3),(3,2),(2,1)\nmust exceed (14/15)r^2; with(5), at least one exceeds7/(30m).\n\nFor the actual specified values(10), both reverse adjacent rows are\nnonpositive by(11), so the literal nonadjacent row satisfies\n\n Q_(1,3)>=C_1>(14/5)r^2,\n Q_(1,3)>7/(10m) for(5).                             (26)\n\nAdding or moving an optimum does not affect this obstruction. Neither\ndoes discarding incoming terminal rows: queries1,2,3 are all queried\nprefix indices for m>=8. A corrected function value at the final\nunqueried point cannot change it.\n\n## 6. Exact necessary interface outside the rejected damping band\n\nFor the SAME vector geometry(4) and the same recurrence with arbitrary\nfirst three dampings, equation(21) still holds with the definitions(20).\nAny proposed legal repair preserving that recurrence must satisfy\n\n theta_1*A*B*k*[(theta_1*B-1)*k+theta_2*t*l]\n <=theta_1*(1-theta_1)*B^2*k^2\n      +theta_2*(1-theta_2)*A^2*l^2. The right side displays the local slack that\nwould have to pay for the incompatible change of direction.\nSection5 proves that the complete band[99/100,1]^3 cannot pay it.\nNo favorable payoff, legal extension, or impossibility is asserted\noutside that band. In particular the block-to-block or all-word\nproblem is not replaced by asserting that an unknown target-equivalent\ninequality holds.\n\n## 7. Retuning every Haar level does not repair the undamped rule\n\nHere, and ONLY here, set theta_0=theta_1=theta_2=1 and replace(4) by\n\n 0<w_0<1, r>0, w_ell>=0,\n r^2+sum_(ell=0)^(m-1)w_ell=1.\n\nDefine tau=1-w_0 in(0,1). All other directions and the initial\ngradient remain as in(6),(9). Then\n\n c=<u_0,u_1>=2tau-1,\n t=<u_0,u_2>=<u_1,u_2>=tau-2w_1, |t|<=tau.\n\nInitial overlaps are still <u_j,g_0>=r for j=0,1,2.\nThe first cycle, on indices0,1,2, follows from(19):\n\n C_0=Q_(0,2)+Q_(2,1)+Q_(1,0)\n    =A*B*r^2*(1-c)*[A*(1+c)-1]\n    =2*A*B*r^2*(1-tau)*(2*A*tau-1).                (28)\n\nIf tau>1/(2A), every factor in the last line is positive.\nThe law is then impossible for arbitrary values.\n\nIt remains to treat 0<tau<=1/(2A), including equality. Put\n\n k=1-A*c=1+A-2A*tau>=A,\n l=1-t*(A+B*k).\n\nThe second cycle on1,2,3 becomes\n\n C_1=A*B*r^2*k*F(t),\n F(t)=(B-1)*k+t-(A+B*k)*t^2.                        (29)\n\nUsing |t|<=tau gives\n\n F(t)>=(B-1)*k-tau-(A+B*k)*tau^2\n      =k*(B-1-B*tau^2)-tau-A*tau^2.\n\nThe coefficient B-1-B*tau^2 is positive: it is at least\n7/8-2/[4*(13/10)^2]=7/8-50/169>0. Hence k>=A and\ntau<=1/(2A) imply\n\n F(t)>=A*(B-1)-tau-A*(B+1)*tau^2\n      >=A*(B-1)-(B+3)/(4A)\n       >(13/10)*(7/8)-5/[4*(13/10)]\n        =183/1040>0.                               (30)\n\nEvery factor of C_1 in(29) is now positive. This completes the\nundamped obstruction for ALL those weights. No fact is claimed, and no additional round is requested or assumed.\n\nAccepted correctness inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 (normalization and the original\nall-pair interpolation convention), b98bb807d514e80d (K and the original\nbenchmark recurrences), 1fca3e5dd555cb4a (the complete ray reference), and\ndfde2fa326c908bf (the specified full complex reference). All new ingredients\nare derived jointly here. Earlier ellipsoid and reference drafts are awareness,\nnot premises. This is an H_m-family investigation, not the all-word target.\n\n## 1. One growing body and the original physical word\n\nFix an integer m>=128. All vector norms and inner products are Euclidean. Put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n    b_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n=2^m-1, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))=H_m,\n    S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m, M_j=1+S_j, M=M_m, T=2S+1,\n    alpha=a/(c-2), Cax=c^m, D=Cax^2.\n\nEvery child uses this same enclosing m. Let e_0,...,e_m be the standard basis\nof R^(m+1), and define the diagonal positive definite matrix and FILLED body\n\n    d_l=c^(2l), 0<=l<=m, A=diag(d_0,...,d_m),\n    E={g: <g,A g><=1},\n    F(x)=max_(g in E) {<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                 (1)\n\nThe squared semiaxis ratio is D=c^(2m), and dimension is m+1. The selected\nfull law allows EVERY x_0!=0 in this space. Orthogonal rotations or a common\nrescaling produce equivalent instances but are unnecessary here. The actual\nGD is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad F(x_i), retaining all n original steps.\nWe write f_i=F(x_i) and P=2f_n/||x_0||^2. Section2 proves that the unique\nminimizer is0, so this denominator is the true squared initial distance.\nMultiplying F by any L>0 and using h_i/L preserves the trajectory and P.\n\nThe elementary mass identities used below are\n\n    M_j=alpha c^j+(1-alpha)2^j,\n    0<alpha<1, M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j,\n    a c^j<=(c-2)M_j.                                  (2)\n\nIndeed S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j gives the formula by induction. Also\nc(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta, so alpha<1. The other inequalities follow\nfrom c>2 and the two nonnegative terms in the mass formula. We shall use\n\n    12/5<lambda<c<5/2, 3/8<a<1/2, Cax>16, S>1248.       (3)\n\nFor the upper bound on c, sqrt(2)<17/12 gives\nc<(29/12)(129/128)<5/2. Then a=beta/c>(127/128)(2/5)>3/8.\nThe lower bound lambda>12/5 follows from sqrt(2)>7/5. The remaining bounds\nfollow from m>=128 and S>=2^m-1.\n\n## 2. Exact static geometry, smoothness, minimum, and every original row\n\nCompleting the square in (1) identifies its unique maximizing vector g(x)\nwith the Euclidean projection of x onto E. Existence is by compactness;\nuniqueness follows by strict convexity of squared distance on a convex set.\nFor x in E set t(x)=0 and g(x)=x. For x outside E let t(x)>0 be the unique root\n\n    sum_(l=0)^m d_l x_l^2/(1+t(x)d_l)^2=1,\n    g_l(x)=x_l/(1+t(x)d_l).                            (4)\n\nThe sum on the left is continuous, strictly decreasing for nonzero x,\nlarger than1 at zero, and tends to0. For the resulting g, <g,A g>=1 and\n\n    <x-g,z-g>=t(<g,A z>-1)<=0, z in E,\n\nby Cauchy--Schwarz in the A inner product. Expanding squared distances\nproves that this g is the projection, including coordinates equal to zero.\nThere are no negative multipliers: projection onto the boundary SURFACE\nwould be a different problem for interior points.\n\nFor two projection pairs (x,g),(y,p), adding their normal inequalities gives\n||g-p||^2<=<g-p,x-y>. Thus g is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maxima defining\nF(x+u) and F(x) shows that\n0<=F(x+u)-F(x)-<g(x),u><=||u||^2. Therefore F is differentiable with gradient\ng, is1-smooth, and is convex as a maximum of affine functions. Integrating\nthe Lipschitz gradient along a segment also gives the sharper upper estimate\nF(x+u)<=F(x)+<g(x),u>+||u||^2/2, used in Section5.\n\nThe feasible vector0 gives F>=0 and F(0)=0. For x!=0, a sufficiently small\nepsilon in (0,2) has epsilon*x in E and yields the strictly positive test\nvalue epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2. Hence argmin F={0}.\n\nWrite t_i=t(x_i), including the optimum sample x_*=g_*=t_*=f_*=0. Exactly,\n\n    x_i=(I+t_i A)g_i,\n    f_i=||g_i||^2/2+t_i,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =t_j(<g_j,A g_i>-<g_j,A g_j>)<=0.              (5)\n\nFor t_j=0 the expression is zero. Otherwise <g_j,A g_j>=1 and its other\ninner product is at most1. Thus EVERY ordered adjacent, remote, and optimum\nrow of the original interpolation system holds, without selecting a subset.\nFor two exterior samples, Q_ij=-(t_j/2)||g_i-g_j||_A^2, where\n||v||_A^2=<v,A v>. These formulas apply to the same single F throughout.\n\nThe query is genuinely coupled. At an exterior point put\nJ(x)=sum_l d_l^2 x_l^2/(1+t d_l)^3>0. Implicit differentiation of (4) gives\n\n    partial t/partial x_l=[d_l x_l/(1+t d_l)^2]/J(x),\n    partial g_k/partial x_l\n      =-[d_k x_k/(1+t d_k)^2][d_l x_l/(1+t d_l)^2]/J(x), k!=l. (6)\n\nThis is nonzero when both coordinates are active. The denominators have\nthe SAME changing multiplier. In the core the Hessian is I. Outside, the\nidentity <g,A g>=1 forces (A g)^T Dg=0, so its Hessian is singular. The\nfunction is not a fixed quadratic, nor a sum of independent scalar modes.\n\n## 3. An exact global state law with no loss of core amplitude\n\nAt each query retain ALL the weights\n\n    w_i,l=d_l g_i,l^2, sigma_i=sum_l w_i,l,\n    G_i=sum_l w_i,l/d_l=||g_i||^2,\n    R_i^2=sum_l w_i,l(1+t_i d_l)^2/d_l=||x_i||^2.       (7)\n\nThey satisfy w_i,l>=0, sigma_i<=1, t_i>=0 and t_i(1-sigma_i)=0. In the\ncore t_i=0, but sigma_i and G_i retain both A radius and Euclidean radius;\nthey are NOT replaced by zero or by a fresh boundary point.\n\nFor a physical step h_i define\n\n    u_i,l=w_i,l(1-h_i+t_i d_l)^2, U_i=sum_l u_i,l.      (8)\n\nIf U_i<=1, then t_(i+1)=0 and w_(i+1),l=u_i,l. If U_i>1, the next multiplier\nis the unique positive solution of\n\n    sum_l u_i,l/(1+t_(i+1)d_l)^2=1,\n    w_(i+1),l=u_i,l/(1+t_(i+1)d_l)^2.                  (9)\n\nIndeed sqrt(d_l)x_(i+1),l=(1-h_i+t_i d_l)sqrt(d_l)g_i,l, and applying (4)\nto this ACTUAL next position gives (8)--(9). Signs, if a full vector\ntranscript is wanted, are carried by multiplication by\nsign(1-h_i+t_i d_l); a zero coordinate stays zero. Formula (9) is therefore\nan exact iteration, not an independent-coordinate relaxation.\n\nAn initial state is any t_0>=0 and w_0,l>=0 satisfying the stated\ncomplementarity, except the all-zero state. Choosing signs and setting\nx_0,l=(1+t_0 d_l)sign_l sqrt(w_0,l/d_l) realizes exactly that state by (4).\nConversely every initial vector has such a state. The global payoff is\n\n    P=[G_n+2t_n]/R_0^2.                               (10)\n\nThe exact radius payment over ANY original prefix of length k is\n\n    R_k^2=R_0^2-sum_(i<k) h_i(2-h_i)G_i\n                   -2sum_(i<k) h_i t_i.              (11)\n\nExpand ||x_i-h_i g_i||^2 and use <x_i,g_i>=G_i+t_i. Thus all normal work\nand every negative coefficient from h_i>2 remain in (11). Dropping either\nthe weights at a core entry or those negative coefficients changes the\nproblem. Equations (7)--(11) are global for arbitrary initial vectors and\narbitrary eccentricity; no kappa<=2 drainage estimate was invoked.\n\n## 4. A same-word counterexample to closing the state with two scalars\n\nThe pair (normal multiplier, Euclidean radius) does NOT determine even\nthe first transition of the selected law. Take the SAME E and F, and\n\n    X_0=(1+1/Cax)e_0, Y_0=(1+1/Cax)e_m.                (12)\n\nBoth have t_0=1/Cax, radius1+1/Cax, and sigma_0=1. Their first original\nstep b_0=1+a gives\n\n    X_1=(1/Cax-a)e_0 in E,  t(X_1)=0,\n    Y_1=(1-a/Cax)e_m outside E,\n    t(Y_1)=(Cax-1-a)/D>0.                             (13)\n\nThe distinction is their weights, concentrated respectively at l=0 and\nl=m. It arises at the actual first query, not at an unrealized block state.\n\nBoth entire H_m trajectories can be given exactly. Let q_j be the product\nof all positive step excesses b_l-1 in H_j. Induction from\nq_(j+1)=q_j^2 a c^j gives\n\n    q_j=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).                            (14)\n\nEvery nonempty prefix product in H_j is at most a: a prefix in the left\ncopy has this bound inductively; the product through the bridge is\nq_j a c^j=a beta^(2^j-1)<=a; and continuing into the right copy multiplies\nit by a prefix product at most1. Consequently each prefix product of the\ntail obtained by deleting the FIRST physical step is at most1. Since X_1\nis in E, every remaining X query stays in E, and\n\n    P_X=(q_m/a)^2(a-1/Cax)^2/(1+1/Cax)^2.               (15)\n\nFor Y, (2) gives S<Cax-1. Hence all proposed positions\nY_i=(Cax+1-sum_(k<i)h_k)e_m/Cax are strictly exterior, have projection\ne_m/Cax, and satisfy the actual recursion. Thus\n\n    P_Y=[1+2(Cax-S)]/(Cax+1)^2.                        (16)\n\nThey have the same initial pair in (12), but different entire trajectories.\nThis does not refute an H_m-specific payoff bound. It refutes the proposed\nscalar-state closure, even if sigma=1 is also recorded initially. The\ncounterexample explains why the full weights in Section3 are load-bearing.\n\n## 5. A fully coupled calibration with a quantitative true-radius gap\n\nDefine the explicit finite amplification bound\n\n    J_m=product_(j=0)^(m-1) max(1,a c^j)^(2^(m-1-j)).   (17)\n\nIt is a symbolic product, not a computed trajectory or an evaluation.\nConsider the ENTIRE following tube of initial vectors for the same body:\n\n    x_0=(T/Cax)e_m+z, <z,e_m>=0,\n    ||z||<=1/(4Cax J_m).                              (18)\n\nIn particular z=(sum_(l=0)^(m-1)e_l)/(4Cax J_m sqrt(m)) has every coordinate\nnonzero. The body still has all m+1 scales. We prove, uniformly over (18),\n\n    1/[T(1+1/(16J_m^2 T^2))] <= P\n          <= (1/T)(1+9/(16T)),\n    R_0^2=T^2/D+||z||^2,\n    T/D <= 2F(x_n) <= (T+9/16)/D.                     (19)\n\nThis is a weak calibration family, not a restriction imposed on the full\nlaw from Section1. Its tube width can be extremely small. That limitation\nis essential to its obstruction and cannot be omitted in a future citation.\n\nProof. For z=0, every query is exterior and\n\n    x_i^b=(T-P_i)e_m/Cax, P_i=sum_(k<i)h_k,\n    g_i^b=e_m/Cax, t_i^b=(2S-P_i)/D,\n    2F(x_n^b)=T/D.                                   (20)\n\nFor (18), |g_i,m|<=1/Cax always. Summing actual steps implies\nx_i,m>=(T-P_i)/Cax>1/Cax for every i<=n. Thus every actual query and the\nterminal point is exterior as well. This validates the regime globally.\n\nThe map U_h(x)=x-h P_E(x) has Lipschitz constant at most max(1,h-1) for\nh>=0. To prove this, set delta=x-y and v=P_E(x)-P_E(y). Firm\nnonexpansiveness gives <delta,v>>=||v||^2, so\n||delta-hv||^2<=||delta||^2+h(h-2)||v||^2. If h<=2 this is at most\n||delta||^2. If h>=2 use ||v||<=||delta|| to obtain (h-1)^2||delta||^2.\nAll these factors are at least1, so each physical prefix satisfies\n\n    ||x_i-x_i^b||<=J_m||z||<=1/(4Cax).                (21)\n\nAt the terminal point the feasible gradient e_m/Cax in (1) and\nx_n,m>=x_n,m^b show F(x_n)>=F(x_n^b). Smoothness from Section2 gives\n\n    F(x_n)<=F(x_n^b)+(1/Cax)||x_n-x_n^b||\n                          +||x_n-x_n^b||^2/2\n           <=F(x_n^b)+9/(32D).\n\nOrthogonality in (18) gives the exact initial radius and (19).\n\nThe displayed dense choice actually changes gradient direction. From (4),\nt_0<=sqrt(sum_l x_0,l^2/d_l)<=T/D+||z||<9/(4Cax)<a. Its l=0 coordinate\ntherefore changes sign at the first step, because 1-b_0+t_0=t_0-a<0.\nIts l=m coordinate stays positive by the preceding exterior proof. Both\ncoordinates of g_0 are positive, whereas g_1 has these different signs.\nThe two gradients are not parallel, and (6) is nonzero initially. The\ncalibration is an actual nonlinear coupled trajectory, despite being\nquantitatively close to the axial Huber trajectory.\n\n## 6. Exact local departure law: the missing feedback is a positive term\n\nThe tube obstruction does not prove that a larger transverse displacement\nfails. Here is an exact second variation at its axial center, retaining\nthe nonlinear feedback instead of freezing its effect on the slow axis.\nFor each transverse l<m define\n\n    r_l=d_l/D, v_i,l=1/[1+r_l(2S-P_i)],\n    p_0,l=1, p_(i+1),l=(1-h_i v_i,l)p_i,l,\n    V_l=v_n,l p_n,l^2+r_l sum_(i<n) h_i v_i,l^2 p_i,l^2. (22)\n\nAll P_i and h_i are the ORIGINAL chronological data. These formulas are\nnot the fixed-curvature polynomial modes of an independent quadratic.\nFor fixed m and arbitrary transverse z tending to0, the actual objective is\n\n    2F(x_n)=T/D+sum_(l<m) V_l z_l^2+O_m(||z||^4),\n    P=1/T+(D/T^2)sum_(l<m)(V_l-1/T)z_l^2+O_m(||z||^4). (23)\n\nThe constants implicit in O_m can depend on m; no uniform nonlinear\nconclusion is inferred from this expansion.\n\nProof. Every baseline query has t_i^b>0. The derivative in t in (4) is\nnonzero there, so the usual implicit differentiation proof (or its local\npower series) makes the projection analytic in a neighborhood of each\nof the finitely many baseline queries. Compose these finitely many maps.\nReflection symmetry in each transverse coordinate makes its own output\ncoordinate odd in z_l and all other output coordinates even in z_l. Thus\n\n    x_i,l=p_i,l z_l+O_m(||z||^3),\n    g_i,l=v_i,l p_i,l z_l+O_m(||z||^3).\n\nThe boundary equation, with the positive last gradient coordinate, gives\n\n    g_i,m=(1/Cax)sqrt(1-sum_(l<m)d_l g_i,l^2)\n          =1/Cax-(1/(2Cax))sum_(l<m)d_l v_i,l^2 p_i,l^2 z_l^2\n                       +O_m(||z||^4).\n\nHence\n\n    x_n,m-x_n,m^b=(1/(2Cax))sum_(l<m)\n          [d_l sum_(i<n) h_i v_i,l^2 p_i,l^2]z_l^2+O_m(||z||^4).\n\nAt a baseline point the Hessian\nof F has transverse diagonal entries v_i,l and last entry0, as follows\nfrom (4); all mixed entries there are zero. Taylor expansion at the\nterminal baseline therefore gives (22)--(23). In particular the sum in\nV_l is the actual second-order DELAY in the last coordinate. Omitting it\nwould compute a different dynamical system.\n\nIf V_l>1/T for any l, sufficiently small nonzero displacement in that\ncoordinate beats the axial Huber value at this fixed m. If all V_l<1/T,\nthe axis is a strict local maximum for that fixed m. Neither observation\nsupplies a benchmark-scale gain. A growing fast range and the nearest slow\ntransverse axis can be screened analytically; the intermediate range and finite displacements\nare not settled.\n\n### 6.1 Fast-axis local obstruction, including a growing number of axes\n\nFor every l<m with\n\n    d_l <= Cax/[8(5/4)^m],                             (24)\n\none has V_l<1/T. This set contains l=0 and grows with m.\n\nIndeed 2S r_l<=2d_l/Cax<1, so 1/2<v_i,l<=1. Since h_i>=1+a>4/3,\nboth signs give |1-h_i v_i,l|<=h_i-1. Thus every |p_i,l| is bounded by\nthe corresponding product of positive unit-curvature excesses.\nLet E_j be sum h_i times the square of that prefix product over H_j.\nBy (14) and concatenation,\n\n    E_(j+1)=[1+(q_j a c^j)^2]E_j+b_j q_j^2\n           <=(5/4)E_j+(3/2)(5/12)^j, E_0=0.\n\nThe inequality uses q_j a c^j<=a<1/2, q_j<=c^(-j), and c>12/5.\nSumming the geometric upper bound gives\nE_m<2(5/4)^m. Also p_n,l^2<=q_m^2<=1/D. Consequently\n\n    V_l <= r_l E_m+1/D\n         <1/(4Cax)+1/Cax^2<1/(2Cax)<1/T.\n\nThe last inequality follows from T=2M-1<2Cax. Condition (24) contains\nall l with 2l log(c)<=m log(4c/5)-log(8), so its size tends to infinity.\n\n### 6.2 The slow transverse neighbor is also a strict local loss\n\nFor l=m-1, again V_l<1/T. Put r=c^(-2), v_i=v_i,m-1 and p_i=p_i,m-1.\nWe have 1/6<r<1/5. A slightly sharper rational c bound is useful:\nsqrt(2)<99/70 implies c<21801/8960<73/30. If b=b_(m-1), (2) yields\n\n    S/b >= c/(c-2)-[c/(c-2)+1]/b > 28/5-7/b > 559/100,\n\nbecause c/(c-2)>73/13>28/5, c/(c-2)+1<7, and b>700 by (3).\nEvery physical step has h_i<=b, and 2S-P_i>=S+h_i. Therefore\n\n    0<h_i v_i<=c^2 h_i/(S+h_i)<=c^2 b/(S+b)<9/10.    (25)\n\nFor the last strict bound it suffices that\n(5329/900)/(659/100)<9/10, equivalently 5,329,000<5,337,900.\n\nThe actual linked denominators satisfy v_(i+1)=v_i/(1-rh_i v_i). Direct\nsubtraction gives\n\n    v_(i+1)p_(i+1)^2-v_i p_i^2\n      =-h_i v_i^2 p_i^2 [2-r-h_i v_i]/[1-rh_i v_i]\n      <=-(45/41)h_i v_i^2 p_i^2.                     (26)\n\nThe bracket decreases with h_i v_i: its derivative is\n-(1-r)^2/(1-rh_i v_i)^2. At h_i v_i=9/10 the bracket is\n(11/10-r)/(1-9r/10), decreasing in r, so it is at least45/41 for r<=1/5.\nTelescoping (26) and using (22) gives\n\n    V_(m-1) <= (41r/45)v_0+(1-41r/45)v_n p_n^2\n             <41/(90S)+6 exp(-499/100)/S.             (27)\n\nFor the last term, (25) ensures positive factors and\np_n^2<=exp(-2sum h_i v_i)<=exp[-2S/(1+2rS)]<=exp(-499/100), since\nS>1248 and r<1/5. Also v_n<1/(rS)<6/S.\n\nFor a rational bound, the first seven Taylor terms of exp(5/3) sum to\n2,774,305/524,880>21/4. Moreover exp(1/100)<100/99 by its series. Hence\nexp(499/100)>99(21/4)^3/100=916839/6400>143. Substitution into (27) gives\n\n    V_(m-1) < [41/90+6/143]/S\n             =6403/(12870S)<499/(1000S)<=1/(2S+1).\n\nThe last inequality uses S>1248. This proves the claimed local loss.\nIt does not sign all other V_l and does not control nonlocal initial states.\n\n## 7. Comparison with the complete K and ray references on this word\n\nHere K_n(h) has its COMPLETE accepted definition: the maximum of\n1/(1+2sum h_i) and all (h_i-1)_+^2/[(1+P_i)^2(1+2T_i)], where\nP_i=sum_(k<i)h_k and T_i=sum_(k>i)h_k. We reproduce directly that\n\n    K_n(H_m)=F_ray_n(H_m)=1/T.                         (28)\n\nThis reference calculation is recalled from shared awareness but its\ncomplete argument is given here; it is not an unverified predecessor.\n\nFor a fixed level step b_j, let p range over the preceding masses at its\noccurrences. The reciprocal cut denominator is\n(1+p)^2[1+2(S-b_j-p)]. Its derivative changes sign at most once, from\npositive to negative. Its minimum over the interval from first to last\noccurrence is therefore at an endpoint. For the first occurrence, p=S_j\nand the following mass is at least S_(m-1). Write r_c=c-2 and N=M_(m-1).\nUsing a c^j<=r_c M_j bounds this cut by r_c^2/(2N-1). For the last\noccurrence the preceding mass is at least S_(m-1), the following mass\nequals S_j, and its cut is at most\nr_c^2 M_j^2/[N^2(2M_j-1)]<=r_c^2/(2N-1). The last inequality follows\nby differentiating x^2/(2x-1) on x>=1 and using M_j<=N. Finally\n\n    r_c^2(2M-1)<=(5N-1)/4<=2N-1,\n\nby c<5/2, r_c<1/2, M<=cN and N>=1. Thus every cut is at most1/T.\n\nFor completeness, the accepted ray reference maximizes over ALL ordered\ncheckpoint subsets. If the steps are split as\n(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k), put s_i=sum block_i. For k>=1\nits nonterminal magnitudes and offsets are\nm_0=1/(1+s_0), O_0=0,\nm_(i+1)=((b_i-1)m_i-O_i)/(1+s_(i+1)),\nO_(i+1)=O_i+s_i m_i, i<k-1. This is the original flow.\n\nFor completeness, direct multiplication of the original child T,L,D for\nword(X,Y) and array [[0,2,0],[2(1+X),0,u],[0,v,0]] yields\nM_child=w0(g0-g1)^2+w1(g1-g2)^2, with\nw0=2-X^2=2(1-t)(1+t),\nw1=((X+Y)/Y)[2-Y^2+(2-Y)X]=2(p/b)(1-t)(1+lambda t).\nThe diagonal is (w0,w0+w1,w1), off-diagonal entries -w0,-w1,0;\nthese are obtained from M_00=2-X^2,M_12=(v(Y-1)-u)/2,\nM_22=-(X+Y)(X+Y+2)+v and the full flow/null identities.\n\nHere is also the parent coefficient audit. Let P=2,Z=5 and\nE=X Q_(3,P)+Y Q_(4,P)+Q_(Z,P)+d Q_(P,Z)+X Q_(3,Z)+Y Q_(4,Z).\nIts value part is 2X f3+2Y f4+(d-C)fP+(2-C-d)fZ;\nits gradient part is X(1-X)||g3||^2+Y(1-Y)||g4||^2+\n[(C-d)/2-d^2]||gP||^2+(C+d)||gZ||^2/2.\nAll mixed products cancel by substituting the actual GD positions.\nSubtracting the two child energies from the parent and using\nc Delta=k d^2-C^2 gives\n\n V_parent=V_left+k V_right-2c E+c Delta(||gZ||^2-||gP||^2).\n\nThe displayed array embeds the two child arrays, adds2c E and adds\neta to both (P,Z),(Z,P). With z=gP-gZ,J=X(g3-gZ)+Y(g4-gZ), direct\nsubstitution gives Q_(P,Z)+Q_(Z,P)=-d||z||^2-(C+d)<z,gZ>-<z,J>.\nThe full parent remainder is therefore\n\n M=w0||g0-g1||^2+w1||g1-g2||^2\n     +k[w0||g3-g4||^2+w1||g4-g5||^2]\n     +tau[(C-d)||z||^2-2<z,J>].\n\nThis identity includes every original gradient and mixed entry. To prove\nPSD, write U=g3-g4,V=g4-g5, R=X^2/w0+(X+Y)^2/w1,\nq=tau/(C-d), and sigma=k-qR. Then\nR=(1-t)^(-1)t^2[1/(1+t)+lambda/(1+lambda t)]. The bracketed\nexpression multiplied by t^2 is increasing for t>=0 by differentiation,\nso at t=9/10 it is at most lambda/2. Hence\n\n (C-d)^2 sigma\n  =(3C+d)(C-d)-(2a C-delta)t^2[1/(1+t)+lambda/(1+lambda t)]\n  >(3/2)C^2-a lambda C >(15/4)C-(7/2)C=C/4>0.\n\nThe exact full sum of squares is\n\n w0||g0-g1||^2+w1||g1-g2||^2+sigma(w0||U||^2+w1||V||^2)\n +q||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U-X sqrt(w1/w0)V||^2\n +tau(C-d)||z-J/(C-d)||^2.\n\nIndeed R(w0||U||^2+w1||V||^2)-||XU+(X+Y)V||^2 is exactly the\nfourth squared norm before its q factor. All displayed weights are\npositive. Its kernel is precisely the common-gradient line, proving\npositive definiteness of the leading principal block. This reproduces\nthe fixed start's original signs, flow, and full PSD jointly from the\naccepted primitive identity. The raw tool emitted Unset::norep and a graphics\nmessage outside its exported kernel message list; both are preserved.\nSubsequent source uses independent atomic symbols instead of indexed\nexpressions as decision variables to avoid unnecessary localization\nmessages; the polynomial equations and method are unchanged.\n\n\n---\n\n# C112 bounded joint-variable pilot: no credible seed\n\nThe ONE round authorized by TASK.md and guidance81b4e3cbbe96d65a is\ncomplete. Their raw encoded responses are also saved.\n\nKey files in this public directory:\n\n* model_and_plan.md: complete joint derivations and the fixed-start audit.\n* calibration.wl, calibration_metadata.json, calibration_raw.json,\n  calibration_result.json, calibration_outcome.json.\n* warm5.wl, warm5_metadata.json, warm5_raw.json, warm5_exported_text.txt,\n  warm5_sdp_readback.json, warm5_start_vector.wl, warm5_outcome.json,\n  warm5_selected_full_readback_from_joint5.json.\n* joint5.wl, joint5_metadata.json, joint5_raw.json, joint5_result.json.gz,\n  joint5_result.json, joint5_outcome.json.\n* warm6.wl, warm6_metadata.json, warm6_raw.json, warm6_result.json.gz,\n  warm6_result.json, warm6_start_vector.wl, warm6_outcome.json.\n* joint6.wl, joint6_metadata.json, joint6_raw.json, joint6_result.json.gz,\n  joint6_result.json, joint6_outcome.json.\n* ledger.py and manifest.json; the authoritative budget JSON is one directory\n  above, under the exact name in TASK.md.\n\nGuidance0fdd0f1dbfd2b8f1 was read before choosing/reserving warm5. No claim that it was read earlier is\nmade. Current TASK remains the one-round allocation.\n\n## What the bounded outcome supports\n\nThis pilot supplies no actual sufficient-capacity certificate and no seed\nproof. {"id": "38da1067bcd1a0c8", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T00:13:38.951232+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max51 COMPLETE joint UNVERIFIED Gibbs pilot: three explicit actual static-function geometries with attained minima, true radii and full physical GD. The growing tree-simplex function has sharp Ltree=(1+w0)/2, exact radius and nonlinear coupled Gibbs dynamics, but useful terminal entropy and ALL four final benchmark comparisons remain BLOCKED.", "evidence": "# Actual finite Gibbs functions: three analytical screens\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, round51,\nunder TASK.md and master_guidance a935cdbe3d2539bd. This is the ONE\nallocated round after completed50/attempted51, with completed ceiling51\nand attempted ceiling52. No fact_submit or formal verification is\nauthorized or performed. There is no self-renewal.\n\nAll new mathematics below is a joint candidate argument, not a fact.\nNo unverified earlier finding is a mathematical premise. No host\nmathematics, numerical evaluation, solver, DP, machine algebra, trajectory\nreplay, consult, installation, or native subagent was used.\n\n## Verdict and scope\n\nThree explicit finite feature geometries were screened.\n\n1. A centered three-feature simplex gives an actual nonquadratic Gibbs\n   function, a finite unique minimizer, sharp smoothness, a paid initial\n   radius, and actual noncollinear gradients. Its selected concentrated\n   family is strictly below the SAME-word Huber reference. Its original\n   H_m benchmark ratio tends to Z_1/(2 exp(1))<1. This regime is closed.\n2. A coupled log-cosh chain gives another actual finite Gibbs function,\n   an attained minimum, exact radius, and exact finite propagation under\n   arbitrary physical steps. Its untouched-coordinate lower certificate\n   is at most O(n^-2). Only that certificate route is too weak. An upper\n   bound on the ACTUAL terminal error of the chain is NOT proved.\n3. A growing tree simplex gives a legal static function on every original\n   H_m, with sharp global smoothness, unique minimum, exact initial radius,\n   every physical query, and a nonlinear coupled probability recurrence.\n   Its target payoff is BLOCKED at an explicitly priced terminal entropy\n   inequality. It is not a prescribed-label transcript or a feature-fit\n   oracle. Neither successful concentration nor the desired payoff is\n   asserted. Two proposed simplifications of its dynamics fail exactly.\n\nThe original every-n/every-h exact-profile target remains OPEN. Even a\nsuccessful H_m entropy bound would be component progress, not the required\nuniform bridge. The new tree does not repair the invalid transcript in\n88f3db7b0a2bb506 by assigning new values to its old vectors: here values,\ngradients and queries all come from a specified finite static function.\n\n## 1. Original word, normalization, and accepted reference scopes\n\nFix m>=8 and put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_i h_i, M=1+S.\n\nEvery child uses these SAME enclosing-m parameters. The first physical\nstep satisfies 1<h_0=1+a<3/2. Counting occurrences of each level gives\n\n M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)\n   =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta*c^m,\n zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in (0,1).                         (1)\n\nIn particular M<c^m and M/c^m tends to1, since zeta tends to1 and\n(2/c)^m tends to0. Also c^m/lambda^m tends to exp(1).\n\nFor any base function Phi with a proved positive gradient Lipschitz\nconstant Lbase and minimum Phi_star, use the actual project function\n\n f=(L/Lbase)(Phi-Phi_star),\n x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Lbase) grad Phi(x_i),\n V=2(Phi(x_n)-Phi_star)/(Lbase*R_init^2),              (2)\n\nwhere R_init is the distance to the WHOLE minimizer set. Thus f has\nL-Lipschitz gradient and uses exactly the original steps h_i/L. The\nquantity V is a single actual-instance score in the W=2R convention.\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d supplies the complete functional\n\n K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),\n   max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/\n       [(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2(1+2sum_(i>j)h_i)]},\n\nand the original ALL-split recurrence C_0=B_0=1,\n\n C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3).                 (3)\n\nAccepted 1fca3e5dd555cb4a defines F_ray_n as the maximum over ALL feasible\nchronological alternating-ray checkpoint choices, with its original\nretained offsets, areas and terminal optimization. In particular\n\n Huber(h):=1/(2M-1) <= K_n(h) <= F_ray_n(h).          (4)\n\nAccepted dfde2fa326c908bf defines Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t*z),\nxi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z*Poly_i(z), with a zero optimum sample. Its FULL\ndomain D_h imposes\n\n (Re(z)-|z|^2)*Energy_C >= 2*|Im(z)*Area_C|\n\non EVERY directed simple cycle C of {star,0,...,n}, where\nEnergy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2 and\nArea_C=sum_(j->i in C)Im(conj(xi_j)*xi_i). Define the comparison\n\n Cfull(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z),                   (5)\n\nwhere Gap_h is the maximal legal terminal score for those specified\npoints and gradients in the accepted fact. This is the full unaugmented\nchannel, not its universal lens and not a short-cycle screen.\n\nOne lower bound on this COMPLETE channel will suffice for several\ncomparisons. Set theta=1/(2M). It is real in (0,1), hence in D_h. The\nactual scalar quadratic theta*x^2/2 gives\n\n Gap_h(theta)>=theta*Poly_n(theta)^2>1/(8M).         (6)\n\nIndeed each theta*h_i is in [0,1/2), and multiplying the inequalities\n(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v gives Poly_n(theta)>=1-theta*S>1/2.\nThis uses the maximality of Gap_h, not a false identification of Gap_h\nwith a quadratic value.\n\nAccepted 04219554e41d9750 supplies the ORIGINAL ray limit and enclosure\n\n B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1,\n 0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000,             (7)\n\nwhere B_1=4 follows directly from (3). The proof's Section7 and the\nfull stated enclosure were read. No seed existence or seed consequence\nfrom that fact is used. No new external theorem is imported.\n\nThe original problem asks whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where\nW_n^*=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h). Its affirmative lower bound\nrequires one N_epsilon working for EVERY word at EVERY n>=N_epsilon.\nNeither the diagnostic family (1) nor any result here provides that.\n\n## 2. Direct finite-Gibbs calculations and all original rows\n\nFor an explicitly specified finite list of vectors v_s, positive weights\nb_s, and temperature tau>0, put\n\n Phi_tau(x)=tau*log sum_s b_s*exp(<v_s,x>/tau),\n p_s(x)=b_s*exp(<v_s,x>/tau)/sum_t b_t*exp(<v_t,x>/tau),\n mu(x)=sum_s p_s(x)*v_s.\n\nDifferentiation of a finite sum gives, on the whole Euclidean space,\n\n grad Phi_tau=mu,\n Hess Phi_tau=(1/tau)[sum_s p_s v_s v_s^T-mu mu^T].  (8)\n\nThe quadratic form is a variance, so Phi_tau is convex. If the feature\ndiameter is Dfeat, its variance in any unit direction is at most\nDfeat^2/4. To prove this, for a random scalar T in [b,d], the nonnegative\nquantity (T-b)(d-T) gives\nVar(T)<=(d-E T)(E T-b)<=(d-b)^2/4.\nConsequently Lbase=Dfeat^2/(4tau) is valid globally. One may also use\nan explicitly proved sharper covariance bound in a particular geometry.\n\nThe EXACT probability update at the physical step is\n\n p_(i+1),s = p_i,s * exp[-h_i <v_s,mu_i>/(Lbase*tau)]\n             / sum_t p_i,t*exp[-h_i <v_t,mu_i>/(Lbase*tau)]. (9)\n\nThis equation follows from the actual vector update; no label is chosen\nafter a step. Every weight remains strictly positive at every finite\nquery. All functions and recurrences in this manuscript are finite even\nwhen their feature counts are large; none is evaluated by a host job.\n\nAttainment must be checked separately. In the centered uniform examples\nbelow, sum_s v_s=0 and the features span their stated ambient space.\nJensen's inequality gives Phi_1(x)>=0=Phi_1(0). Equality requires all\n<v_s,x> equal; their mean is zero, so they all vanish and span implies\nx=0. This proves the actual unique minimum, not an infimum at infinity.\nThe chain's attained minimum is computed explicitly in Section4.\n\nTemperature is not a free normalization improvement. Choose x_star to\nbe a closest attained minimizer to the chosen initial point, and let\npi_s=p_s(x_star), z=(x-x_star)/tau and\nLbase=Ccov/tau, for a fixed proved covariance bound Ccov. Then\n\n Phi_tau(x)-Phi_tau(x_star)=tau*log sum_s pi_s exp(<v_s,z>),\n z_(i+1)=z_i-(h_i/Ccov)sum_s p_i,s v_s,\n V=2 log(sum_s pi_s exp(<v_s,z_n>))/(Ccov*||z_0||^2). (10)\n\nHere sum_s pi_s v_s=0. The temperature cancels simultaneously from the\nphysical recurrence and the paid score at a fixed scaled initial state.\nChanging that state, the features, weights, or dimension can still matter;\n(10) does not exclude those choices or holding x_0 fixed while changing tau.\n\nAll original directed and optimum interpolation inequalities follow\nfrom the actual function and its stated smoothness, as follows. For any\nconvex differentiable Lbase-smooth Phi and any x, define\nk(u)=Phi(u)-<grad Phi(x),u>. It is minimized at x. Integrating its\nLipschitz gradient gives\nk(y-grad k(y)/Lbase)<=k(y)-||grad k(y)||^2/(2Lbase).\nComparison with k(x) proves\n\n Phi(y)>=Phi(x)+<grad Phi(x),y-x>\n                  +||grad Phi(y)-grad Phi(x)||^2/(2Lbase). (11)\n\nWith F_i=(Phi(x_i)-Phi_star)/Lbase and g_i=grad Phi(x_i)/Lbase,\n(11) is exactly\n\n Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0\n\nfor EVERY ordered pair of distinct physical queries, including BOTH\nterminal orientations and EVERY optimum pair. This agrees with accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290. No local-to-global inference about prescribed jets is\nbeing used. Zero steps simply repeat an actual query.\n\n## 3. Candidate one: a genuine simplex Gibbs plateau, but below Huber\n\nUse the two-dimensional Euclidean subspace E_A={x in R^3:sum_j x_j=0}.\nLet u=(1,1,1)/3, v_j=e_j-u, and\n\n Phi_A(x)=log[(exp(x_1)+exp(x_2)+exp(x_3))/3].\n\nThese are three explicit uniform affine features on E_A. The minimum is\nuniquely zero at0, and grad Phi_A=p-u. The feature diameter is sqrt(2),\nso L_A=1/2 is valid. It is sharp: logits can make p tend to(1/2,1/2,0),\nand the covariance in direction (1,-1,0)/sqrt(2) tends to1/2.\n\nFor ANY nonnegative word of total S>=4, specify BEFORE iteration\n\n x_0=4S*v_1+(0,1,-1),\n R_A^2=||x_0||^2=32S^2/3+2,\n x_(i+1)=x_i-2h_i*(p_i-u).                          (12)\n\nThe transverse vector is orthogonal to v_1, so the displayed radius is\nthe exact closest-minimizer distance. Initially x_1-x_2=4S-1 and\nx_1-x_3=4S+1. At a step either difference decreases by at most 2h_i.\nHence at EVERY physical query, including the terminal,\n\n x_i,1-x_i,j>=2S-1 for j=2,3,\n 1-p_i,1 <= eps_A:=2 exp(1-2S).                     (13)\n\nThe exact first-coordinate recurrence gives\n\n x_n,1=4S/3+2sum_i h_i(1-p_i,1).\n\nWriting Phi_A(x_n) as its largest logit plus a remainder, (13) yields\n\n 4S/3-log3 <= Phi_A(x_n)\n <=4S/3-log3+(2S+1)eps_A.                           (14)\n\nFor S>=4, (2S+1)eps_A is decreasing in S and is at most18 exp(-7).\nSince exp(1)>2, this is less than9/64<1/6. Since log3>1, (14) gives\n\n 0<Phi_A(x_n)<4S/3-5/6,\n V_A=4Phi_A(x_n)/(32S^2/3+2)<1/(2S+1).             (15)\n\nFor the final strict comparison, multiply the upper bound's numerator\n16S/3-10/3 by 2S+1. The result is\n32S^2/3-4S/3-10/3, strictly below32S^2/3+2.\nThe elementary inequality exp(1)<3, used for log3>1, follows by bounding\nthe exponential series tail: k!>=2^(k-1) for k>=2, strictly for k>=3.\nThe lower bound in (14), with log3<2, proves positivity for S>=4.\n\nThis actual trajectory is not an exactly constant-gradient ray. On the\noriginal H_m, put t=2h_0 in(2,3), or use the weaker t<4. Initially\np_1>p_2>p_3, eps_A<1/64, and every later p_1>1-eps_A. Under (9), p_1\nstrictly decreases at this first step because exp(-t p_1) is strictly\nsmaller than the other two update factors. Meanwhile p_2-p_3 strictly\nincreases. To check the latter, the derivative of q(z)=z exp(-t z) on\n[p_3,p_2] is at least exp(-t eps_A)(1-t eps_A)>7/8, using\nexp(-v)>=1-v. The probability normalizer is at most\nexp(-2(1-eps_A))+eps_A<1/4+1/64<7/8;\nexp(3/2)>4 follows from its first four series terms. The mean value\ntheorem now proves (p'_2-p'_3)>(p_2-p_3). Thus the positive first\ncoordinate of p-u decreases while its positive transverse difference\nincreases. The first two gradients cannot be collinear.\n\nEquations (14) also give 2S*V_A ->1 as S tends to infinity, uniformly\nover the chronological word at a fixed total S. The family is a real\nGibbs realization of Huber-order performance, but (15) is a decisive\nearly screen. Do not polish it into a claimed improvement.\n\nThe COMPLETE same-word comparisons are\n\n 0<V_A<Huber(h)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h),\n V_A/Cfull(h)<8M/(2M-1)<5  for S>=4.                (16)\n\nThe second inequality compares with the full domain (5), using (6).\nIt is only a factor bound; it asserts NEITHER domination by nor strict\nimprovement over the full complex channel. No equality K=Huber or\nF_ray=Huber from an unverified historical draft is imported.\n\nFor the original H_m, (1), (7), and (14) prove\n\n B_n V_A -> Z_1/(2 exp(1))\n <=41209/[20000 exp(1)]<41209/50000<1.              (17)\n\nHere exp(1)>5/2 follows from its first four series terms. This is a\nconstant-gap failure of THIS explicit family on the diagnostic, not\nan upper bound on W_n(H_m) or on other simplex initial conditions.\n\n## 4. Candidate two: coupled finite sign features and a weak support bound\n\nLet a_chain>0 and let D=2(n+1). There are D-1 variable coordinates.\nFix the boundary values x_0=a_chain and x_D=0 only in this section, and\ndefine on the variables x_1,...,x_(D-1)\n\n Phi_B(x)=sum_(j=0)^(D-1) log cosh(x_j-x_(j+1)).     (18)\n\nThe boundary indices are spatial, not time indices. This is EXACTLY a\nfinite Gibbs function, not a sum of quadratic surrogates:\n\n Phi_B(x)=log sum_(sigma in {-1,1}^D) 2^(-D)\n       exp[a_chain*sigma_0\n               +sum_(j=1)^(D-1)(sigma_j-sigma_(j-1))*x_j]. (19)\n\nEquation (19) explicitly specifies every feature and positive weight.\nNo enumeration is needed to define this finite sum. Its Hessian is\nthe path difference matrix transposed, times a diagonal matrix of\nsech^2 edge differences, times the difference matrix. For any variation\nz with z_0=z_D=0,\n\n sum_(j=0)^(D-1)(z_j-z_(j+1))^2<=4sum_(j=1)^(D-1)z_j^2.\n\nThus the actual function is globally convex and L_B=4 is a valid\nsmoothness constant. We do not claim this bound is sharp. Since the\nedge differences sum to a_chain, strict convexity of log cosh and\nJensen give the UNIQUE attained minimum\n\n x_star,j=a_chain*(1-j/D),\n Phi_B,star=D log cosh(a_chain/D).                   (20)\n\nStart the variable vector at zero. Its true squared distance is\n\n R_B^2=a_chain^2*(D-1)(2D-1)/(6D).                  (21)\n\nThe finite sum of squares in (21) follows from\nsum_(j=1)^(D-1)(D-j)^2=(D-1)D(2D-1)/6; the last identity is proved\nby induction using the difference of the two cubic expressions.\n\nAt each physical time i the update is exactly\n\n x_(i+1),j=x_i,j+(h_i/4)\n     [tanh(x_i,j-1-x_i,j)-tanh(x_i,j-x_i,j+1)].      (22)\n\nThe fixed boundary values are retained at every time. Induction on i\nshows x_i,j=0 for all j>i: if j>i+1 both adjacent differences in (22)\nare zero, including the right boundary. This holds for all finite\nnonnegative steps, however large, and keeps all queries.\n\nPut r_hor=n+1. At the terminal, x_n,j=0 for j>=r_hor. The first\nr_hor edge differences sum to a_chain; all subsequent differences\nvanish. Jensen gives the actual LOWER bound\n\n V_B >= Aprop := [r_hor log cosh(a_chain/r_hor)\n                   -D log cosh(a_chain/D)]/(2 R_B^2)>0.   (23)\n\nThe numerator is positive because k log cosh(a_chain/k) is strictly\ndecreasing in positive k: with u=a_chain/k, its derivative is\nlog cosh(u)-u tanh(u)<0, as the latter expression has derivative\n-u sech^2(u)<0 and is zero at u=0.\n\nBut the CERTIFICATE Aprop itself is too small. The inequality\nlog cosh(u)<=u^2/2 follows by integrating tanh(u)<=u for u>=0 and\nusing evenness. Dropping the positive subtracted minimum in (23),\n\n 0<Aprop<=3D/[2r_hor(D-1)(2D-1)]<=3/(2r_hor^2).    (24)\n\nThe final bound uses D-1>=r_hor and D/(2D-1)<=1. This is an upper\nbound on a proved lower guarantee; it is NOT an upper bound on V_B.\nNo assertion that (23) is attained by the physical GD trajectory is made.\n\nThis certificate failure is not repaired by making the chain's valid\nsmoothness constant sharp. When D>=3, at the zero variable vector an\ninternal edge has zero difference and Hessian contribution\n(e_1-e_2)(e_1-e_2)^T. All other edge contributions are positive\nsemidefinite, so every valid global smoothness constant is at least2.\nUsing ANY such constant instead of4 preserves finite propagation and\nthe same constrained value in (23), and its normalized support guarantee\nis at most2*Aprop. Thus all the vanishing comparisons below persist,\nwith at most a factor2. The original H_m domain has D>=3.\n\nOn original H_m, r_hor=N, so the COMPLETE references obey\n\n Aprop/Huber <=3(c/4)^m,\n Aprop/K_n <=3(c/4)^m,\n Aprop/F_ray_n <=3(c/4)^m,\n Aprop/Cfull <12(c/4)^m,\n B_n Aprop <(15/2)(3/4)^m ->0.                      (25)\n\nThe first and fourth estimates use M<c^m and (6); the last uses (3).\nFor m>=8, c< (9/8)(5/2)=45/16<4, and c tends to lambda<4, so\nall four reference ratios vanish. The scope is the untouched-coordinate\ncertificate only. The coupled chain itself is not excluded: a large\nphysical step could create an actual error much greater than (23).\nNo computational or qualitative claim about that actual error is used\nto discard the weak certificate route and move to the third geometry.\n\n## 5. Candidate three: an explicit weighted tree simplex\n\nKeep the original enclosing m and c of Section1. To avoid collision with\nthe accepted denominator-ray symbols Z_b, define\n\n r_tree=2/c in(0,1),\n Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)r_tree^j=(1-r_tree^m)/(1-r_tree),\n w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom, 0<=j<m.                        (26)\n\nThere is one orthonormal coordinate e_v for EVERY binary word v of\nlength j<m, including the empty root. The dimension is N-1. For a leaf\ns=(s_1,...,s_m) in {0,1}^m define the explicit feature\n\n a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) sqrt(w_j)*(-1)^(s_(j+1))*e_(s_1...s_j),\n Phi_T(x)=log[N^(-1)sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)].             (27)\n\nThe library has N features and all weights are 1/N. It may know m and\nthe entire original word. No online or dimension restriction is imposed\non competing adversaries. The tree is a choice of geometry, not a\nlossless representation theorem for all smooth convex functions.\n\nThe features sum to zero and each has squared norm1. Their coordinate\nrows are orthogonal: a row at depth j has N/2^j nonzero entries of\nmagnitude sqrt(w_j); a proper descendant row cancels separately inside\nits support, and disjoint rows have zero product. Thus the feature\nmatrix has full row rank N-1. The centered Jensen argument from Section2\ngives the unique attained minimum\n\n                x_star=0, Phi_T,star=0.             (28)\n\nThe SAME argument also proves affine independence of the N vertices.\nThe only linear dependence among their columns is the all-ones vector;\na dependence with coefficients summing to zero must consequently vanish.\nEquivalently, the map x -> (<a_s,x>)_s is onto the zero-mean logit space.\n\nIf two leaves first diverge at depth j, their squared feature distance is\n\n             4w_j+2sum_(ell>j)w_ell.               (29)\n\nTheir common prefix cancels, the divergent coordinate contributes4w_j,\nand their later paths use orthogonal coordinates. The quarter-distances\nw_j+(1/2)sum_(ell>j)w_ell strictly decrease with j, since the difference\nbetween consecutive ones is w_j-w_(j+1)/2>0. Therefore (8) gives the\nglobally valid constant\n\n Ltree=(1+w_0)/2.                                  (30)\n\nIt is SHARP. Choose a pair diverging at the root and prescribe equal\nlogits for that pair and logits smaller by T for all other leaves,\nthen subtract the common mean. The onto map just proved supplies an\nactual point for every finite T. As T tends to infinity, its Gibbs\ndistribution tends to half mass on each chosen vertex. Covariance in\nthe difference direction tends to their squared distance divided by4,\nwhich equals (30). This is a proof of the global supremum, not a\nnumeric eigenvalue calculation or an implicit feature-fitting step.\n\nThe full, actual, persistent vector and probability updates are\n\n x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Ltree)sum_s p_i,s*a_s,\n p_(i+1),s=p_i,s exp[-(h_i/Ltree)sum_t K_st*p_i,t]\n          /sum_u p_i,u exp[-(h_i/Ltree)sum_t K_ut*p_i,t],\n K_st=<a_s,a_t>.                                    (31)\n\nAll entries of K are fixed by the explicit coordinates (27); K is not\nan unknown Gram matrix. If s,t diverge at j, then\nK_st=sum_(ell<j)w_ell-w_j; K_ss=1. Formula (31) defines every physical\nquery, not just level checkpoints. Values are Phi_T(x_i), and (11)\nproves all original rows and optimum rows without separately fitting them.\n\n## 6. A specified initial state, exact radius, and actual nonlinear reduction\n\nLet t0=log2. Specify the complete initial point by\n\n x_0,(0^j)=t0/sqrt(w_j) for 0<=j<m,\n x_0,v=0 for every other internal node v.           (32)\n\nHere 0^j is the all-left prefix of length j, including the root at j=0.\nThe full unique minimum is still (28), hence the TRUE squared radius is\n\n R_T^2=t0^2 sum_(j=0)^(m-1)1/w_j\n      =t0^2*r_tree^(1-m)*Zgeom^2.                  (33)\n\nThe second equality follows from the two finite geometric sums in (26).\nThere is no discarded orthogonal component or convenient surrogate minimum.\n\nLeaves can be grouped by the depth k of their first right turn,\n0<=k<m, and by the all-left leaf k=m. Their uniform reference masses are\n\n q_k=2^(-(k+1)) for k<m, q_m=2^(-m).\n\nAll coordinates outside the leftmost spine remain ZERO at every physical\nquery. Indeed, if they are zero, logits are constant on each first-exit\ngroup. Every non-spine node lies inside one such group, and its two\nhalves have equal counts and logits, so its mean feature coordinate is\nzero. The next update preserves zero, completing induction.\n\nDefine the ACTUAL spine fields y_i,j=sqrt(w_j)*x_i,(0^j), exit logits\n\n ell_i,k=sum_(j<k)y_i,j-y_i,k for k<m,\n ell_i,m=sum_(j<m)y_i,j,\n P_i,k=q_k exp(ell_i,k)/sum_(u=0)^m q_u exp(ell_i,u).\n\nThe exact reduction of (31) is\n\n y_(i+1),j=y_i,j-(h_i*w_j/Ltree)\n                    [sum_(k>j)P_i,k-P_i,j],\n y_0,j=t0.                                         (34)\n\nThese m equations are NONLINEAR and COUPLED through the same probabilities.\nThey are not independent scalar quadratics or a prescribed label policy.\nFor example, at the initial state the Hessian covariance between the root\nand any deeper spine coordinate j is\n\n Cov(a_root,a_(0^j))\n   =2sqrt(w_0)*P_0,0*E[a_(0^j)]>0.                 (35)\n\nThe equality holds because the root feature is +sqrt(w_0) wherever\nthe deeper coordinate is nonzero. Positivity follows from the explicit\ninitial means computed next. The changing distribution in (34), rather\nthan a fixed Hessian, determines later interactions.\n\nInitially ell_0,k=(k-1)t0 for k<m and ell_0,m=m*t0. Thus\n\n P_0,k=1/(m+4) for k<m, P_0,m=4/(m+4),\n Phi_T(x_0)=log[(m+4)/4],\n E[a_(0^j)]=sqrt(w_j)*(m+2-j)/(m+4).                (36)\n\nThe weights and the starting point are fully specified before the\ntrajectory. Formula (34) is an exact analytic recurrence, not evidence\nthat its final value has a desired size.\n\nSince sum_k q_k ell_i,k=0, there is an exact objective identity\n\n D_i:=Phi_T(x_i)=sum_(k=0)^m q_k log(q_k/P_i,k),\n V_T=2D_n/(Ltree*R_T^2).                            (37)\n\nThus D_i is relative entropy with the stated orientation: the uniform\nleaf reference is the FIRST argument, not the current Gibbs law.\nAll probabilities are positive. Its nonnegativity also follows directly\nfrom Jensen. This entropy is the actual value, not an added potential.\n\n## 7. The priced missing estimate and exact same-word comparison interface\n\nEquations (26), (30), (33), (34), and (37) specify a globally legal family\non every original H_m. They do NOT yet provide a useful terminal payoff.\nThe diagnostic target B_n V_T>=1 is EXACTLY\n\n D_n>=eta_m,\n eta_m=Ltree*t0^2*r_tree^(1-m)*Zgeom^2/(2B_n).       (38)\n\nIt is a concrete inequality for the entirely specified recurrence (34),\nnot an unknown initial state, unknown feature library, or Gram oracle.\nLet r_inf=2/lambda, Zgeom_inf=1/(1-r_inf), and\nLtree_inf=(2-r_inf)/2. From (1) and (7),\n\n N*eta_m -> eta_inf\n   =exp(1)*Ltree_inf*t0^2*r_inf/\n                     [2 Z_1 (1-r_inf)^2] in(0,infinity).  (39)\n\nIndeed N*r_tree^(-m)=c^m. Thus the needed entropy has order1/N after\nthe exact radius has been paid. The assertion D_n>=eta_m is UNPROVED.\nNo claim of even a uniform constant fraction of (38) is made.\n\nOne rigorous terminal diagnostic follows by conditioning the relative\nentropy (37) on the last two leaves. They have reference masses1/N each,\nand their current log probability ratio is2y_n,m-1. Splitting the\nrelative-entropy sum into this pair and its complement gives a binary\ngroup divergence plus two nonnegative conditional divergences. Therefore\n\n D_n >= (2/N) log cosh(y_n,m-1).                    (40)\n\nFor detail, the conditional pair law has probabilities proportional to\nexp(y_n,m-1),exp(-y_n,m-1). Its divergence from the equal pair law is\nlog cosh(y_n,m-1). The pair's reference weight is2/N. Nonnegativity\nof the other divergences is Jensen applied to log. This is a LOWER\nbound and need not capture the whole final entropy.\n\nA SUFFICIENT deep-coordinate condition for (38) is consequently\n\n log cosh(y_n,m-1) >= N*eta_m/2.                    (41)\n\nThe exact accumulated coordinate update is\n\n y_n,m-1=t0-(w_(m-1)/Ltree)\n                  sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i*(P_i,m-P_i,m-1).   (42)\n\nNeither (41) nor a useful bound on the signed sum in (42) has been proved.\nThe trivial bound by S gives a coordinate uncertainty of order N and\ndoes not supply a terminal lower bound. It also permits exact cancellation.\n\nFor clarity, the complete SAME-word comparisons remain the following\nexplicit, unresolved scalar thresholds, with NO replacement of a full\nreference by a selected path or a lens:\n\n V_T>=K_n(h)       iff D_n>=Ltree*R_T^2*K_n(h)/2,\n V_T>=F_ray_n(h)   iff D_n>=Ltree*R_T^2*F_ray_n(h)/2,\n V_T>=Cfull(h)     iff D_n>=Ltree*R_T^2*Cfull(h)/2,\n B_n V_T>=1       iff D_n>=eta_m.                   (43)\n\nThe equality (37) is a complete payoff formula, but (43) is NOT a\ncompleted performance comparison. All four terminal comparisons for\nthis third candidate are BLOCKED. In particular, legality alone and a\nlarge initial entropy do not prove a useful hard instance.\n\n## 8. Two direct attempts, their exact failures, and counterexample checks\n\nAttempt A: propagate the equal initial spine fields or compare all leaf\nmasses to a constant multiple of uniform. Both proposed shortcuts fail\nalready in the explicit initial/first-step data. Equation (34) gives\n\n y_1,j=t0-[h_0*w_j/Ltree]*(m+2-j)/(m+4).\n\nThus\n\n y_1,0-y_1,1\n =-h_0*[w_0*(m+2)-w_1*(m+1)]/[Ltree*(m+4)]<0.      (44)\n\nThe equal-field ansatz is not invariant. Also P_0,m=4/(m+4), so\nP_0,m/q_m=4N/(m+4) is unbounded. A proposed absolute, all-time\nnear-uniform probability cap is false for this very initial condition.\nThese observations do not exclude a later mixing statement with a paid\ntransient. No such statement is assumed in (38).\n\nAttempt B: use the repeated original word H_j as an independent child\nevolution. Split the feature tree at the root and write\n\n Phi_T=log[(exp(y_root)*exp(Phi_left)\n                  +exp(-y_root)*exp(Phi_right))/2].\n\nEach child Phi is the log-partition function of its explicitly inherited\ndescendant features on its own Euclidean coordinates. If P_left is the\ncurrent subtree mass, then\n\n grad_left Phi_T=P_left*grad Phi_left,\n x_left' =x_left-(h/Ltree)*P_left*grad Phi_left.       (45)\n\nThus, with the child's own sharp constant\n\n L_left=w_1+(1/2)sum_(j>1)w_j < Ltree,\n\nits actual normalized step is\n\n               h_eff=h*P_left*L_left/Ltree.         (46)\n\nThis is a state-dependent number, not the original physical h. Initially\nP_left=(m+3)/(m+4), and it strictly decreases after the FIRST actual step.\nTo prove the strict change, the right child starts at0 and has zero\ngradient, so stays at0. The root field strictly decreases because\nP_left>1/2. The left child makes a positive step of size\nh_0 P_left/Ltree <2/L_left, since h_0<2 and L_left<Ltree.\nThe smooth descent inequality shows its value strictly decreases, as\nits gradient is nonzero by (36). Its odds\nP_left/(1-P_left)=exp(2y_root+Phi_left-Phi_right)\ntherefore strictly decrease. This proves that even the first two\neffective clock factors in (46) differ.\n\nConsequently a recursion that inserts fresh copies of the ORIGINAL\nchild schedule silently changes the physical steps. An adaptive clock\nbound might still be useful, but it must account for the full history\nof subtree masses, coupling to ancestors, all returns and the exact\nradius (33). No such bound has been proved in this round.\n\nAn additional counterexample check rules out a global entropy-monotonicity\nshortcut for (34)'s function. In this SAME Phi_T, put only the root field\nequal to s and all descendants zero. The function is log cosh(s), and a\nstep h gives s'=s-h*(w_0/Ltree)*tanh(s). Whenever\nh*w_0/Ltree>2, sufficiently small positive s has s'<-s and therefore\nPhi_T(x')>Phi_T(x). Such a level occurs in H_m for all sufficiently\nlarge m, since its maximum h grows without bound whereas w_0/Ltree\ntends to a positive constant. This point is a test state of the same\nfunction, not a claim that the trajectory (32) visits it. It refutes\nglobal monotonicity at those steps, not a trajectory-specific inequality.\nThis scalar slice is ONLY a counterexample test; it is not substituted\nfor the actual coupled construction (32)--(37).\n\nAfter these two genuine attempts and the counterexample checks, the\nunresolved estimate is (38), or an alternative actual terminal bound\nstrong enough to decide (43). Its scope is the specified growing tree\nGibbs family and original enclosing-m word. Mark it BLOCKED, not a\nroutine missing lemma, and not a theorem-level impossibility claim.\n\n## 9. Literature, scope guards, and remaining work\n\nThe standard background is Boyd and Vandenberghe, Convex Optimization,\nthe log-sum-exp Hessian discussion on printed page74 and the entropy\nconjugacy discussion on printed page93:\nhttps://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf .\nThe exact primary-source passages and the page74 Cauchy--Schwarz proof\nwere read through web extraction. All actual claims used here were\nderived in this joint manuscript; no theorem from that text is imported\nas a Danus fact.\n\nRetrieval audit: Matlas was queried for the finite-Gibbs covariance and\nattainment mechanism. {"id": "16cd03fc572cda7b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T00:14:23.042183+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max51 subgoal A, screened weak (UNVERIFIED): the explicit 2D simplex-Gibbs plateau is legal with sharp smoothness1/2, true R_A^2=32S^2/3+2 and noncollinear first gradients on original H_m, but for every word with S>=4 its actual score is strictly below Huber. Original-B ratio tends Z_1/(2 exp(1))<1; the full complex comparison is only a factor<5, not claimed domination.", "evidence": "This is a subgoal extraction of canonical joint38da1067bcd1a0c8, not a verified predecessor.\n\n## 1. Original word, normalization, and accepted reference scopes\n\nFix m>=8 and put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_i h_i, M=1+S.\n\nEvery child uses these SAME enclosing-m parameters. The first physical\nstep satisfies 1<h_0=1+a<3/2. Counting occurrences of each level gives\n\n M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)\n   =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta*c^m,\n zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in (0,1).                         (1)\n\nIn particular M<c^m and M/c^m tends to1, since zeta tends to1 and\n(2/c)^m tends to0. Also c^m/lambda^m tends to exp(1).\n\nFor any base function Phi with a proved positive gradient Lipschitz\nconstant Lbase and minimum Phi_star, use the actual project function\n\n f=(L/Lbase)(Phi-Phi_star),\n x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Lbase) grad Phi(x_i),\n V=2(Phi(x_n)-Phi_star)/(Lbase*R_init^2),              (2)\n\nwhere R_init is the distance to the WHOLE minimizer set. Thus f has\nL-Lipschitz gradient and uses exactly the original steps h_i/L. The\nquantity V is a single actual-instance score in the W=2R convention.\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d supplies the complete functional\n\n K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),\n   max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/\n       [(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2(1+2sum_(i>j)h_i)]},\n\nand the original ALL-split recurrence C_0=B_0=1,\n\n C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3).                 (3)\n\nAccepted 1fca3e5dd555cb4a defines F_ray_n as the maximum over ALL feasible\nchronological alternating-ray checkpoint choices, with its original\nretained offsets, areas and terminal optimization. In particular\n\n Huber(h):=1/(2M-1) <= K_n(h) <= F_ray_n(h).          (4)\n\nAccepted dfde2fa326c908bf defines Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t*z),\nxi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z*Poly_i(z), with a zero optimum sample. Its FULL\ndomain D_h imposes\n\n (Re(z)-|z|^2)*Energy_C >= 2*|Im(z)*Area_C|\n\non EVERY directed simple cycle C of {star,0,...,n}, where\nEnergy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2 and\nArea_C=sum_(j->i in C)Im(conj(xi_j)*xi_i). Define the comparison\n\n Cfull(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z),                   (5)\n\nwhere Gap_h is the maximal legal terminal score for those specified\npoints and gradients in the accepted fact. This is the full unaugmented\nchannel, not its universal lens and not a short-cycle screen.\n\nOne lower bound on this COMPLETE channel will suffice for several\ncomparisons. Set theta=1/(2M). It is real in (0,1), hence in D_h. The\nactual scalar quadratic theta*x^2/2 gives\n\n Gap_h(theta)>=theta*Poly_n(theta)^2>1/(8M).         (6)\n\nIndeed each theta*h_i is in [0,1/2), and multiplying the inequalities\n(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v gives Poly_n(theta)>=1-theta*S>1/2.\nThis uses the maximality of Gap_h, not a false identification of Gap_h\nwith a quadratic value.\n\nAccepted 04219554e41d9750 supplies the ORIGINAL ray limit and enclosure\n\n B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1,\n 0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000,             (7)\n\nwhere B_1=4 follows directly from (3). The proof's Section7 and the\nfull stated enclosure were read. No seed existence or seed consequence\nfrom that fact is used. No new external theorem is imported.\n\nThe original problem asks whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where\nW_n^*=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h). Its affirmative lower bound\nrequires one N_epsilon working for EVERY word at EVERY n>=N_epsilon.\nNeither the diagnostic family (1) nor any result here provides that.\n\n## 2. Direct finite-Gibbs calculations and all original rows\n\nFor an explicitly specified finite list of vectors v_s, positive weights\nb_s, and temperature tau>0, put\n\n Phi_tau(x)=tau*log sum_s b_s*exp(<v_s,x>/tau),\n p_s(x)=b_s*exp(<v_s,x>/tau)/sum_t b_t*exp(<v_t,x>/tau),\n mu(x)=sum_s p_s(x)*v_s.\n\nDifferentiation of a finite sum gives, on the whole Euclidean space,\n\n grad Phi_tau=mu,\n Hess Phi_tau=(1/tau)[sum_s p_s v_s v_s^T-mu mu^T].  (8)\n\nThe quadratic form is a variance, so Phi_tau is convex. If the feature\ndiameter is Dfeat, its variance in any unit direction is at most\nDfeat^2/4. To prove this, for a random scalar T in [b,d], the nonnegative\nquantity (T-b)(d-T) gives\nVar(T)<=(d-E T)(E T-b)<=(d-b)^2/4.\nConsequently Lbase=Dfeat^2/(4tau) is valid globally. One may also use\nan explicitly proved sharper covariance bound in a particular geometry.\n\nThe EXACT probability update at the physical step is\n\n p_(i+1),s = p_i,s * exp[-h_i <v_s,mu_i>/(Lbase*tau)]\n             / sum_t p_i,t*exp[-h_i <v_t,mu_i>/(Lbase*tau)]. (9)\n\nThis equation follows from the actual vector update; no label is chosen\nafter a step. Every weight remains strictly positive at every finite\nquery. All functions and recurrences in this manuscript are finite even\nwhen their feature counts are large; none is evaluated by a host job.\n\nAttainment must be checked separately. In the centered uniform examples\nbelow, sum_s v_s=0 and the features span their stated ambient space.\nJensen's inequality gives Phi_1(x)>=0=Phi_1(0). Equality requires all\n<v_s,x> equal; their mean is zero, so they all vanish and span implies\nx=0. This proves the actual unique minimum, not an infimum at infinity.\nThe chain's attained minimum is computed explicitly in Section4.\n\nTemperature is not a free normalization improvement. Choose x_star to\nbe a closest attained minimizer to the chosen initial point, and let\npi_s=p_s(x_star), z=(x-x_star)/tau and\nLbase=Ccov/tau, for a fixed proved covariance bound Ccov. Then\n\n Phi_tau(x)-Phi_tau(x_star)=tau*log sum_s pi_s exp(<v_s,z>),\n z_(i+1)=z_i-(h_i/Ccov)sum_s p_i,s v_s,\n V=2 log(sum_s pi_s exp(<v_s,z_n>))/(Ccov*||z_0||^2). (10)\n\nHere sum_s pi_s v_s=0. The temperature cancels simultaneously from the\nphysical recurrence and the paid score at a fixed scaled initial state.\nChanging that state, the features, weights, or dimension can still matter;\n(10) does not exclude those choices or holding x_0 fixed while changing tau.\n\nAll original directed and optimum interpolation inequalities follow\nfrom the actual function and its stated smoothness, as follows. For any\nconvex differentiable Lbase-smooth Phi and any x, define\nk(u)=Phi(u)-<grad Phi(x),u>. It is minimized at x. Integrating its\nLipschitz gradient gives\nk(y-grad k(y)/Lbase)<=k(y)-||grad k(y)||^2/(2Lbase).\nComparison with k(x) proves\n\n Phi(y)>=Phi(x)+<grad Phi(x),y-x>\n                  +||grad Phi(y)-grad Phi(x)||^2/(2Lbase). (11)\n\nWith F_i=(Phi(x_i)-Phi_star)/Lbase and g_i=grad Phi(x_i)/Lbase,\n(11) is exactly\n\n Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0\n\nfor EVERY ordered pair of distinct physical queries, including BOTH\nterminal orientations and EVERY optimum pair. This agrees with accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290. No local-to-global inference about prescribed jets is\nbeing used. Zero steps simply repeat an actual query.\n\n## 3. Candidate one: a genuine simplex Gibbs plateau, but below Huber\n\nUse the two-dimensional Euclidean subspace E_A={x in R^3:sum_j x_j=0}.\nLet u=(1,1,1)/3, v_j=e_j-u, and\n\n Phi_A(x)=log[(exp(x_1)+exp(x_2)+exp(x_3))/3].\n\nThese are three explicit uniform affine features on E_A. The minimum is\nuniquely zero at0, and grad Phi_A=p-u. The feature diameter is sqrt(2),\nso L_A=1/2 is valid. It is sharp: logits can make p tend to(1/2,1/2,0),\nand the covariance in direction (1,-1,0)/sqrt(2) tends to1/2.\n\nFor ANY nonnegative word of total S>=4, specify BEFORE iteration\n\n x_0=4S*v_1+(0,1,-1),\n R_A^2=||x_0||^2=32S^2/3+2,\n x_(i+1)=x_i-2h_i*(p_i-u).                          (12)\n\nThe transverse vector is orthogonal to v_1, so the displayed radius is\nthe exact closest-minimizer distance. Initially x_1-x_2=4S-1 and\nx_1-x_3=4S+1. At a step either difference decreases by at most 2h_i.\nHence at EVERY physical query, including the terminal,\n\n x_i,1-x_i,j>=2S-1 for j=2,3,\n 1-p_i,1 <= eps_A:=2 exp(1-2S).                     (13)\n\nThe exact first-coordinate recurrence gives\n\n x_n,1=4S/3+2sum_i h_i(1-p_i,1).\n\nWriting Phi_A(x_n) as its largest logit plus a remainder, (13) yields\n\n 4S/3-log3 <= Phi_A(x_n)\n <=4S/3-log3+(2S+1)eps_A.                           (14)\n\nFor S>=4, (2S+1)eps_A is decreasing in S and is at most18 exp(-7).\nSince exp(1)>2, this is less than9/64<1/6. Since log3>1, (14) gives\n\n 0<Phi_A(x_n)<4S/3-5/6,\n V_A=4Phi_A(x_n)/(32S^2/3+2)<1/(2S+1).             (15)\n\nFor the final strict comparison, multiply the upper bound's numerator\n16S/3-10/3 by 2S+1. The result is\n32S^2/3-4S/3-10/3, strictly below32S^2/3+2.\nThe elementary inequality exp(1)<3, used for log3>1, follows by bounding\nthe exponential series tail: k!>=2^(k-1) for k>=2, strictly for k>=3.\nThe lower bound in (14), with log3<2, proves positivity for S>=4.\n\nThis actual trajectory is not an exactly constant-gradient ray. On the\noriginal H_m, put t=2h_0 in(2,3), or use the weaker t<4. Initially\np_1>p_2>p_3, eps_A<1/64, and every later p_1>1-eps_A. Under (9), p_1\nstrictly decreases at this first step because exp(-t p_1) is strictly\nsmaller than the other two update factors. Meanwhile p_2-p_3 strictly\nincreases. To check the latter, the derivative of q(z)=z exp(-t z) on\n[p_3,p_2] is at least exp(-t eps_A)(1-t eps_A)>7/8, using\nexp(-v)>=1-v. The probability normalizer is at most\nexp(-2(1-eps_A))+eps_A<1/4+1/64<7/8;\nexp(3/2)>4 follows from its first four series terms. The mean value\ntheorem now proves (p'_2-p'_3)>(p_2-p_3). Thus the positive first\ncoordinate of p-u decreases while its positive transverse difference\nincreases. The first two gradients cannot be collinear.\n\nEquations (14) also give 2S*V_A ->1 as S tends to infinity, uniformly\nover the chronological word at a fixed total S. The family is a real\nGibbs realization of Huber-order performance, but (15) is a decisive\nearly screen. Do not polish it into a claimed improvement.\n\nThe COMPLETE same-word comparisons are\n\n 0<V_A<Huber(h)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h),\n V_A/Cfull(h)<8M/(2M-1)<5  for S>=4.                (16)\n\nThe second inequality compares with the full domain (5), using (6).\nIt is only a factor bound; it asserts NEITHER domination by nor strict\nimprovement over the full complex channel. No equality K=Huber or\nF_ray=Huber from an unverified historical draft is imported.\n\nFor the original H_m, (1), (7), and (14) prove\n\n B_n V_A -> Z_1/(2 exp(1))\n <=41209/[20000 exp(1)]<41209/50000<1.              (17)\n\nHere exp(1)>5/2 follows from its first four series terms.
