(6)\n\nIndeed each theta*h_i is in [0,1/2), and multiplying the inequalities\n(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v gives Poly_n(theta)>=1-theta*S>1/2.\nThis uses the maximality of Gap_h, not a false identification of Gap_h\nwith a quadratic value.\n\nAccepted 04219554e41d9750 supplies the ORIGINAL ray limit and enclosure\n\n B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1,\n 0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000,             (7)\n\nwhere B_1=4 follows directly from (3). The proof's Section7 and the\nfull stated enclosure were read. No seed existence or seed consequence\nfrom that fact is used. No new external theorem is imported.\n\nThe original problem asks whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where\nW_n^*=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h). Its affirmative lower bound\nrequires one N_epsilon working for EVERY word at EVERY n>=N_epsilon.\nNeither the diagnostic family (1) nor any result here provides that.\n\n## 2. Direct finite-Gibbs calculations and all original rows\n\nFor an explicitly specified finite list of vectors v_s, positive weights\nb_s, and temperature tau>0, put\n\n Phi_tau(x)=tau*log sum_s b_s*exp(<v_s,x>/tau),\n p_s(x)=b_s*exp(<v_s,x>/tau)/sum_t b_t*exp(<v_t,x>/tau),\n mu(x)=sum_s p_s(x)*v_s.\n\nDifferentiation of a finite sum gives, on the whole Euclidean space,\n\n grad Phi_tau=mu,\n Hess Phi_tau=(1/tau)[sum_s p_s v_s v_s^T-mu mu^T].  (8)\n\nThe quadratic form is a variance, so Phi_tau is convex. If the feature\ndiameter is Dfeat, its variance in any unit direction is at most\nDfeat^2/4. To prove this, for a random scalar T in [b,d], the nonnegative\nquantity (T-b)(d-T) gives\nVar(T)<=(d-E T)(E T-b)<=(d-b)^2/4.\nConsequently Lbase=Dfeat^2/(4tau) is valid globally. One may also use\nan explicitly proved sharper covariance bound in a particular geometry.\n\nThe EXACT probability update at the physical step is\n\n p_(i+1),s = p_i,s * exp[-h_i <v_s,mu_i>/(Lbase*tau)]\n             / sum_t p_i,t*exp[-h_i <v_t,mu_i>/(Lbase*tau)]. (9)\n\nThis equation follows from the actual vector update; no label is chosen\nafter a step. Every weight remains strictly positive at every finite\nquery. All functions and recurrences in this manuscript are finite even\nwhen their feature counts are large; none is evaluated by a host job.\n\nAttainment must be checked separately. In the centered uniform examples\nbelow, sum_s v_s=0 and the features span their stated ambient space.\nJensen's inequality gives Phi_1(x)>=0=Phi_1(0). Equality requires all\n<v_s,x> equal; their mean is zero, so they all vanish and span implies\nx=0. This proves the actual unique minimum, not an infimum at infinity.\nThe chain's attained minimum is computed explicitly in Section4.\n\nTemperature is not a free normalization improvement. Choose x_star to\nbe a closest attained minimizer to the chosen initial point, and let\npi_s=p_s(x_star), z=(x-x_star)/tau and\nLbase=Ccov/tau, for a fixed proved covariance bound Ccov. Then\n\n Phi_tau(x)-Phi_tau(x_star)=tau*log sum_s pi_s exp(<v_s,z>),\n z_(i+1)=z_i-(h_i/Ccov)sum_s p_i,s v_s,\n V=2 log(sum_s pi_s exp(<v_s,z_n>))/(Ccov*||z_0||^2). (10)\n\nHere sum_s pi_s v_s=0. The temperature cancels simultaneously from the\nphysical recurrence and the paid score at a fixed scaled initial state.\nChanging that state, the features, weights, or dimension can still matter;\n(10) does not exclude those choices or holding x_0 fixed while changing tau.\n\nAll original directed and optimum interpolation inequalities follow\nfrom the actual function and its stated smoothness, as follows. For any\nconvex differentiable Lbase-smooth Phi and any x, define\nk(u)=Phi(u)-<grad Phi(x),u>. It is minimized at x. Integrating its\nLipschitz gradient gives\nk(y-grad k(y)/Lbase)<=k(y)-||grad k(y)||^2/(2Lbase).\nComparison with k(x) proves\n\n Phi(y)>=Phi(x)+<grad Phi(x),y-x>\n                  +||grad Phi(y)-grad Phi(x)||^2/(2Lbase). (11)\n\nWith F_i=(Phi(x_i)-Phi_star)/Lbase and g_i=grad Phi(x_i)/Lbase,\n(11) is exactly\n\n Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0\n\nfor EVERY ordered pair of distinct physical queries, including BOTH\nterminal orientations and EVERY optimum pair. This agrees with accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290. No local-to-global inference about prescribed jets is\nbeing used. Zero steps simply repeat an actual query.\n\n## 4. Candidate two: coupled finite sign features and a weak support bound\n\nLet a_chain>0 and let D=2(n+1). There are D-1 variable coordinates.\nFix the boundary values x_0=a_chain and x_D=0 only in this section, and\ndefine on the variables x_1,...,x_(D-1)\n\n Phi_B(x)=sum_(j=0)^(D-1) log cosh(x_j-x_(j+1)).     (18)\n\nThe boundary indices are spatial, not time indices. This is EXACTLY a\nfinite Gibbs function, not a sum of quadratic surrogates:\n\n Phi_B(x)=log sum_(sigma in {-1,1}^D) 2^(-D)\n       exp[a_chain*sigma_0\n               +sum_(j=1)^(D-1)(sigma_j-sigma_(j-1))*x_j]. (19)\n\nEquation (19) explicitly specifies every feature and positive weight.\nNo enumeration is needed to define this finite sum. Its Hessian is\nthe path difference matrix transposed, times a diagonal matrix of\nsech^2 edge differences, times the difference matrix. For any variation\nz with z_0=z_D=0,\n\n sum_(j=0)^(D-1)(z_j-z_(j+1))^2<=4sum_(j=1)^(D-1)z_j^2.\n\nThus the actual function is globally convex and L_B=4 is a valid\nsmoothness constant. We do not claim this bound is sharp. Since the\nedge differences sum to a_chain, strict convexity of log cosh and\nJensen give the UNIQUE attained minimum\n\n x_star,j=a_chain*(1-j/D),\n Phi_B,star=D log cosh(a_chain/D).                   (20)\n\nStart the variable vector at zero. Its true squared distance is\n\n R_B^2=a_chain^2*(D-1)(2D-1)/(6D).                  (21)\n\nThe finite sum of squares in (21) follows from\nsum_(j=1)^(D-1)(D-j)^2=(D-1)D(2D-1)/6; the last identity is proved\nby induction using the difference of the two cubic expressions.\n\nAt each physical time i the update is exactly\n\n x_(i+1),j=x_i,j+(h_i/4)\n     [tanh(x_i,j-1-x_i,j)-tanh(x_i,j-x_i,j+1)].      (22)\n\nThe fixed boundary values are retained at every time. Induction on i\nshows x_i,j=0 for all j>i: if j>i+1 both adjacent differences in (22)\nare zero, including the right boundary. This holds for all finite\nnonnegative steps, however large, and keeps all queries.\n\nPut r_hor=n+1. At the terminal, x_n,j=0 for j>=r_hor. The first\nr_hor edge differences sum to a_chain; all subsequent differences\nvanish. Jensen gives the actual LOWER bound\n\n V_B >= Aprop := [r_hor log cosh(a_chain/r_hor)\n                   -D log cosh(a_chain/D)]/(2 R_B^2)>0.   (23)\n\nThe numerator is positive because k log cosh(a_chain/k) is strictly\ndecreasing in positive k: with u=a_chain/k, its derivative is\nlog cosh(u)-u tanh(u)<0, as the latter expression has derivative\n-u sech^2(u)<0 and is zero at u=0.\n\nBut the CERTIFICATE Aprop itself is too small. The inequality\nlog cosh(u)<=u^2/2 follows by integrating tanh(u)<=u for u>=0 and\nusing evenness. Dropping the positive subtracted minimum in (23),\n\n 0<Aprop<=3D/[2r_hor(D-1)(2D-1)]<=3/(2r_hor^2).    (24)\n\nThe final bound uses D-1>=r_hor and D/(2D-1)<=1. This is an upper\nbound on a proved lower guarantee; it is NOT an upper bound on V_B.\nNo assertion that (23) is attained by the physical GD trajectory is made.\n\nThis certificate failure is not repaired by making the chain's valid\nsmoothness constant sharp. When D>=3, at the zero variable vector an\ninternal edge has zero difference and Hessian contribution\n(e_1-e_2)(e_1-e_2)^T. All other edge contributions are positive\nsemidefinite, so every valid global smoothness constant is at least2.\nUsing ANY such constant instead of4 preserves finite propagation and\nthe same constrained value in (23), and its normalized support guarantee\nis at most2*Aprop. Thus all the vanishing comparisons below persist,\nwith at most a factor2. The original H_m domain has D>=3.\n\nOn original H_m, r_hor=N, so the COMPLETE references obey\n\n Aprop/Huber <=3(c/4)^m,\n Aprop/K_n <=3(c/4)^m,\n Aprop/F_ray_n <=3(c/4)^m,\n Aprop/Cfull <12(c/4)^m,\n B_n Aprop <(15/2)(3/4)^m ->0.                      (25)\n\nThe first and fourth estimates use M<c^m and (6); the last uses (3).\nFor m>=8, c< (9/8)(5/2)=45/16<4, and c tends to lambda<4, so\nall four reference ratios vanish. The scope is the untouched-coordinate\ncertificate only. The coupled chain itself is not excluded: a large\nphysical step could create an actual error much greater than (23).\nNo computational or qualitative claim about that actual error is used\nto discard the weak certificate route and move to the third geometry.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["38da1067bcd1a0c8"], "status": "partial: function/dynamics complete, support certificate weak, actual payoff unresolved"}, "glossary": {"H_m": "Original enclosing-m diagnostic word defined in Section1; distinct from conjugate jet quadratics H_j.", "Phi_A": "Centered three-feature simplex log-partition function on E_A.", "Phi_B": "Finite-sign Gibbs function equal to the coupled log-cosh Dirichlet chain.", "Phi_T": "Uniform log-partition function of the explicitly weighted binary-tree simplex features.", "E_A": "The Euclidean zero-sum subspace of R^3 used by candidate one.", "Lbase": "Proved positive global gradient Lipschitz constant of a base function Phi.", "L_A": "Sharp base smoothness1/2 for Phi_A.", "L_B": "Valid base smoothness4 for Phi_B.", "Ltree": "Sharp global smoothness(1+w_0)/2 of Phi_T.", "R_init": "Distance from the actual initial point to the whole minimizer set.", "R_A": "True initial distance for the simplex candidate.", "R_B": "True initial distance to the chain's unique linear-ramp minimizer.", "R_T": "True initial distance to the tree candidate's unique minimizer0.", "V_A": "Actual normalized terminal score of the specified simplex family.", "V_B": "Actual normalized terminal score of the chain, distinguished from its lower guarantee Aprop.", "V_T": "Actual normalized terminal score2D_n/(Ltree*R_T^2) of the tree candidate.", "r_tree": "The geometric weight ratio2/c for the original enclosing m.", "Zgeom": "Finite geometric sum of r_tree^j for0<=j<m; not an accepted ray constant Z_b.", "w_j": "Tree feature energy r_tree^j/Zgeom at root-oriented depth j.", "a_s": "Explicit signed root-to-leaf feature vector of leaf s in the tree candidate.", "K_st": "The explicitly determined feature inner product <a_s,a_t>, not the benchmark functional K_n.", "y_i,j": "Actual scaled left-spine coordinate sqrt(w_j)*x_i,(0^j).", "P_i,k": "Actual Gibbs mass of first-right-turn group k at time i, with k=m the all-left leaf.", "q_k": "Uniform leaf reference mass of exit group k:2^(-k-1) for k<m, and2^(-m) for k=m.", "ell_i,k": "Actual common logit of exit group k at physical time i.", "D_i": "Actual tree objective Phi_T(x_i), equal to relative entropy sum_k q_k log(q_k/P_i,k).", "t0": "The specified initial spine field log2.", "eta_m": "Exact entropy threshold Ltree*R_T^2/(2B_n) for diagnostic benchmark matching.", "eta_inf": "Positive limit of2^m*eta_m explicitly displayed in equation39.", "Aprop": "The chain's positive untouched-coordinate lower guarantee, not its actual error.", "r_hor": "The chain cutoff index n+1.", "a_chain": "Positive fixed left boundary value in the Dirichlet chain.", "Huber": "Complete-word zero-cut reference1/(1+2S).", "Cfull": "Supremum of accepted Gap_h(z) over the FULL schedule-dependent domain D_h.", "Dfeat": "Diameter of the explicit affine feature list in the general Gibbs calculation.", "Ccov": "A fixed proved covariance bound, with smoothness Ccov/tau."}}
{"id": "1ad14d90628c1312", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T00:33:06.199423+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round49 COMPLETE joint UNVERIFIED tree-Gibbs analysis: exact conditional entropy sum, cumulative subtree clocks, conditional KL/radius payments and entropy-to-mass bounds. Original-H_m integrated occupation relaxation admits terminal zero for m>=8; deepest-pair persistence cannot certify the exact benchmark for m>=32 and that certificate needs actual conditional overstepping. The SPECIFIED actual terminal-entropy inequality remains UNRESOLVED.", "evidence": "# The assigned tree-Gibbs trajectory: conditional clocks and entropy payments\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, one\nallocated round after completed48/attempted49, with completed ceiling49\nand attempted ceiling50. TASK.md and master_guidance d70a595e12f4b50f\nauthorize this analytical investigation and explicitly forbid computation\nand fact_submit. No new fact is claimed or submitted. All new arguments\nare reproduced together here; no unverified finding is a premise.\n\n## 1. Verdict and the precise remaining question\n\nThe specified terminal inequality is still UNRESOLVED. This draft proves\nneither that the specified trajectory reaches the benchmark nor an\nasymptotic upper obstruction for that trajectory. Its new interfaces are:\n\n* an exact decomposition of the objective over conditional tree fields;\n* an exact formula for every cumulative subtree clock, including its\n  dependence on the actual endpoint ancestral coordinates;\n* exact conditional relative-entropy and Euclidean-radius payment\n  identities, with the squared-step and inter-iterate entropy terms;\n* quantitative control of every instantaneous subtree mass by the actual\n  entropy, with no assumed concentration;\n* an explicit demonstration that the integrated linear occupation\n  constraints alone admit endpoint zero on the ORIGINAL word;\n* a finite original-benchmark comparison showing that preservation of\n  the initial deepest field cannot by itself certify the target. That\n  particular certificate needs amplification and an actual overstep in\n  its changing conditional clock.\n\nThe last two items exclude only stated proof shortcuts. They do not\nexclude this tree-Gibbs candidate. Even a positive answer for this\ncandidate would leave the original all-word/all-horizon bridge open.\n\n## 2. Definitions, legality, and normalization reproduced from scratch\n\nFix an integer m>=8. Write\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n n=2^m-1, N=2^m, h=H_m,\n r=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) r^j, w_j=r^j/Zgeom.\n\nEvery occurrence of a child word retains these SAME enclosing-m\nparameters. Let S_t=sum_(i<t)h_i and S=S_n. Counting the two copies\nat each join, or solving S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a*c^j, gives\n\n 1+S=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)\n     =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta*c^m,  zeta=beta/[c(c-2)].       (2.1)\n\nHere 0<zeta<1: lambda(lambda-2)=1, c>lambda, and beta<1.\n\nUse one orthonormal coordinate e_v for each binary word v of length\nless than m. For a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m), define\n\n a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))e_(s_1...s_j),\n F(x)=log[N^(-1)sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)].                 (2.2)\n\nThe feature mean is zero, and every feature has squared norm one.\nDifferent coordinate rows of the feature matrix are orthogonal. Rows\nwith disjoint supports have zero product. If one node is below the\nother, the descendant row has equal positive and negative entries on\nits support, on which the ancestor row is constant. Each row has\npositive squared norm. Thus the matrix has row rank N-1.\n\nJensen's inequality applied to the exponential gives F>=0. Equality\nrequires all leaf logits to be equal; their sum is zero, so they must\nall vanish. Full row rank then gives x=0. Consequently F has the UNIQUE\nattained minimum zero at zero. The same rank argument shows that the\nmap from x to the N logits is onto their zero-sum subspace.\n\nDifferentiating this finite sum gives grad F=E_p a and Hess F=Cov_p(a),\nwhere p_s=exp(<a_s,x>)/sum_t exp(<a_t,x>). To bound the Hessian, fix a\nunit vector u and let A and B be the smallest and largest values of\n<a_s,u>. For any random variable z in [A,B],\n\n E z^2 <=(A+B)E z-AB,\n Var(z)<=(B-A)^2/4-(E z-(A+B)/2)^2<=(B-A)^2/4.\n\nThe first inequality follows by averaging (z-A)(B-z)>=0.\nIf leaves first separate at level j, their squared distance is\n\n             4w_j+2sum_(l>j)w_l.                   (2.3)\n\nThe quarter-distances strictly decrease with j, since the difference\nbetween consecutive ones is w_j-w_(j+1)/2>0. The diameter bound above\ntherefore gives the globally valid constant\n\n                  Ltree=(1+w_0)/2.                (2.4)\n\nIt is sharp. Choose a pair separating at the root. Prescribe equal\nlogits for that pair and logits smaller by T for every other leaf,\nthen subtract the common mean. Surjectivity of the logit map realizes\nthese logits at an actual finite x. As T tends to infinity the Gibbs\nlaw tends to the equal law on that pair. Its covariance in the unit\nfeature-difference direction tends to the squared diameter divided by\nfour, namely Ltree. This establishes the global supremum.\n\nTake t0=log(2) and the exact initial point\n\n x_0,(0^j)=t0/sqrt(w_j), 0<=j<m,\n x_0,v=0 on every other node.\n\nThe squared distance to the whole minimizer set is exactly\n\n R^2=t0^2 sum_(j=0)^(m-1)1/w_j\n    =t0^2 r^(1-m) Zgeom^2.                          (2.5)\n\nThe last equality reverses the finite geometric sum. Run the actual\niteration x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Ltree)grad F(x_i). For an arbitrary desired\nsmoothness L, the function (L/Ltree)F uses exactly steps h_i/L and has\nthe legal instance score 2F(x_n)/(Ltree R^2). All positions, values,\ngradients, and the minimum are from this one finite static function.\n\n## 3. Actual group probabilities and conditional fields\n\nAll coordinates off the all-left spine remain zero. At a point on\nthat spine, leaves with the same first right turn have equal logits.\nA non-spine coordinate splits one such group into equal halves, so\nits mean feature coordinate vanishes. Induction through every physical\nstep proves invariance.\n\nDefine y_(i,j)=sqrt(w_j)x_(i,0^j). For first exit k<m and the final\nall-left leaf k=m, respectively, set\n\n q_k=2^(-k-1) (k<m), q_m=2^(-m),\n ell_(i,k)=sum_(j<k)y_(i,j)-y_(i,k) (k<m),\n ell_(i,m)=sum_(j<m)y_(i,j),\n P_(i,k)=q_k exp(ell_(i,k))/sum_(u=0)^m q_u exp(ell_(i,u)).  (3.1)\n\nThese are probabilities of the original leaves, aggregated only over\nidentical logits. In particular they are not free control variables.\nLet\n\n Q_(i,j)=sum_(k=j)^m P_(i,k), 0<=j<=m,\n d_(i,j)=Q_(i,j+1)-P_(i,j)=2Q_(i,j+1)-Q_(i,j).\n\nThen Q_(i,0)=1 and the exact coordinate update is\n\n y_(i+1,j)=y_(i,j)-(h_i w_j/Ltree)d_(i,j).           (3.2)\n\nInitially the denominator in (3.1) is (m+4)/4, each side-group\nunnormalized mass is 1/4, and the final unnormalized mass is 1. Thus\n\n P_(0,k)=1/(m+4) (k<m), P_(0,m)=4/(m+4),\n Q_(0,j)=(m-j+4)/(m+4),\n F(x_0)=log[(m+4)/4].                               (3.3)\n\nFor each time define conditional log-partitions recursively by\n\n A_(i,m)=0,\n A_(i,j)=log[(exp(-y_(i,j))+exp(y_(i,j)+A_(i,j+1)))/2],\n T_(i,j)=y_(i,j)+A_(i,j+1)/2.                       (3.4)\n\nThis is the conditional partition on the actual all-left depth-j\nsubtree with its original uniform conditional reference. The side\nsubtree contributes one because all its coordinates are zero. Direct\nfactorization gives\n\n A_(i,j)=A_(i,j+1)/2+log cosh T_(i,j),\n Q_(i,j+1)/Q_(i,j)=(1+tanh T_(i,j))/2,\n d_(i,j)=Q_(i,j)tanh T_(i,j),\n D_i:=F(x_i)=A_(i,0)=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^(-j)log cosh T_(i,j). (3.5)\n\nEvery A_(i,j) is nonnegative. In particular the objective is the FULL\nsum in (3.5), rather than just its final two-leaf contribution.\nThe exact conditional-field update is\n\n T_(i+1,j)=T_(i,j)-alpha_(i,j)tanh T_(i,j)\n                   +(A_(i+1,j+1)-A_(i,j+1))/2,\n alpha_(i,j)=h_i w_j Q_(i,j)/Ltree.                 (3.6)\n\nEquation (3.6) retains the changing child partition. Dropping its last\nterm, or replacing alpha by a fresh copy of the original child word,\nchanges the actual process.\n\nSince sum_k q_k ell_(i,k)=0, substitution in (3.1) also proves\n\n D_i=sum_k q_k log(q_k/P_(i,k)).                    (3.7)\n\nThe reference law is the FIRST argument of this relative entropy.\n\n## 4. Exact cumulative subtree clocks\n\nFor any physical prefix length t and depth 0<=j<m, put\n\n Cclock_(t,j)=sum_(i<t)alpha_(i,j)\n            =(w_j/Ltree)sum_(i<t)h_i Q_(i,j).\n\nThe complete history sum admits the exact identity\n\n Cclock_(t,j)=S_t/(Ltree Zgeom c^j)\n               +sum_(l<j)c^(l-j)(t0-y_(t,l)).       (4.1)\n\nTo prove it, the relation d_(i,j)=2Q_(i,j+1)-Q_(i,j) and Q_(i,0)=1\ngive by finite induction\n\n Q_(i,j)=2^(-j)[1+sum_(l<j)2^l d_(i,l)].\n\nMultiply by h_i and sum. Equation (3.2) gives\n\n sum_(i<t)h_i d_(i,l)=Ltree(t0-y_(t,l))/w_l.\n\nFinally 2^l/w_l=Zgeom c^l and w_j 2^(-j)=1/(Zgeom c^j), proving (4.1).\nThere is also the mass version, valid through j=m:\n\n sum_(i<t)h_i Q_(i,j)\n  =2^(-j)[S_t+Ltree Zgeom sum_(l<j)c^l(t0-y_(t,l))]. (4.2)\n\nThese formulas hold for every prefix, not just recursive block ends.\nThey do not assert that a nonlinear trajectory depends only on the\nsum of its effective steps.\n\n## 5. Actual entropy and radius payment identities\n\nLet p_i^[j] be the conditional Gibbs law on the leaves of the depth-j\nall-left subtree, and let u^[j] be the uniform conditional law there.\nFor probability vectors p and v with positive entries, define\nKL(p||v)=sum_s p_s log(p_s/v_s). Set\n\n I_(i,j)=KL(p_i^[j]||u^[j]),\n Kstep_(i,j)=KL(p_i^[j]||p_(i+1)^[j]),\n E_(i,j)=sum_(l=j)^(m-1) w_l Q_(i,l)^2 tanh^2 T_(i,l),\n Rsub_(i,j)^2=sum_(l=j)^(m-1)y_(i,l)^2/w_l.\n\nEvery symbol uses the SAME actual iterate. Conditioned at depth j,\nthe subtree gradient has squared norm E_(i,j)/Q_(i,j)^2. Its actual\ncoordinate update has multiplier h_i Q_(i,j)/Ltree, because global\ngradients in that subtree equal Q_(i,j) times conditional gradients.\nIt follows exactly that\n\n A_(i+1,j)-A_(i,j)\n      =-h_i E_(i,j)/(Ltree Q_(i,j))+Kstep_(i,j),     (5.1)\n\n Rsub_(i+1,j)^2-Rsub_(i,j)^2\n      =-2h_i Q_(i,j)(A_(i,j)+I_(i,j))/Ltree\n                         +h_i^2 E_(i,j)/Ltree^2.   (5.2)\n\nFor completeness, the finite exponential-family calculation behind\n(5.1) is as follows. If A is a conditional log-partition, g=grad A,\nand z'=z-vg, then\n\n KL(p_z||p_z')=<g,z-z'>+A(z')-A(z)\n              =v||g||^2+A(z')-A(z).\n\nHere v=h_i Q_(i,j)/Ltree. Also the centered conditional feature mean\nat the origin is zero, and\n\n <z,grad A(z)>=A(z)+KL(p_z||u^[j]).\n\nExpanding ||z-vg||^2 proves (5.2). No limiting, continuous-time, or\nindependent-child approximation was taken. All probabilities are\nstrictly positive, so these expressions are finite. Nonnegativity of\neach KL follows from -sum_s p_s log(v_s/p_s)>=sum_s(p_s-v_s)=0,\nusing log z<=z-1.\n\nAt the root, these give the exact telescopes\n\n D_n=D_0-(1/Ltree)sum_(i<n)h_i E_(i,0)\n                                  +sum_(i<n)Kstep_(i,0),           (5.3)\n Rsub_(n,0)^2=R^2-(2/Ltree)sum_(i<n)h_i(D_i+I_(i,0))\n                                  +(1/Ltree^2)sum_(i<n)h_i^2 E_(i,0). (5.4)\n\nThe positive Kstep terms in (5.1) and the squared-step terms in (5.2)\ncannot be discarded to claim monotonicity or a terminal upper bound.\nThe forward conditional entropy has the additional exact decomposition\n\n I_(i,j)=sum_(l=j)^(m-1)[Q_(i,l)/Q_(i,j)]\n          [T_(i,l)tanh T_(i,l)-log cosh T_(i,l)].    (5.5)\n\nIndeed the conditional probability of reaching each spine node is\nQ_(i,l)/Q_(i,j), and the divergence of its Bernoulli split from the\nequal split is T tanh T-log cosh T. Side subtrees have zero conditional\ndivergence. This also records explicitly the different weights in\nforward entropy (5.5) and reverse entropy (3.5). The same expansion\nbetween consecutive conditional laws gives the further exact formula\n\n Kstep_(i,j)=sum_(l=j)^(m-1)[Q_(i,l)/Q_(i,j)]\n   [log cosh T_(i+1,l)-log cosh T_(i,l)\n      -(T_(i+1,l)-T_(i,l))tanh T_(i,l)].             (5.6)\n\nFor a single split its log probability is sigma*T-log(2 cosh T),\nwhere sigma=+1 or -1. Taking the old-law expectation of the difference\nbetween the old and new log probabilities gives the bracket in (5.6).\nMultiplying by each node's old conditional reach probability and\nsumming proves the formula. Thus the payments in (5.1) have explicit\nvalues in the same consecutive conditional fields; they are not free\nnonnegative slack variables.\n\n## 6. A quantitative mass bound without a concentration assumption\n\nFor every time i and depth 1<=j<=m, put u=2^j Q_(i,j) and\nPhi(u)=u-1-log u. Then\n\n                  Phi(2^j Q_(i,j))<=2^j D_i.        (6.1)\n\nSplit the sum in (3.7) between the subtree and its complement. The\nconditional divergences are nonnegative, so D_i is at least the\nbinary divergence with reference mass p=2^(-j) and current mass Q:\n\n -p log(Q/p)-(1-p)log[(1-Q)/(1-p)].\n\nSet u=Q/p. The inequality -log(1-z)>=z for z<1, applied to\nz=p(u-1)/(1-p), bounds the second term below by p(u-1).\nThis proves (6.1), including Q<p. Further,\n\n Phi(u)-(sqrt(u)-1)^2=2[sqrt(u)-1-log sqrt(u)]>=0.\n\nConsequently\n\n (1-sqrt(2^j D_i))_+^2 <=2^j Q_(i,j)\n                         <=(1+sqrt(2^j D_i))^2,     (6.2)\n alpha_(i,j)<=h_i/(Ltree Zgeom c^j)\n                         *(1+sqrt(2^j D_i))^2.     (6.3)\n\nThese are bounds from the actual entropy at the SAME time; terminal\nsmall entropy alone supplies no bound for earlier clocks. The initial\nstate (3.3), for example, is very far from uniform at deep levels.\n\nThere is also a quantitative endpoint enclosure for (4.1). Define\nU_l(D)=arcosh(exp(2^l D)), with arcosh z=log(z+sqrt(z^2-1)) for z>=1.\nThe nonnegative summands in (3.5) give\n\n |T_(t,l)|<=U_l(D_t),  0<=A_(t,l+1)/2<=2^l D_t,\n -U_l(D_t)-2^l D_t<=y_(t,l)<=U_l(D_t).\n\nInserting these into the exact clock formula yields\n\n Cbase_(t,j)-sum_(l<j)c^(l-j)U_l(D_t)\n <= Cclock_(t,j)\n <= Cbase_(t,j)+sum_(l<j)c^(l-j)[U_l(D_t)+2^l D_t], (6.4)\n\nwhere Cbase_(t,j)=S_t/(Ltree Zgeom c^j)+t0 sum_(l<j)c^(l-j).\nUnlike (6.3), this bounds an entire accumulated clock using the\nCURRENT entropy and the elapsed physical step sum. It still does not\ncontrol the individual factors in the nonlinear update (3.6).\n\n## 7. Why cumulative occupation constraints do not settle the target\n\nFor the ORIGINAL S in (2.1), and every m>=8, terminal y_(n,j)=0 is\ncompatible with ALL linear integrated gradient equations and positive\ntime-averaged group occupations. This is an endpoint relaxation only:\nthe following probability vector is not claimed to be an actual GD\nhistory.\n\nSet K0=Ltree Zgeom t0 and define\n\n Tbar_j=2^(-j)[S+K0 sum_(l<j)c^l], 0<=j<=m,\n Vbar_j=Tbar_j-Tbar_(j+1)\n       =2^(-j-1)[S-K0(c^j-sum_(l<j)c^l)], 0<=j<m,\n Vbar_m=Tbar_m.                                    (7.1)\n\nThen sum_j Vbar_j=S and 2Tbar_(j+1)-Tbar_j=K0(c/2)^j\n=Ltree t0/w_j. Thus Pbar_j=Vbar_j/S, if positive, satisfies exactly\nthe integrated version of (3.2) with terminal zero. We now prove\npositivity using the original word rather than an asymptotic schedule.\n\nFor m>=8, beta>=7/8 and c<11/4. The latter follows from\nsqrt(2)<13/9 and c<=(9/8)lambda. Equation (2.1) gives\n\n S>=beta*c^(m-1)/(c-2)-1\n   >(7/6)c^(m-1)-1>c^(m-1).                        (7.2)\n\nFor the final inequality, c^(m-1)>2^(m-1)>=128. Since\nZgeom<c/(c-2), Ltree Zgeom=(Zgeom+1)/2<(c-1)/(c-2).\nAlso c>2 makes c^j-sum_(l<j)c^l increase with j. Hence its largest\nprice in (7.1) satisfies\n\n K0[c^(m-1)-sum_(l<m-1)c^l]\n <t0[c^(m-1)+1/(c-2)]\n <(3/4)[c^(m-1)+5/2]<c^(m-1)<S.                  (7.3)\n\nHere log2<3/4 follows by the strict trapezoid bound for the integral\nof 1/t on [1,2], and 1/(c-2)<lambda<5/2. Thus all Vbar_j are\nstrictly positive, including Vbar_m. This proves the stated relaxation\nclaim. It does NOT construct an actual trajectory ending at the\nminimum, and it does not satisfy or replace the nonlinear identities\n(3.1), (3.6), or (5.1). It precisely shows what extra information a\nproof using (4.1) must retain: chronological conditional response.\n\nA separate exact counterexample rules out replacing that response by\nthe sum of clocks. On the root-only slice of the SAME F, all descendants\nare zero and F=log cosh y. Its root update is y'=y-k tanh y, where\nk=h w_0/Ltree. Start this test state at y=t0, set A=5t0/3, B=A/2,\nand use physical steps A Ltree/w_0 and B Ltree/w_0. For order (A,B),\nthe first step reaches zero because tanh t0=3/5, and the terminal is\nzero. For order (B,A), the first state is t0/2 and tanh(t0/2)=1/3,\nso the terminal is t0/2-5t0/9=-t0/18. Both orders have the same total\nclock A+B. These are actual states and gradients of F, but their test\nstart and test word differ from the assignment. This refutes the\nclock-sum substitution, not the assigned terminal inequality.\n\n## 8. The exact original threshold and a finite deepest-pair screen\n\nRetain the original recurrences\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)(C_k+C_l+sqrt(C_k^2+6C_k C_l+C_l^2))/2,\n B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)[2C_k+B_l/2+sqrt(B_l^2+8C_k B_l)/2].\n\nThe exact requested bound is D_n>=eta_m, where\n\n eta_m=Ltree t0^2 r^(1-m)Zgeom^2/(2B_n),\n Theta_m=N eta_m=Ltree t0^2 r Zgeom^2 c^m/(2B_n).    (8.1)\n\nThe accepted fact04219554e41d9750 supplies, with Z_1 its denominator\nray constant rather than our geometric sum,\n\n B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^m Z_1,\n Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000.              (8.2)\n\nThe identity B_1=4 follows immediately from its single defining split.\nOnly this existing exact enclosure is used; no benchmark evaluation,\nDP, replay, or unproved numerical constant occurs here.\n\nFor every m>=32, there is the explicit finite bound\n\n                    Theta_m>51200/41209>1.         (8.3)\n\nIndeed sqrt(2)<47/33 implies c<=(33/32)lambda<5/2, so r>4/5.\nThe first sixteen terms of Zgeom give\n\n Zgeom>5[1-(4/5)^16]>24/5,\n\nsince 4^8=65536<78125=5^7. The integral of 1/t on [1,2] is strictly\nlarger than its midpoint lower bound 2/3, giving t0^2>4/9. Also\nLtree>1/2. The first four terms of the binomial expansion give, for\nm>=8,\n\n (1+1/m)^m >=2+(m-1)/(2m)+(m-1)(m-2)/(6m^2)\n            >=2+7/16+7/64=163/64>5/2.\n\nSubstitute these bounds and (8.2) into (8.1):\n\n Theta_m > [(1/2)(4/9)(4/5)(24/5)^2(5/2)/2]\n                    *(10000/41209)=51200/41209.\n\nThe exact entropy decomposition gives the familiar deepest-pair lower\ncertificate D_n>=(2/N)log cosh y_(n,m-1). Its being strong enough for\nthe target would require\n\n             2 log cosh y_(n,m-1)>=Theta_m.         (8.4)\n\nBut preservation of the initial amplitude would give only\n\n 2 log cosh y_(n,m-1)<=2 log cosh(log2)=2 log(5/4)<1/2.\n\nThis is below (8.3). More strongly, (8.4) requires\n|y_(n,m-1)|>1, because log cosh z<=z^2/2, proved by integrating\ntanh z<=z for z>=0 and using evenness. Thus a deepest-field-only\ncertificate needs amplification beyond the initial log2, not merely\npersistence of a nonzero initial tail coordinate. The full entropy\ncould still reach the target through other levels.\n\nAt the last level (3.6) has no forcing term:\n\n y_(i+1,m-1)=y_(i,m-1)-alpha_(i,m-1)tanh y_(i,m-1). (8.5)\n\nIf 0<=alpha<=2, then |y-alpha tanh y|<=|y|: for y>=0 use\n0<=alpha tanh y<=2y, and then use oddness. Consequently success of\nthe particular certificate (8.4), for m>=32, requires at least one\nactual alpha_(i,m-1)>2. Here the largest original step is\nh_max=1+beta*c^(m-2), and h_i/(Ltree Zgeom c^(m-1))<1:\nuse h_max<2c^(m-2), c>2, and Ltree Zgeom>1. By (6.3) such an index\nmust satisfy\n\n N D_i >2( sqrt(2 Ltree Zgeom c^(m-1)/h_i)-1 )^2.   (8.6)\n\nThis necessary intermediate-entropy condition uses that SAME actual\nh_i. It does not assert that such an index occurs. In particular it\ncannot be inferred from an assumed fresh isolated child law.\n\nFor orientation only, (8.1), the accepted ray limit, and the elementary\nlimit (1+1/m)^m->exp(1) give\n\n Theta_m -> exp(1) L_inf t0^2 r_inf /\n                        [2 Z_1(1-r_inf)^2],\n r_inf=2/lambda, L_inf=(2-r_inf)/2.                 (8.7)\n\nNeither this limit nor (8.3) replaces the exact finite threshold in\nthe remaining problem.\n\n## 9. Two direct routes and what remains missing\n\nRoute A attempted to integrate the changing subtree clocks and use\nthe endpoint constraints to force residual entropy. Equations (4.1)\nand (4.2) succeed exactly. The positive occupations (7.1)--(7.3) show\nthat their linear endpoint relaxation permits D_n=0. This is a scoped\nfailure of that relaxation, not evidence that the actual endpoint is\nzero. The scalar test in Section7 separately defeats replacing\nchronological nonlinear updates by their total effective time.\n\nRoute B attempted to obtain the threshold from survival of the deepest\ninitial field. The conditional decomposition is exact, and (6.1)\nsupplies a quantitative change-of-measure bound. The finite price in\nSection8 shows that persistence alone cannot make the deepest-pair\ncertificate sufficient. Its amplification alternative needs an actual\nconditional overstep and the intermediate entropy required by (8.6).\nNeither amplification nor its absence has been established on H_m.\nThis route does not control the contribution of the other levels.\n\nThe remaining inequality for the completely specified trajectory is\n\n sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^(m-j)log cosh T_(n,j)\n             >= Ltree t0^2 r Zgeom^2 c^m/(2B_(2^m-1))              (9.1)\n\nfor every sufficiently large m, or a strict eventual reverse inequality\nfor this same object. T, A, and Q in (9.1) must be generated by\n(3.1)--(3.6), with initial values (3.3) and the original physical word.\nA next estimate must control the chronological conditional partition\nchanges or the inter-iterate conditional KL payments across the large\nsteps and their retained suffixes. A bound on final mass alone, a\nuniform-reset clock, or a deepest-coordinate persistence statement\ndoes not fill this gap. No global or trajectory-specific entropy\nmonotonicity has been assumed.\n\n## 10. Retrieval and scope record\n\nThe verified graph was searched before reasoning; no accepted Gibbs\nterminal-entropy or subtree-clock fact was returned. The first paper's exact PDF\nwas downloaded to downloads/asadi_abbe_multiscale_entropy_2006.14614.pdf.\nIts AppendixA.1 Lemma2 proof and Lemmas6--7 were read through the web\nPDF text. (1)\n\nThus the required object is in the original coordinates, with no\nrescaling of Lambda, physical steps, or M_prim. The project retains\nW=2R. (2)\n\nFor the joint derivation allow 99/100<=t<1, and set\n\n    delta=1-t, X=t a, Y=t b, C=1+X+Y,\n    d=rho C-delta,\n    h=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y),\n    A=2C+d=lambda C-delta=a+t(lambda p+1).              (3)\n\nThe concrete output means precisely t=99/100, delta=1/100 in every\nformula below. There are no parameters to be fitted or solved for.\n\nHere are elementary strict bounds used throughout:\n\n    7/5<a<71/50,  8/5<b<161/100,  3<p<303/100,\n    397/100<C<403/100,\n    1<X<71/50, 1<Y<161/100,\n    3/2<d<17/10,   5/2<1+d<27/10,\n    39/100<d/C<21/50,\n    (29/50)C<C-d<(61/100)C<3.                          (4)\n\nFor completeness, F(u)=u^2+a u-2(1+a) is increasing for u>0.\nF(8/5)=14/25-(2/5)a<0. Since\nrho>2/5, rho<21/50, delta<=1/100 and C>397/100,\n\n    d>(2/5)(397/100)-1/100>3/2,\n    d<(21/50)(403/100)<17/10,\n    d/C>2/5-1/397>39/100.\n\nThe remaining bounds in(4) follow. Put S=X+Y=C-1 and\n\n    u=2S(1+X)/Y=2(p/b)(1+t a),\n    v=2SC/Y=2(p/b)C,\n    w0=2-X^2=2delta(1+t),\n    w1=(S/Y)[2-Y^2+(2-Y)X]\n       =2(p/b)delta(1+lambda t). Verdict and exact scope\n\nThere are exactly TWO actual design mechanisms in this pilot.\n\n1. For every m>=128\n   a rigorously selected backward branch gives terminal value log cosh1,\n   an explicit nonzero initial state, and its full original Euclidean\n   radius. It is strictly below the same-word\n   canonical Huber value. This is a limitation of the selected branch.\n\nA general coercive preimage argument is a preliminary to mechanism2,\nnot a third candidate. It proves existence but its generic norm bound\ncannot certify B_n V>=1 for ANY terminal point on these words. The\nunrestricted cheapest-preimage problem, including other branches for\nour explicit terminal point, remains substantively BLOCKED. Neither\nthe whole tree function nor its arbitrary initial states are excluded.\n\nThe original all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile Goal remains OPEN.\nEven a successful tree construction on the diagnostic words would not\nprove the required uniform statement over all words and all horizons.\n\n## 2. Original words and original all-split benchmark\n\nFix an integer m>=128 for the rest of the document. Put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt2, beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1) h_i.\n\nAll children retain these SAME enclosing-m parameters. Solving the\nrecursion for the sum, or counting the copies of each bridge, gives\n\n 1+S=N+(beta/c)(c^m-N)/(c-2)\n     =(1-zeta)N+zeta*c^m,\n zeta=beta/[c(c-2)].                                      (2.1)\n\nThe largest physical step is hmax=1+beta*c^(m-2). Since\nlambda(lambda-2)=1, one has 0<zeta<1 and S<c^m.\n\nRetain the ORIGINAL recurrences, for integers k in the indicated ranges:\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_t=max_(0<=k<t) Omega(C_k,C_(t-k-1)),\n B_t=max_(0<=k<t) G(C_k,B_(t-k-1)),\n Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n U_t^ZJ=1/B_t.                                            (2.2)\n\nThere is no surrogate recurrence or new benchmark evaluation here.\nAccepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives, with the SAME B_t,\n\n B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1,\n B_(2^m-1)<=Z_1*lambda^m,\n 0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000<33/8.              (2.3)\n\nIndeed B_1=G(1,1)=4. The full statement and its Section7 ray proof were\nread. This accepted fact, not either unverified Gibbs draft, supplies\nthe benchmark limit and enclosure used below.\n\nSeveral elementary bounds will be useful. Write epsilon=1/m. Expanding\nc(c-2) gives\n\n c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2.\n\nFor m>=128, lambda<5/2 implies the last two terms total less than\n8epsilon. Hence\n\n zeta>(1-epsilon)/(1+8epsilon)>=1-9epsilon>=119/128.\n\nAlso c<5/2: for example lambda<29/12 and\n(29/12)(129/128)<5/2. Since c^m>128, (2.1) now gives\n\n             (9/10)c^m<S<c^m.                           (2.4)\n\nFor completeness, the lower bound follows from\nS/c^m>119/128-1/128=59/64>9/10.\n\nThe binomial expansion, already for m>=16, gives\n\n (1+1/m)^m >= 2+(1-1/m)/2\n                   +(1-1/m)(1-2/m)/6\n             >=2+15/32+35/256>13/5.\n\nBecause lambda^m>32, (2.1)-(2.3) imply the convenient finite screen\n\n S/lambda^m>(119/128)(13/5)-1/32=1527/640,\n B_n/(2S)<1320/1527<7/8.                                  (2.5)\n\nFinally zeta->1, (2/c)^m->0, and\nm log(1+1/m)->1 show\n\n S/c^m->1, c^m/lambda^m->exp(1),\n B_n/(2S)->Z_1/(2 exp(1))<99/128<1.                       (2.6)\n\nFor the displayed rational gap, use Z_1<33/8 and exp(1)>8/3.\nThe logarithmic limit follows, for example, by integrating\n1-u<=1/(1+u)<=1 between0 and1/m.\n\n## 3. The fixed function, sharp smoothness, and true minimum\n\nSet r_tree=2/c and\n\n Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) r_tree^j,\n w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom.\n\nFor every binary word v of length j<m use an orthonormal coordinate e_v.\nFor a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m) define\n\n a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))*e_(s_1...s_j),\n F_m(x)=log[N^(-1)sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)].                      (3.1)\n\nThis is exactly the fixed function in TASK.md. Each feature has norm1.\nThe feature mean is zero. Distinct coordinate rows of the feature\nmatrix are orthogonal: their supports are disjoint, or the descendant\nrow sums to zero inside a region on which the ancestor row is constant.\nEach row has positive squared norm. The matrix therefore has rank N-1.\n\nJensen gives F_m(x)>=0, with equality only when all leaf logits are\nequal. Their sum is zero; rank then implies x=0. Thus the WHOLE\nminimizer set is {0} and the minimum is0. In particular every radius\nbelow is the actual Euclidean distance to the unique minimizer.\n\nFor finite x define p_s(x)=exp(<a_s,x>)/sum_t exp(<a_t,x>) and\ng(x)=sum_s p_s(x)a_s. Differentiating the finite sum gives\n\n grad F_m=g, Hess F_m=sum_s p_s a_s a_s^T-g g^T.            (3.2)\n\nThe Hessian is a covariance, hence positive semidefinite. For a scalar\nrandom variable T in [b,d], averaging (T-b)(d-T)>=0 yields\nVar(T)<=(d-E T)(E T-b)<=(d-b)^2/4. Applying this in every unit direction\nbounds the covariance by one quarter of the squared feature diameter.\n\nIf two leaves first separate at depth j, their squared distance is\n\n                 4w_j+2sum_(ell>j)w_ell.\n\nThe quarter-distances strictly decrease with j, because their successive\ndifferences are w_j-w_(j+1)/2>0. Consequently\n\n              Ltree=(1+w_0)/2=(Zgeom+1)/(2Zgeom)            (3.3)\n\nis a global gradient Lipschitz constant. It is sharp. The logit map\nis onto the zero-mean subspace, by the rank calculation. Prescribe equal\nlogits on two leaves separating at the root and logits lower by T on\nthe others, and subtract the common mean. These are realized by a finite\nx. As T tends to infinity the covariance tends to that of the equal\ntwo-point distribution, whose variance in the feature-difference\ndirection is Ltree. No new fitting problem is hidden in this argument.\n\nThe full physical iteration throughout is\n\n             x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Ltree)g(x_i).                (3.4)\n\nFor a requested smoothness L>0 take the project objective\nf=(L/Ltree)F_m. It is L-smooth and (3.4) uses exactly steps h_i/L.\nThe actual normalized score is\n\n                    V=2F_m(x_n)/(Ltree||x_0||^2).         (3.5)\n\nLegality includes every original interpolation row. To reproduce this\nwithout importing a draft, fix x and set k(u)=F_m(u)-<g(x),u>.\nConvexity makes x a minimizer of k. Integrating its Lipschitz gradient\nalong a step of length1/Ltree gives\nk(y-grad k(y)/Ltree)<=k(y)-||grad k(y)||^2/(2Ltree).\nComparison with k(x) yields\n\n F_m(y)>=F_m(x)+<g(x),y-x>\n                         +||g(y)-g(x)||^2/(2Ltree).\n\nAfter division by Ltree this is every directed normalized original\nQ_ij<=0, including both terminal orientations and all optimum rows.\nThere is no independently prescribed jet or label transition.\n\nWe will use\n\n 9/2<Zgeom<6,  1/2<Ltree<1,\n 11/4<Ltree*Zgeom<7/2,\n 4/7<kappa_m:=c/(Ltree*Zgeom)<10/11.                       (3.6)\n\nFor the upper bound, Zgeom<c/(c-2)<=lambda/(lambda-2)\n=lambda^2<6. For the lower bound, c<5/2 gives r_tree>4/5;\nthe first11 terms have sum greater than 5(1-(4/5)^11)>9/2. The last strict inequality\nis the exact integer inequality10*4^11<5^11. The other assertions\nfollow from (3.3) and2<c<5/2. In particular\n\n Zgeom->lambda^2, Ltree*Zgeom->2+sqrt2,\n                       kappa_m->1/sqrt2.                 (3.7)\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: actual symmetric inverse branches with paid norm\n\nFor a fixed depth d in {0,...,m-1} define the full vector X_d(y) by\n\n X_d(y)_v=y/sqrt(w_d) if |v|=d, and0 otherwise.\n\nExactly half the leaves have logit+y and half have logit-y, so\nF_m(X_d(y))=log cosh y. Every depth-d subtree has the same partition\nsum. Thus gradients at ancestors vanish. Below depth d, each relevant\npair of halves has equal logits and gradients vanish. At a node of\ndepth d its Gibbs subtree mass is2^(-d), and its left-minus-right\nconditional mass is tanh y. Therefore\n\n g(X_d(y))_v=2^(-d)sqrt(w_d)tanh y for |v|=d,\n g(X_d(y))_v=0 at other depths.                            (4.1)\n\nThis proves invariance at EVERY physical query for any new starting y.\nIt proves the norm identity as well:\n\n ||X_d(y)||^2=2^d y^2/w_d=Zgeom*c^d*y^2.                  (4.2)\n\nDefine q_d=1/(Ltree*Zgeom*c^d) and alpha_i=q_d h_i. The exact\nscalar physical recurrence and its original score are\n\n y_(i+1)=y_i-alpha_i tanh y_i,\n                 V=2q_d log cosh(y_n)/y_0^2.             (4.3)\n\nFor every d>=m-3, all alpha_i<1. Indeed\n\n alpha_i<=hmax/(Ltree*Zgeom*c^(m-3))\n          =(c^(3-m)+beta*c)/(Ltree*Zgeom)<1,               (4.4)\n\nbecause the numerator is less than1/4+5/2=11/4 and the denominator\nexceeds11/4 by (3.6).\n\nHere is a concrete backward construction, choosing d=m-1 and y_n=1.\nFor i=n-1,...,0 let y_i be the unique positive solution of\n\n                     y_i-alpha_i tanh y_i=y_(i+1).        (4.5)\n\nExistence and the branch are proved, not assumed: the function\ns-alpha_i tanh s has derivative at least1-alpha_i>0, maps the real\nline onto itself because its difference from s is bounded by alpha_i,\nand is zero at0. At s=y_(i+1) its value is smaller than y_(i+1),\nwhile at s=y_(i+1)+alpha_i it is larger. Thus\n\n             y_(i+1)<y_i<y_(i+1)+alpha_i.\n\nThis also makes (4.5) an explicit inverse-branch specification using a\nunique root in a displayed bounded interval. Define x_0=X_(m-1)(y_0).\nBy (4.1), its actual forward trajectory is x_i=X_(m-1)(y_i), and\n\n x_n=X_(m-1)(1), F_m(x_n)=log cosh1,\n 1<y_0<1+q_(m-1)S,\n R_init^2=Zgeom*c^(m-1)*y_0^2\n          <Zgeom*c^(m-1)*(1+q_(m-1)S)^2.                  (4.6)\n\nThis is a new initial point and its own true norm. The old all-left\nlog2 radius is not used. Every scalar state is in the SAME F_m.\n\nLet qdeep=q_(m-1). The exact score and explicit positive bound are\n\n V_sym=2qdeep log cosh1/y_0^2\n        >2qdeep log cosh1/(1+qdeep S)^2.                  (4.7)\n\nIt is useful to screen this whole stratum before further work. For\nANY nonzero initial y_0 at ANY d>=m-3, oddness allows y_0>0.\nEquation (4.4) implies 0<y_n<=...<=y_0. With t=y_n and T=q_d S,\n\n       y_0=t+sum_i alpha_i tanh y_i>=t+T tanh t.\n\nFor t>0, log cosh t<t tanh t, since their difference has derivative\nt sech^2 t>0 and is0 at0. Also (t+T tanh t)^2>=4T t tanh t.\nCombining these inequalities with (4.3) proves the ACTUAL upper bound\n\n              V<1/(2S), hence B_n V<7/8.                 (4.8)\n\nThis is an obstruction for these specified invariant strata and all\ntheir initial states, not only for a selected lower-bound certificate.\nIt says nothing about depths d<=m-4 with overstepping, simultaneous\nfields at different depths, or general starts.\n\nThe constructed branch does have the correct exponential order:\nusing qdeep*c^m=kappa_m, equations (2.6),(3.7),(4.7)-(4.8) give\n\n liminf B_n V_sym >= (Z_1/exp(1))\n                 *[sqrt2 log cosh1/(1+1/sqrt2)^2]>0,\n limsup B_n V_sym <=Z_1/(2 exp(1))<1.                     (4.9)\n\nNo limit of y_0 is asserted or needed. The early screen (4.8) stops\nthis subfamily without pursuing a sharper scalar optimum.\n\n## 5. Mechanism2 preliminary: full preimages exist, but a generic price fails\n\nThe finite-Gibbs function satisfies ||g(x)||<=1 and F_m(x)<=||x||,\nbecause all features have norm1. For any terminal point y and any\nphysical step h>=0 set tau=h/Ltree and minimize over the full space\n\n             Psi_y(x)=||x-y||^2/2-tau F_m(x).\n\nThe quadratic term dominates the displayed linear upper bound on F_m,\nso Psi_y is coercive and continuous and has a global minimizer. At\nevery minimizer its gradient vanishes, which says\n\n                      x-tau g(x)=y.                     (5.1)\n\nThus every one-step map is onto even when its long step is noninjective.\nIterating this existence proof backwards gives an actual complete\norbit to ANY prescribed terminal point z. Its original radius obeys\n\n       ||x_0||<=||z||+sum_i h_i/Ltree=||z||+A,\n                         A:=S/Ltree.                    (5.2)\n\nThe same bound holds for every preimage, by telescoping its actual\nforward path and using ||g||<=1. It is not an invertibility assertion.\n\nThe normalized payoff certified by (5.2) alone is\n\n       Cgeneric(z)=2F_m(z)/(Ltree(||z||+A)^2).\n\nFor t=||z||, F_m(z)<=t and (t+A)^2>=4tA show\n\n     Cgeneric(z)<=1/(2S),\n                     B_n Cgeneric(z)<7/8                 (5.3)\n\nfor EVERY terminal z. This rules out this proof certificate at benchmark\nscale; it is NOT an upper bound on the actual score of an arbitrary\npreimage. Merely citing global solvability and (5.2) cannot solve the\nassigned diagnostic target.\n\nThe next section selects and prices a concrete full-path branch. It is\nthe actual construction within mechanism2. It does not assume that\nits selected preimages are the global minimizers used in this preliminary.\n\n## 6. Mechanism2: one global backward branch concentrated at a leaf\n\nFix the all-left leaf s0=0^m and its unit feature v=a_(s0). Define\n\n A_i=sum_(k=i)^(n-1) h_k/Ltree, 0<=i<=n, so A_0=A and A_n=0,\n ellN=log N,\n t=A+2ellN, D=A+ellN,\n z=t v, u_i=(t+A_i)v,\n delta_min=2w_(m-1),\n Econc=(N-1)exp(2-t delta_min).                               (6.1)\n\nThe centers u_i are determined by the WHOLE original physical word.\nThey are reference vectors for a proved construction, not alleged GD\nstates. The terminal point will be exactly z.\n\nFor a leaf s!=s0 first separating at depth j,\n\n <v,a_s>=sum_(ell<j)w_ell-w_j,\n delta_j:=1-<v,a_s>=2w_j+sum_(ell>j)w_ell.                 (6.2)\n\nThe successive differences delta_j-delta_(j+1)=2w_j-w_(j+1)>0,\nso the smallest gap is delta_min. If ||x-u_i||<=1, then\n\n <v-a_s,x> >= (t+A_i)delta_j-||v-a_s||\n             >=t delta_min-2.\n\nThe total inactive Gibbs mass is at most Econc. Consequently, on EVERY\none of these balls,\n\n        ||g(x)-v||<=2*Econc,  ||Hess F_m(x)||op<=4*Econc.           (6.3)\n\nThe first bound follows by summing p_s(a_s-v). For the second, in\nany unit direction, variance is at most the second moment about v;\n||a_s-v||<=2 and only inactive leaves contribute. This proves the\nHessian bound directly from (3.2), with no concentration assumption\non an unknown orbit.\n\nHere Econc is quantitatively tiny. Equations (2.4),(3.6) give\n\n t delta_min>=2S w_(m-1)/Ltree\n             =(S/c^m)kappa_m N>(18/35)N>N/2.              (6.4)\n\nAlso A<2c^m<2*3^m. Thus\n\n A*Econc<2*6^m exp(2-N/2)<exp(2m+3-2^(m-1))<1/1000.           (6.5)\n\nFor the second inequality use2<exp(1) and6<exp(2). For the third,\n2^(m-1)>=2m+16 for every m>=8, by induction starting at8, and\nexp(13)>2^13>1000. In particular A>1 and Econc<1/1000 as well.\nThese are analytical inequalities, not host numerical evaluations.\n\nOn the finite product of closed Euclidean balls\n\n          Ball=product_(i=0)^(n-1) {x:||x-u_i||<=1},\n\nuse the maximum coordinate norm. Define the simultaneous map\n\n       T(X)_i=z+sum_(k=i)^(n-1)(h_k/Ltree)g(X_k).          (6.6)\n\nEquations (6.3)-(6.5) give\n\n ||T(X)_i-u_i||<=2A_i Econc<=2A*Econc<1,\n ||T(X)-T(Y)||max<=qfix||X-Y||max,\n                         qfix=4A*Econc<1.                    (6.7)\n\nThe Hessian bound applies along every line segment between two points\nin the same ball, since the ball is convex. Thus (6.7) is one bound\nfor the entire simultaneous path map. No product of loose one-step\ninverse constants is used.\n\nThis defines an explicit initial point. Start X^(0)_i=u_i and set\nX^(r+1)=T(X^(r)) for integer r>=0. The iterates stay in Ball. Successive\ndifferences are bounded by qfix^r times the first difference, so the\ngeometric series makes them a Cauchy sequence in the finite product\nspace. They converge to a fixed point (x_0,...,x_(n-1)), with error\n\n ||X^(r)-x||max <= 2A*Econc*qfix^r/(1-qfix).                    (6.8)\n\nContinuity gives the fixed-point equation. Applying (6.7) to two fixed\npoints proves uniqueness WITHIN Ball. Define x_n=z. Subtracting the\nfixed-point equations at consecutive indices, including the last one,\nnow proves exactly\n\n                 x_i-x_(i+1)=(h_i/Ltree)g(x_i)\n\nfor every physical i. Thus the limit construction is an ACTUAL GD\norbit for the fixed F_m, not a system of independent inverse choices.\nNo convergence iteration has been run on the host.\n\nWrite eps_path=2A*Econc. At the fixed point, (6.7) sharpens to\n\n ||x_i-u_i||<=2A_i Econc,\n           2D-eps_path<=R_init:=||x_0||<=2D+eps_path.      (6.9)\n\nIn particular x_0!=0. This is the original full Euclidean radius.\nIts scale is S, quite different from the old log2-spine initial radius.\nThe proof worked in the whole feature space and discarded no component.\n\n## 7. Exact terminal value and actual benchmark failure of this branch\n\nThere are exactly2^(m-j-1) leaves first separating from s0 at depth j.\nDefine the explicit finite positive sum\n\n Rleaf=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) 2^(m-j-1)exp(-t delta_j).           (7.1)\n\nThe actual terminal value, not an auxiliary entropy, is exactly\n\n F_m(x_n)=F_m(t v)=t-log N+log(1+Rleaf)\n                      =D+log(1+Rleaf),\n                  0<Rleaf< Econc.                           (7.2)\n\nThe strict upper bound follows from Rleaf<= (N-1)exp(-t delta_min)\n=exp(-2)Econc. Let V_leaf denote the score of the initial point defined\nby (6.6)-(6.8). Its exact value and two-sided estimates are\n\n V_leaf=2[D+log(1+Rleaf)]/(Ltree||x_0||^2),\n\n 1/[2(S+Ltree log N)(1+Econc)^2]\n       <=V_leaf\n       <=(1+Econc)/[2(S+Ltree log N)(1-Econc)^2]\n       <=(1+5*Econc)/[2(S+Ltree log N)].                      (7.3)\n\nTo check the estimates, eps_path/(2D)=A*Econc/D<=Econc, D>1, and\nlog(1+Rleaf)<=Rleaf<Econc. For0<=Econc<=1/8 the elementary inequality\n(1+Econc)/(1-Econc)^2<=1+5*Econc follows after multiplication by (1-Econc)^2:\nthe remaining expression is Econc(2-9*Econc+5*Econc^2)>=0.\n\nThese bounds prove an actual performance statement. From (2.5),(6.5),\n\n       B_n V_leaf <(7/8)(1+5/1000)<9/10                 (7.4)\n\nfor every m>=128. Furthermore Econc->0 and log N/S->0, so (2.6),(7.3)\ngive the exact asymptotic ratio\n\n           B_n V_leaf -> Z_1/(2 exp(1))<99/128<1.        (7.5)\n\nThe price that prevents success is proved, not merely suspected:\nR_init/(2S/Ltree)->1 while F_m(x_n)/(S/Ltree)->1.\n\nThis branch also lies strictly below the same-word canonical Huber\nvalue1/(2S+1). Indeed (6.5), Ltree<1, and Econc<1/1000 give\n5*Econc(2S+1)<15/1000<1. Thus\n\n (1+5*Econc)(2S+1)<2S+2<2S+2Ltree log N,\n\nwhere Ltree log N>1 follows from m>=128, Ltree>1/2 and log2>1/2.\nCombining with (7.3) proves\n\n                       V_leaf<1/(2S+1).                  (7.6)\n\nConsequently it cannot improve the complete K channel of accepted\nfactb98bb807d514e80d or the complete alternating-ray channel of accepted\nfact1fca3e5dd555cb4a: each includes the canonical Huber witness. These\ntwo accepted facts are used only for this optional reference comparison. No\ncomparison with the full complex channel is asserted, and none is\nneeded for the original-B failure (7.4). This does not replace any\ncomplete reference by a selected path or a universal lens.\n\n## 8. Counterexample checks for the remaining inverse problem\n\nNeither injectivity nor norm contractivity of a long step is available,\neven on a simple slice of this SAME function. At root-only points\nx=X_0(y), the exact map is\n\n           y -> map_root(y)=y-alpha_root tanh y,\n              alpha_root=hmax/(Ltree Zgeom).\n\nHere hmax>9 and Ltree Zgeom<7/2 by (3.6), so alpha_root>2.\nSince map_root(y)/y tends to1-alpha_root<-1 as y tends to0,\nsmall nonzero actual root states satisfy |map_root(y)|>|y|.\n\nAlso map_root is odd, is negative immediately to the right of0, and\nmap_root(alpha_root)=alpha_root(1-tanh alpha_root)>0. The intermediate\nvalue theorem gives a positive gamma<alpha_root with map_root(gamma)=0.\nThus 0, gamma, and -gamma are three distinct actual preimages of0.\nAn inverse choice can have positive radius while its target is0.\nThis refutes universal uniqueness and universal inverse norm\ncontractivity. It is not a counterexample to existence of a better\nselected branch, and terminal0 is not used as a payoff witness.\n\nTwo direct attempts at the missing useful norm estimate are now\ndistinguished. The universal bounded-gradient price (5.2) gives the\ncertificate obstruction (5.3). Selecting a fully coherent exposed-leaf\nbranch avoids that logical gap, but (6.9)-(7.5) show that its ACTUAL\nprice is also too high. The three-preimage example prevents silently\nextending either conclusion to all branches.\n\n## 9. The substantive BLOCKED interface and stopping scope\n\nLet Gamma_m be the composition of all actual maps in (3.4), in their\noriginal chronological order. For z!=0 define\n\n    Rmin_m(z)=min {||x|| : Gamma_m(x)=z}.                  (9.1)\n\nThis minimum exists: Section5 proves the set nonempty, it is closed by\ncontinuity, and every preimage lies in the ball of radius||z||+A by\nthe forward telescope. Also0 is not a preimage of nonzero z. A useful\nglobal branch would need, for an explicitly chosen nonzero z,\n\n               Rmin_m(z)^2 <= 2B_n F_m(z)/Ltree.          (9.2)\n\nFor the entirely explicit terminal point z=(A+2log N)v of Section6,\nthis is the concrete still-open price\n\n Rmin_m(z)^2 <= (2B_n/Ltree)[A+log N+log(1+Rleaf)].         (9.3)\n\nThe constructed branch has radius squared asymptotic to4A^2. The\nright side of (9.3), divided by that radius squared, tends to\nZ_1/(2 exp(1))<1. Therefore a successful different branch for this\nsame terminal point would require a fixed relative improvement in\nradius, not a negligible repair of the contraction estimate. Existence\nof such a branch is NOT established or refuted here. Different terminal\npoints may also matter.\n\nThis global payment estimate is substantively BLOCKED. It is not\ncalled a routine inverse lemma, and no all-family upper bound is\ninferred. Root/depth strata with overstepping, several simultaneous\ndepths, and global branch changes outside the concentration balls\nremain open. The original diagnostic payoff and the unrestricted\nall-word bridge are not solved.\n\n## 10. No fact_id and no formal grant. All premises required for this component are reproduced below; the accepted benchmark input is explicitly cited.\n\n## 2. Original words and original all-split benchmark\n\nFix an integer m>=128 for the rest of the document. Put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt2, beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1) h_i.\n\nAll children retain these SAME enclosing-m parameters. Solving the\nrecursion for the sum, or counting the copies of each bridge, gives\n\n 1+S=N+(beta/c)(c^m-N)/(c-2)\n     =(1-zeta)N+zeta*c^m,\n zeta=beta/[c(c-2)].                                      (2.1)\n\nThe largest physical step is hmax=1+beta*c^(m-2). Since\nlambda(lambda-2)=1, one has 0<zeta<1 and S<c^m.\n\nRetain the ORIGINAL recurrences, for integers k in the indicated ranges:\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_t=max_(0<=k<t) Omega(C_k,C_(t-k-1)),\n B_t=max_(0<=k<t) G(C_k,B_(t-k-1)),\n Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n U_t^ZJ=1/B_t.                                            (2.2)\n\nThere is no surrogate recurrence or new benchmark evaluation here.\nAccepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives, with the SAME B_t,\n\n B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1,\n B_(2^m-1)<=Z_1*lambda^m,\n 0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000<33/8.              (2.3)\n\nIndeed B_1=G(1,1)=4. The full statement and its Section7 ray proof were\nread. This accepted fact, not either unverified Gibbs draft, supplies\nthe benchmark limit and enclosure used below.\n\nSeveral elementary bounds will be useful. Write epsilon=1/m. Expanding\nc(c-2) gives\n\n c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2.\n\nFor m>=128, lambda<5/2 implies the last two terms total less than\n8epsilon. Hence\n\n zeta>(1-epsilon)/(1+8epsilon)>=1-9epsilon>=119/128.\n\nAlso c<5/2: for example lambda<29/12 and\n(29/12)(129/128)<5/2. Since c^m>128, (2.1) now gives\n\n             (9/10)c^m<S<c^m.                           (2.4)\n\nFor completeness, the lower bound follows from\nS/c^m>119/128-1/128=59/64>9/10.\n\nThe binomial expansion, already for m>=16, gives\n\n (1+1/m)^m >= 2+(1-1/m)/2\n                   +(1-1/m)(1-2/m)/6\n             >=2+15/32+35/256>13/5.\n\nBecause lambda^m>32, (2.1)-(2.3) imply the convenient finite screen\n\n S/lambda^m>(119/128)(13/5)-1/32=1527/640,\n B_n/(2S)<1320/1527<7/8.                                  (2.5)\n\nFinally zeta->1, (2/c)^m->0, and\nm log(1+1/m)->1 show\n\n S/c^m->1, c^m/lambda^m->exp(1),\n B_n/(2S)->Z_1/(2 exp(1))<99/128<1.                       (2.6)\n\nFor the displayed rational gap, use Z_1<33/8 and exp(1)>8/3.\nThe logarithmic limit follows, for example, by integrating\n1-u<=1/(1+u)<=1 between0 and1/m.\n\n## 3. The fixed function, sharp smoothness, and true minimum\n\nSet r_tree=2/c and\n\n Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) r_tree^j,\n w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom.\n\nFor every binary word v of length j<m use an orthonormal coordinate e_v.\nFor a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m) define\n\n a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))*e_(s_1...s_j),\n F_m(x)=log[N^(-1)sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)].                      (3.1)\n\nThis is exactly the fixed function in TASK.md. Each feature has norm1.\nThe feature mean is zero. Distinct coordinate rows of the feature\nmatrix are orthogonal: their supports are disjoint, or the descendant\nrow sums to zero inside a region on which the ancestor row is constant.\nEach row has positive squared norm. The matrix therefore has rank N-1.\n\nJensen gives F_m(x)>=0, with equality only when all leaf logits are\nequal. Their sum is zero; rank then implies x=0. Thus the WHOLE\nminimizer set is {0} and the minimum is0. In particular every radius\nbelow is the actual Euclidean distance to the unique minimizer.\n\nFor finite x define p_s(x)=exp(<a_s,x>)/sum_t exp(<a_t,x>) and\ng(x)=sum_s p_s(x)a_s. Differentiating the finite sum gives\n\n grad F_m=g, Hess F_m=sum_s p_s a_s a_s^T-g g^T.            (3.2)\n\nThe Hessian is a covariance, hence positive semidefinite. For a scalar\nrandom variable T in [b,d], averaging (T-b)(d-T)>=0 yields\nVar(T)<=(d-E T)(E T-b)<=(d-b)^2/4. Applying this in every unit direction\nbounds the covariance by one quarter of the squared feature diameter.\n\nIf two leaves first separate at depth j, their squared distance is\n\n                 4w_j+2sum_(ell>j)w_ell.\n\nThe quarter-distances strictly decrease with j, because their successive\ndifferences are w_j-w_(j+1)/2>0. Consequently\n\n              Ltree=(1+w_0)/2=(Zgeom+1)/(2Zgeom)            (3.3)\n\nis a global gradient Lipschitz constant. It is sharp. The logit map\nis onto the zero-mean subspace, by the rank calculation. Prescribe equal\nlogits on two leaves separating at the root and logits lower by T on\nthe others, and subtract the common mean. These are realized by a finite\nx. As T tends to infinity the covariance tends to that of the equal\ntwo-point distribution, whose variance in the feature-difference\ndirection is Ltree. No new fitting problem is hidden in this argument.\n\nThe full physical iteration throughout is\n\n             x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Ltree)g(x_i).                (3.4)\n\nFor a requested smoothness L>0 take the project objective\nf=(L/Ltree)F_m. It is L-smooth and (3.4) uses exactly steps h_i/L.\nThe actual normalized score is\n\n                    V=2F_m(x_n)/(Ltree||x_0||^2).         (3.5)\n\nLegality includes every original interpolation row. To reproduce this\nwithout importing a draft, fix x and set k(u)=F_m(u)-<g(x),u>.\nConvexity makes x a minimizer of k. Integrating its Lipschitz gradient\nalong a step of length1/Ltree gives\nk(y-grad k(y)/Ltree)<=k(y)-||grad k(y)||^2/(2Ltree).\nComparison with k(x) yields\n\n F_m(y)>=F_m(x)+<g(x),y-x>\n                         +||g(y)-g(x)||^2/(2Ltree).\n\nAfter division by Ltree this is every directed normalized original\nQ_ij<=0, including both terminal orientations and all optimum rows.\nThere is no independently prescribed jet or label transition.\n\nWe will use\n\n 9/2<Zgeom<6,  1/2<Ltree<1,\n 11/4<Ltree*Zgeom<7/2,\n 4/7<kappa_m:=c/(Ltree*Zgeom)<10/11.                       (3.6)\n\nFor the upper bound, Zgeom<c/(c-2)<=lambda/(lambda-2)\n=lambda^2<6. For the lower bound, c<5/2 gives r_tree>4/5;\nthe first11 terms have sum greater than 5(1-(4/5)^11)>9/2. The last strict inequality\nis the exact integer inequality10*4^11<5^11. The other assertions\nfollow from (3.3) and2<c<5/2. In particular\n\n Zgeom->lambda^2, Ltree*Zgeom->2+sqrt2,\n                       kappa_m->1/sqrt2.                 (3.7)\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: actual symmetric inverse branches with paid norm\n\nFor a fixed depth d in {0,...,m-1} define the full vector X_d(y) by\n\n X_d(y)_v=y/sqrt(w_d) if |v|=d, and0 otherwise.\n\nExactly half the leaves have logit+y and half have logit-y, so\nF_m(X_d(y))=log cosh y. Every depth-d subtree has the same partition\nsum. Thus gradients at ancestors vanish. Below depth d, each relevant\npair of halves has equal logits and gradients vanish. At a node of\ndepth d its Gibbs subtree mass is2^(-d), and its left-minus-right\nconditional mass is tanh y. Therefore\n\n g(X_d(y))_v=2^(-d)sqrt(w_d)tanh y for |v|=d,\n g(X_d(y))_v=0 at other depths.                            (4.1)\n\nThis proves invariance at EVERY physical query for any new starting y.\nIt proves the norm identity as well:\n\n ||X_d(y)||^2=2^d y^2/w_d=Zgeom*c^d*y^2.                  (4.2)\n\nDefine q_d=1/(Ltree*Zgeom*c^d) and alpha_i=q_d h_i. The exact\nscalar physical recurrence and its original score are\n\n y_(i+1)=y_i-alpha_i tanh y_i,\n                 V=2q_d log cosh(y_n)/y_0^2.             (4.3)\n\nFor every d>=m-3, all alpha_i<1. Indeed\n\n alpha_i<=hmax/(Ltree*Zgeom*c^(m-3))\n          =(c^(3-m)+beta*c)/(Ltree*Zgeom)<1,               (4.4)\n\nbecause the numerator is less than1/4+5/2=11/4 and the denominator\nexceeds11/4 by (3.6).\n\nHere is a concrete backward construction, choosing d=m-1 and y_n=1.\nFor i=n-1,...,0 let y_i be the unique positive solution of\n\n                     y_i-alpha_i tanh y_i=y_(i+1).        (4.5)\n\nExistence and the branch are proved, not assumed: the function\ns-alpha_i tanh s has derivative at least1-alpha_i>0, maps the real\nline onto itself because its difference from s is bounded by alpha_i,\nand is zero at0. At s=y_(i+1) its value is smaller than y_(i+1),\nwhile at s=y_(i+1)+alpha_i it is larger. Thus\n\n             y_(i+1)<y_i<y_(i+1)+alpha_i.\n\nThis also makes (4.5) an explicit inverse-branch specification using a\nunique root in a displayed bounded interval. Define x_0=X_(m-1)(y_0).\nBy (4.1), its actual forward trajectory is x_i=X_(m-1)(y_i), and\n\n x_n=X_(m-1)(1), F_m(x_n)=log cosh1,\n 1<y_0<1+q_(m-1)S,\n R_init^2=Zgeom*c^(m-1)*y_0^2\n          <Zgeom*c^(m-1)*(1+q_(m-1)S)^2.                  (4.6)\n\nThis is a new initial point and its own true norm. The old all-left\nlog2 radius is not used. Every scalar state is in the SAME F_m.\n\nLet qdeep=q_(m-1). The exact score and explicit positive bound are\n\n V_sym=2qdeep log cosh1/y_0^2\n        >2qdeep log cosh1/(1+qdeep S)^2.                  (4.7)\n\nIt is useful to screen this whole stratum before further work. For\nANY nonzero initial y_0 at ANY d>=m-3, oddness allows y_0>0.\nEquation (4.4) implies 0<y_n<=...<=y_0. With t=y_n and T=q_d S,\n\n       y_0=t+sum_i alpha_i tanh y_i>=t+T tanh t.\n\nFor t>0, log cosh t<t tanh t, since their difference has derivative\nt sech^2 t>0 and is0 at0. Also (t+T tanh t)^2>=4T t tanh t.\nCombining these inequalities with (4.3) proves the ACTUAL upper bound\n\n              V<1/(2S), hence B_n V<7/8.                 (4.8)\n\nThis is an obstruction for these specified invariant strata and all\ntheir initial states, not only for a selected lower-bound certificate.\nIt says nothing about depths d<=m-4 with overstepping, simultaneous\nfields at different depths, or general starts.\n\nThe constructed branch does have the correct exponential order:\nusing qdeep*c^m=kappa_m, equations (2.6),(3.7),(4.7)-(4.8) give\n\n liminf B_n V_sym >= (Z_1/exp(1))\n                 *[sqrt2 log cosh1/(1+1/sqrt2)^2]>0,\n limsup B_n V_sym <=Z_1/(2 exp(1))<1.                     (4.9)\n\nNo limit of y_0 is asserted or needed. The early screen (4.8) stops\nthis subfamily without pursuing a sharper scalar optimum.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "8fab4ac8728aa9a6", "finding_ids": ["d23006aab2b6e9be"], "fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "attempt_type": "direct", "subgoal": "M1 actual depth-symmetric backward branch with competitive payoff", "status": "partial", "key_stuck_points": ["This entire sign-preserving deep-stratum family is strictly below benchmark;
