This report supplies\nan equality chart and an exact subthreshold starting point, not an\nimproving seed or a result on the original all-word minimax Goal.\n\nThe accepted correctness inputs are facts04219554e41d9750, Section10,\nand1252b8823bb5a290, first proof paragraph, for the original coefficient\nidentity and interpolation orientation. The threshold is the one in\naccepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e. Findings c6572c6316ca1876 and\nc556fffde4a2c43e are discovery/context only. All new algebra, coverage,\ndegeneracy analysis, and starting-point estimates used here are proved\ntogether in this evidence, including the reproduced derivation in\nAppendix A. No unverified finding is used as a predecessor fact.\n\n## 1. Exact object and the two divergence equations\n\nFor an integer r>=1, let h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) be an original nonnegative\nphysical word, s=sum_i h_i, A=1+s, n=r+1, and e the all-ones vector in\nR^n. All n original formal gradients remain separate variables. Define\n\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t if t<i, T_it=0 if t>i,\n    z=T e, so z_i=1+sum_(t<i)h_t and z_r=A,\n    Z_diag=diag(z),\n    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,\n    D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2)).               (1)\n\nFor every real n-by-n array Lambda with zero diagonal, set\n\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    M=D+(L T+T^T L^T)/2.\n\nThe equality space of interest is exactly\n\n    L^T e=omega,                 M e=0.             (2)\n\nThe original certificate additionally requires Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY\ni!=j and M>=0 on ALL of R^n. At this stage the off-diagonal entries\nare allowed to be arbitrary real numbers so that coverage of the whole\nequality space, before inequalities, can be established.\n\nFor any zero-diagonal real matrix X define two divergences\n\n    d(X)=X e-X^T e,\n    d_z(X)=X z-Z_diag X^T e=d(X Z_diag).                      (3)\n\nBoth have coordinate sum zero. The weighted matrix in (3) is X Z_diag,\nwhose (i,j) entry is X_ij z_j; it is NOT Z_diag X. No division by z_i is\ninvolved.\n\nThe elementary identity driving the construction is\n\n    2D e+T^T omega=-Z_diag omega.                         (4)\n\nHere is every coordinate of its verification. For i<r put\np_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, so z_i=1+p_i. Then\n\n    (T^T omega)_i=-2h_i+h_i sum_(k=i+1)^r omega_k\n                 =-2h_i+2h_i(p_i+h_i).\n\nAdding 2h_i(2-h_i) gives 2h_i(1+p_i)=-z_i omega_i.\nAt i=r the left side is -2s(s+2)+2s=-2s(s+1), also\n-z_r omega_r. Thus (4) holds even at zero steps.\n\nIf d(Lambda)=L^T e=omega, then\n\n    2M e=L z-Z_diag omega=-d_z(Lambda).                   (5)\n\nIndeed L z=Z_diag Lambda e-Lambda z, so\nZ_diag d(Lambda)-L z=Lambda z-Z_diag Lambda^T e. It follows that\n\n    (2) is equivalent to d(Lambda)=omega,\n                         d_z(Lambda)=0.             (6)\n\nNo equation has been dropped: the equivalence holds coordinate by\ncoordinate on all n vertices. The two zero-sum identities are the\nonly redundant equations on the nondegenerate domains proved below.\n\nNor does (2) impose an additional restriction on an original PSD\ncertificate. Under value flow one has\n\n    e^T D e=-(s^2+sum_i h_i^2),\n    z^T omega=s^2+sum_i h_i^2,\n    e^T M e=0.                                      (7)\n\nFor the second equality use\n2 sum_i h_i sum_(t<i)h_t=s^2-sum_i h_i^2. If a symmetric\nmatrix M is PSD and e^T M e=0, then for every real vector v the\nnonnegative quadratic (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) has zero constant term;\nits linear coefficient 2e^T M v must be zero. Hence M e=0.\nConsequently retaining (2), all original signs, and full PSD is exactly\nequivalent to the original full certificate conditions at the same h.\n\n## 2. General tree chart: explicit formulas and both directions\n\nFix ANY undirected spanning tree on {0,...,r}. Give each of its r\nedges an orientation u_l -> v_l. Delete that edge and let S_l be\nthe component containing u_l. Write\n\n    Delta_l=z_(u_l)-z_(v_l).\n\nThe chart domain is\n\n    U_tree={h: Delta_l!=0 for every tree edge l}.     (8)\n\nThe original positive-word domain (0,infinity)^r is contained in\nU_tree for every tree, since z_0<...<z_r. The formulas also apply to\nany nonnegative boundary point lying in U_tree. More generally their\nalgebra is valid for real h in (8), though signed physical steps are\noutside the original problem.\n\nFor EACH ordered off-diagonal pair whose unordered pair is not a tree\nedge, introduce an INDEPENDENT real coordinate N_ij. There are\n\n    n(n-1)-2r=r(r-1)\n\nsuch coordinates. Define N to have these entries, zero diagonal, and\nzero on both directions of each tree edge. For each tree cut compute\n\n    alpha_l=sum_(i in S_l) omega_i\n             -sum_(i in S_l,j not in S_l)(N_ij-N_ji),\n    beta_l=-sum_(i in S_l,j not in S_l)\n                                (N_ij z_j-N_ji z_i). (9)\n\nTerms with tree pairs in these sums are zero by the definition of N.\nThe chart Phi_tree(h,N) is the following COMPLETE array:\n\n    Lambda_ij=N_ij                         off the tree,\n    Lambda_ii=0,\n    Lambda_(u_l,v_l)=(z_(u_l) alpha_l-beta_l)/Delta_l,\n    Lambda_(v_l,u_l)=(z_(v_l) alpha_l-beta_l)/Delta_l. (10)\n\nOnly the explicitly nonzero Delta_l are denominators. In particular\nthere is no denominator h_i-1, h_i-2, 1-product(1-h_i), determinant\nof an unspecified matrix, or fitted completion.\n\nProof of the construction and coverage. For an edge oriented u->v\nwith coefficients a=Lambda_uv and b=Lambda_vu, its contributions to\nthe two divergences in (6) are, respectively,\n\n    (a-b)(e_u-e_v),\n    (z_v a-z_u b)(e_u-e_v).                         (11)\n\nSumming either divergence over S_l cancels all edges internal to\nS_l or to its complement. The ONLY tree edge crossing S_l is edge l.\nThus (6) requires exactly\n\n    a-b=alpha_l,        z_v a-z_u b=beta_l.           (12)\n\nSolving this displayed two-by-two system with Delta_l!=0 gives (10).\nConversely those formulas satisfy BOTH equations in (12) exactly.\n\nIt remains to prove that all these cut equations force every vertex\nequation, rather than only some aggregation. Let q be either residual\nvector d(Lambda)-omega or d_z(Lambda). Its coordinate sum is zero.\nIf every tree cut sum of q is zero, choose a root for the tree.\nEvery subtree cut has zero sum: it is either S_l or its complement,\nand q has total sum zero. A nonroot leaf therefore has q_i=0.\nInducting upward, the subtree sum at a nonroot vertex and all its\nchild subtree sums being zero imply q_i=0. The root then follows\nfrom total sum zero. This proves q=0, and therefore (6) and (2).\n\nNow start with ANY original real Lambda satisfying (2) at h in\nU_tree. Read off all its off-tree ordered entries to form N. Its tree\ncoefficients satisfy (12) by summing the actual vertex equations.\nThe determinant of (12) is z_v-z_u=-Delta_l, which is nonzero, so\neach pair is exactly (10). Thus the inverse is simply\n\n    (h,Lambda) -> (h,(Lambda_ij on all off-tree ordered pairs)). (13)\n\nEquations (10) and (13) are inverse in both directions. They cover\nthe WHOLE equality space, not just arrays reached from an initial\ncurve or through a restricted type of correction. For each fixed h\nin U_tree the equality space is an affine space of dimension r(r-1).\nThe equality map has rank 2r. When h also varies, (10) is a smooth\nrational chart with r+r(r-1)=r^2 independent real coordinates.\nFor r=5 this means 20 independent coefficient coordinates and five\nindependent physical steps. All statements about dimension concern\nthe equality space; the sign/PSD feasible subset is still constrained.\n\nThe tree is a basis for equality elimination ONLY. Non-tree entries\nare not set to zero in Lambda; only the auxiliary matrix N sets the\ndependent tree positions to zero. Opposite non-tree directions are\nindependent. There is no reflection condition or repeated first row.\n\n## 3. The requested length-five chart in original index order\n\nUse the path order (0,1,2,5,4,3), oriented in that order. Its data are\n\n    l   (u_l,v_l)    S_l                   Delta_l\n    0   (0,1)        {0}                   -h_0\n    1   (1,2)        {0,1}                 -h_1\n    2   (2,5)        {0,1,2}               -(h_2+h_3+h_4)\n    3   (5,4)        {0,1,2,5}              h_4\n    4   (4,3)        {0,1,2,5,4}            h_3.         (14)\n\nThe first term of alpha_l in (9) is, in the same order,\n\n    -2h_0, -2(h_0+h_1), -2(h_0+h_1+h_2),\n                  2(h_3+h_4), 2h_3.                  (15)\n\nTogether (1), (9), (10), (14), and (15) are finite explicit formulas\nfor all ten dependent ordered entries in terms of h and the twenty\nindependent ordered entries. In particular no generic LinearSolve\noperation is needed.\n\nFor clarity the ten output locations are, successively,\n(01,10), (12,21), (25,52), (54,45), (43,34). In each pair the first\nentry is (z_u alpha-beta)/Delta and the second is\n(z_v alpha-beta)/Delta, with its corresponding row in (14).\n\nOn nonnegative physical words the exact nonsingular domain of THIS\npath is h_0,h_1,h_3,h_4>0 and h_2>=0. On the specified positive-word\ndomain all five h_i>0, all denominators are nonzero. A box such as\nh_i>=1/4 is an optional SEARCH restriction. It would bound their\nabsolute values below by1/4 (and the middle one by3/4), but it is\nnot a lossless reduction of the original nonnegative-step problem.\nNo upper capacity or step box has been proved or imposed here.\n\n## 4. Every degeneracy of the tree completion, and boundary coverage\n\nFirst keep an arbitrary fixed tree and h, allowing Delta_l=0.\nThe cut equations (12) remain necessary and sufficient: the subtree\nproof never divided by Delta. If Delta_l=0, put c_l=z_u=z_v. Then\nthe exact condition on the independent data at this edge is\n\n    beta_l=c_l alpha_l.                              (16)\n\nIf (16) fails, these particular off-tree coordinates admit NO\ncompletion on this tree. If it holds, the entire solution at this\nedge is\n\n    Lambda_vu=t_l,       Lambda_uv=t_l+alpha_l,\n    t_l any real number.                             (17)\n\nDifferent degenerate edges introduce independent t_l. At every\nnondegenerate edge use (10). These rules and (16) characterize ALL\nsolutions at the fixed h and fixed off-tree data, including every\nsimultaneous degeneracy. Sign conditions on (17), when required, are\nt_l>=0 and t_l+alpha_l>=0, not automatic consequences of (16).\n\nHere is the actual kernel, not just a determinant diagnosis. With h\nand all off-tree entries fixed, a homogeneous change must have zero\nnet flow on each tree cut, hence delta Lambda_uv=delta Lambda_vu.\nIts second cut equation is then -Delta_l delta Lambda_uv=0.\nTherefore the kernel has exactly one basis direction per equal-\npotential tree edge, adding the same number to its two ordered\ncoefficients. There are no other kernel directions. The map from\nthe 2r tree coefficients to the pair of zero-sum divergence vectors\nhas rank 2r-m when exactly m tree differences vanish.\n\nAn explicit diagnostic illustrates why dividing through zero or\ndeclaring every off-tree coordinate free at that point is wrong.\nIn the requested path take h=(0,1,1,1,1), so z=(1,1,2,3,4,5).\nTake N_02=1 and every other off-tree entry zero. At edge(0,1),\nalpha_0=-1 and beta_0=-2, whereas c_0 alpha_0=-1. Thus no\ncompletion exists for these off-tree data. Independently, the\nhomogeneous change delta Lambda_01=delta Lambda_10=t preserves\nboth divergence equations because z_0=z_1. These are an actual\ncompatibility obstruction and actual kernel for THIS singular\ncoordinate choice, not an obstruction to another chart or to the\noriginal certificate cone.\n\nFor every nonzero nonnegative word there is an explicit alternative\nnonsingular tree retaining all original vertices, even when some\nphysical steps vanish. Since s>0, z_0=1 and z_r=1+s are distinct.\nUse the double-star tree\n\n    {0,r};\n    {0,i} for 1<=i<r with z_i>1;\n    {r,i} for 1<=i<r with z_i=1.                      (18)\n\nEvery vertex other than the two centers is a leaf, and the centers\nare joined. Thus (18) is connected with r edges and no cycle. Its\npotential differences are all nonzero: -s on (0,r), 1-z_i on\n(0,i), and s on (r,i). The general formula (10) applies without\nidentifying any two formal gradients. This constructs a covering\nfamily of charts for every h>=0 with s>0. On overlaps, changing\ncharts is simply reconstructing the full Lambda by (10) and taking\nthe other chart's off-tree entries by (13); coverage is exact.\n\nThe remaining stratum is the allzero word h=0. Here T=I, z=e,\nD=0, omega=0, and d_z(Lambda)=d(Lambda). The two systems coincide:\nthe only equality is balanced flow d(Lambda)=0. All equations (16)\nhold automatically because beta_l=alpha_l for every edge. Take all\nr(r-1) off-tree coordinates and r additional t_l freely, and apply\n(17). This gives exactly the whole equality space of dimension r^2\n(25 when r=5), whereas the nonzero-word fibre has dimension r(r-1).\nThe equality rank is r at h=0 and 2r at each nonzero nonnegative h,\nthe latter by (18) and Section2. This rank jump explains why a single\nchart with only r(r-1) coefficient coordinates cannot cover the\norigin fibre as an independent-coordinate bijection. It is not a\nreason to delete a zero-step query.\n\nFor completeness, when h=0 and Lambda>=0 is balanced, M=sym(L)\nis already the undirected Laplacian with weights\n(Lambda_ij+Lambda_ji)/2, so is PSD: its quadratic form is the sum\nof these weights times (g_i-g_j)^2 over i<j. This special boundary\nobservation is not a PSD shortcut at nonzero words. If r=0, the\nempty word has no off-diagonal coefficients, one formal gradient,\nand the one-by-one matrices Lambda=M=0; it is handled separately.\n\nThese exhaust degeneracies of the stated tree formulas and all\nnonnegative-word strata. No exceptional divisions arise at steps1\nor2, repeated positive step values, or a singular PSD remainder.\n\n## 5. All signs and the FULL PSD condition remain\n\nOn any nonsingular chart impose\n\n    N_ij>=0 on EVERY independent ordered pair,\n    (z_u alpha_l-beta_l)/Delta_l>=0,\n    (z_v alpha_l-beta_l)/Delta_l>=0 on EVERY tree edge. (19)\n\nThere are exactly n(n-1) original sign inequalities. Depending on\nthe sign of Delta, multiplying one through by Delta reverses its\nsense; retaining the displayed quotient avoids that ambiguity.\nNo sign of a final tree entry is promised for arbitrary N.\n\nThe full symmetric matrix is always formed by the original formula\nin Section1. Let B be its leading r-by-r principal block and let\none_r be the all-ones vector in R^r. Because M e=0 identically,\n\n    M = [[B, -B one_r],\n         [-one_r^T B, one_r^T B one_r]]\n      = J B J^T,       J=[[I_r],[-one_r^T]].         (20)\n\nThis follows by solving each of the first r row sums for its final\nentry and the final row sum for the final diagonal. Consequently,\nfor EVERY original formal-gradient coordinate vector g,\n\n    g^T M g=(g_(0:r-1)-g_r one_r)^T\n                   B(g_(0:r-1)-g_r one_r).          (21)\n\nThus M>=0 on R^(r+1) if and only if B>=0 on R^r. One direction\nalso follows by taking g=(y,0); the other follows from (21).\nThe variables have not been identified: (21) evaluates the original\nform for arbitrary independent g_0,...,g_r. Furthermore B>0 if and\nonly if M is positive definite on e-perp and ker(M)=span{e}.\nIndeed the difference vector in (21) vanishes exactly on span{e}.\n\nFor completeness the exact Cholesky equivalence, INCLUDING singular\nboundary matrices, is\n\n    M>=0  <=>  B>=0  <=>  B=C C^T\n    for some real lower triangular r-by-r C with C_jj>=0. (22)\n\nA finite construction proving the last implication is as follows.\nProcess columns j=0,...,r-1. Set\n\n    p_j=B_jj-sum_(k<j) C_jk^2,\n    a_ij=B_ij-sum_(k<j) C_ik C_jk  for i>j.\n\nIf p_j>0 take C_jj=sqrt(p_j) and C_ij=a_ij/C_jj. If p_j=0,\nrequire a_ij=0 for all i>j and take the whole remaining part of\ncolumn j, including its diagonal, equal to zero. If p_j<0, or if\np_j=0 with some nonzero a_ij, reject PSD. Set C_ij=0 for i<j.\n\nTo prove this algorithm covers all PSD matrices, suppose the active\ntrailing residual S is PSD. If its leading pivot p>0, for any y the\nminimum of (x,y)^T S(x,y) over x is\ny^T(S_tail-a a^T/p)y; hence that Schur residual is PSD. If p=0,\nnonnegativity of (t,e_i)^T S(t,e_i) for every real t forces the\ncorresponding a_i=0; the trailing principal submatrix is PSD.\nThis proves the assertion inductively. Conversely completing the\nrecursion gives B=C C^T entry by entry and therefore PSD. The same\ntwo tests prove that a rejection rules out PSD. No divisions by\nzero are concealed. Strictly positive diagonal factors describe\nonly B>0 and must not be presented as coverage of the whole cone.\n\nAt fixed h the entries of B are affine in the independent N entries,\nso the exact fibre of feasible certificates is given by the linear\nsign inequalities (19) and B>=0. When h varies the displayed\nfunctions are rational; no convexity or improved convergence of\nthe joint search is claimed. Introducing a factor C and imposing\nB=C C^T is an optional equivalent description, with all zero-pivot\ncases allowed. It introduces additional variables/equalities and\ndoes not replace the actual matrix formula by a freely chosen PSD\nremainder. Nothing here sets M=0 or imposes its rank.\n\n## 6. Exact starting coordinates and inverse-coordinate readback\n\nAll formulas in this section specify ONE starting point. They place\nno relations on the five physical steps or twenty coefficient\ncoordinates in the general chart. To reproduce the known dense\npoint from scratch, define\n\n    a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, rho=a-1,\n    b=(-a+sqrt(10+8a))/2, p=a+b,\n    t=99/100, delta=1/100,\n    X=t a, Y=t b, S=X+Y, C0=1+S,\n    d=rho C0-delta,\n    h_*=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y), A_*=2C0+d,\n    c=(C0+d)/(C0-d), k=1+2c,\n    tau=delta(2a C0-delta)/(C0-d), eta=2tau,\n    u=2S(1+X)/Y, v=2S C0/Y,\n    epsilon=1/10000000.                              (23)\n\nC0 is a child capacity, not the matrix C in (22) or the benchmark\nsequence. Appendix A uses the letter C for this same child capacity;\nit never uses that letter for a Cholesky factor.\n\nThe twenty independent starting coordinates are especially short:\n\n    N_32=N_35=2cX+epsilon,\n    N_42=2cY+epsilon,\n    every other off-tree ordered N_ij=epsilon.       (24)\n\nThe full list of twenty locations in original lexicographic order is\n\n    02,03,04,05; 13,14,15; 20,23,24;\n    30,31,32,35; 40,41,42; 50,51,53.\n\nIn particular there are seventeen entries equal to epsilon. Denote\nthis independent array by N_*. At this\npoint\n\n    z=(1,1+X,C0,C0+1+d,A_*-Y,A_*)^T,\n    Zsum=sum_i z_i=2+C0+3A_*-2Y,\n    K=6I-ee^T,\n    F_l=sum_(i in S_l)(K z)_i,\n    w_l=F_l/Delta_l,       gamma_l=1-w_l. Written without a cut\noperation, the w_l are\n\n    w_0=(Zsum-6)/X,\n    w_1=[2Zsum-6(2+X)]/Y,\n    w_2=[3Zsum-6(2+X+C0)]/(C0+d),\n    w_3=[6(2+X+C0+A_*)-4Zsum]/Y,\n    w_4=[Zsum-6(C0+1+d)]/X.                          (26)\n\nUsing (24), the alpha and beta from (9) are EXACTLY\n\n    l  alpha_l                    beta_l^0\n    0  -2X                         0\n    1  -2S                         0\n    2   2c(d-1)                    2c C0 S\n    3   2kS-2cY                    2c(C0 S+A_* X)\n    4   2kX                        2cX(C0+A_*),\n\n    beta_l=beta_l^0-epsilon Delta_l gamma_l. Thus the contribution is\nepsilon(F_l-Delta_l)=-epsilon Delta_l gamma_l, as claimed.\n\nInserting (27) into (10) returns\n\n    Lambda_01=2+epsilon gamma_0,\n    Lambda_10=2(1+X)+epsilon gamma_0,\n    Lambda_12=u+epsilon gamma_1,\n    Lambda_21=v+epsilon gamma_1,\n    Lambda_25=2cd+eta+epsilon gamma_2,\n    Lambda_52=2c+eta+epsilon gamma_2,\n    Lambda_54=kv+epsilon gamma_3,\n    Lambda_45=ku+2cY+epsilon gamma_3,\n    Lambda_43=2k(1+X)+epsilon gamma_4,\n    Lambda_34=2k+epsilon gamma_4. Neither\nits readback nor the chart is a proof that the earlier local-search\nfailure has been diagnosed or cured.\n\n## 7. Finite implementation specification; no execution\n\nThe following is pseudocode for the requested original-index path.\nIt is supplied as text only; it has NOT been run or numerically\nevaluated during this round.\n\n    input h[0..4], twenty real coordinates N at the listed locations\n    make a 6-by-6 zero matrix N and insert those twenty coordinates\n    z[0] = 1\n    for i = 0..4: z[i+1] = z[i] + h[i]\n    omega = (-2h[0],...,-2h[4],2*sum(h))\n    Lambda = N\n    for (u,v,S) in the five rows of (14):\n        Delta = z[u]-z[v]\n        alpha = sum(omega[i], i in S)\n                 -sum(N[i,j]-N[j,i], i in S, j outside S)\n        beta = -sum(N[i,j]*z[j]-N[j,i]*z[i],\n                                              i in S, j outside S)\n        if Delta == 0:\n            return OUTSIDE_THIS_NONSINGULAR_CHART, with the exact\n            compatibility condition and free variable from (16)-(17)\n            do not output a completed certificate from this routine\n        otherwise:\n            Lambda[u,v] = (z[u]*alpha-beta)/Delta\n            Lambda[v,u] = (z[v]*alpha-beta)/Delta\n    form T,D,L and the FULL M from (1) and Section1\n    retain all thirty inequalities Lambda[i,j] >= 0 for i != j\n    take B = leading 5-by-5 block of the actual M\n    retain B >= 0, equivalently the full PSD condition by (20)-(22)\n    output h,Lambda,M,B and the original capacity A=1+sum(h)\n\nAll sums contain finitely many explicitly bounded terms (at most\nnine crossing unordered pairs in any length-five cut). There is\nno unspecified inverse or auxiliary unknown. A straightforward\ngeneral-tree implementation costs at most O(r^3) arithmetic\noperations using all cut sums and ordinary dense matrix products;\nat r=5 the arrays have fixed sizes. This complexity estimate is a\ncount of the displayed operations, not a performed computation.\n\nFor a boundary word, either use the complete formulas (16)-(17)\nwith their additional inputs and compatibility conditions, or\nselect the tree (18) FIRST and supply its own independent coordinates.\nAn incompatible off-tree assignment cannot be repaired merely by\nrenaming its entries as coordinates of another tree. The transition\nmap described in Section4 applies to a known full equality array.\n\nFor a future search one can use h and N directly, or translate\nthe twenty coordinates as N=N_*+xi with independent xi. One must\nnot continue imposing h=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y), equal paired coordinates,\nor the symmetric epsilon filling during that general search:\nthese were formulas for the SINGLE starting point only. Original notation and the concrete word\n\nNormalize smoothness to1. All six formal gradients g_0,...,g_5 remain\nindependent variables. Set\n\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,-2h_4,2s)^T,\n    D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<5,-s(s+2)),\n    M_prim=D_prim+sym(L_flow T),  sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2.\n\nThe accepted coefficient identity is\n\n    L_flow^T e=omega  ==>  V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n                          =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_5)>. {"id": "4f623d6a2c835679", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T01:42:44.022289+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round50 joint DRAFT: arbitrary-template primitive inflation remains OPEN. Exact N+1=(r+1)(s+1), capacity AC and energy decomposition isolate a collective block-interpolation debt; two nonnegative exact-flow lifts fail PSD. A static quadratic with h=q=[sqrt2], both full M=0, refutes the virtual-oracle shortcut even with child-energy payment. Full basic-template closure is proved with explicit arrays; its repeated exponent restricts the template, not arbitrary length5/6 or M=0 seeds.", "evidence": "# Arbitrary-template primitive inflation: exact interface and scope\n\nStatus: joint analytical DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4's one authorized\nround50 under TASK.md and guidance1c516691cc4c3011. No mathematical evaluator,\nsolver, replay, machine algebra, formal submission, consult, or subagent is\nauthorized or used. The unrestricted inflation claim is not established by\nthe results below. The original exact-profile Goal remains separate and open.\n\nThe mathematical predecessors are accepted04219554e41d9750, proof\nSections10--13, and accepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e, statement and proof Section6.\nThe former supplies the exact primitive identity and full binary join;\nthe latter uses arbitrary seeds inside those basic trees. Neither accepted\nstatement asserts arbitrary-template inflation. Every new calculation below\nis reproduced jointly; no unverified shared finding is used as a premise.\n\n## 1. Original full certificate and the physical word\n\nFor a nonnegative word w of length n, W_n(w) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite Euclidean\ndimensions, all convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz\ngradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under\nx_(t+1)=x_t-(w_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 and the empty word is allowed.\nThis is the project's W=2R normalization. The letter q below follows\nTASK.md and means the child WORD, not a scalar derivative from another\nfact. Other locally defined letters carry the scopes explicitly given.\n\nNormalize the smoothness to1. For a word w=(w_0,...,w_(n-1)), put\nS_w=sum w_t, K_w=1+S_w, x_(t+1)=x_t-w_t g_t, and\n\n Q^w_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_w=2 sum_(t<n) w_t(f_t-f_n)\n       +sum_(t<n)w_t(1-w_t)||g_t||^2-S_w(S_w+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nAll finite ordered interpolation residuals Q^w_ij are nonpositive on an\nactual convex differentiable1-smooth function. Let Lambda^w have zero\ndiagonal and nonnegative entries on every ordered distinct pair. With\ne the all-ones column, T^w_tt=1, T^w_tu=w_u for u<t, zero for u>t, set\n\n L^w=diag(Lambda^w e)-Lambda^w,\n omega^w=(-2w_0,...,-2w_(n-1),2S_w)^T,\n D^w=diag(w_0(2-w_0),...,w_(n-1)(2-w_(n-1)),-S_w(S_w+2)),\n M^w=D^w+(L^w T^w+(T^w)^T(L^w)^T)/2.\n\nThe assumed input conditions are (L^w)^T e=omega^w and M^w PSD on the\nwhole R^(n+1). Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives the polynomial identity\n\n V_w+sum_(i!=j)Lambda^w_ij Q^w_ij=<M^w,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.       (1)\n\nThere is no quotient at a zero step, no restriction to a gradient rank,\nand no support, product, reflection, or multiplier symmetry assumption.\n\nLet h have length r, h_i>=1, capacity A=1+sum h_i, and a full input\ncertificate as above. Let q have length s, nonnegative steps, capacity\nC=1+sum q_a, and an arbitrary full input certificate. Define\n\n beta_i=1+C(h_i-1),\n H=(q,beta_0,q,beta_1,...,q,beta_(r-1),q).                     (2)\n\nThere are r+1 child copies, r bridge steps, and exactly\n\n N=(r+1)s+r=(r+1)(s+1)-1\n\nphysical steps. There are N+1=(r+1)(s+1) formal gradients. Index a node\nas (i,a), where 0<=i<=r, 0<=a<=s, at physical index i(s+1)+a. These\ndisjoint child node sets partition the entire formal space. The endpoints\np_i=i(s+1)+s are the bridge queries for i<r and the final query for i=r.\nThe full array has N(N+1) ordered off-diagonal slots. In particular, all\ngradients at zero child steps are retained.\n\nEvery physical step is nonnegative because beta_i>=1. Direct summation gives\n\n 1+sum H=1+(r+1)(C-1)+r+C(sum h_i-r)=AC.                     (3)\n\nIf r=0 the output is q; if s=0 it is h. An all-zero but nonempty q\nstill contributes all its formal gradients and zero physical steps.\n\n## 2. Exact virtual data and energy decomposition\n\nAll child copies are segments of ONE actual GD trajectory for ONE static\nfunction. For block i write z_i for its starting position, y_i=x_(p_i)\nfor its endpoint, e_i=g_(p_i), f_i^e=f(x_(p_i)), and\n\n P_i=sum_(a<s)q_a g_(i,a),\n dinf_i=P_i-(C-1)e_i,\n Dinf_0=0,  Dinf_i=sum_(k=1)^i dinf_k for i>=1.\n\nThus y_i=z_i-P_i and z_(i+1)=y_i-beta_i e_i. Define formal virtual jets\n\n X_i=[y_i+(C-1)e_i+Dinf_i]/C,\n F_i=f_i^e/C+(C-1)||e_i||^2/(2C).                            (4)\n\nTheir positions obey EXACTLY X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i e_i: indeed the change in\nthe numerator is\n\n -beta_i e_i-P_(i+1)+(C-1)(e_(i+1)-e_i)+dinf_(i+1)\n =-[beta_i+C-1]e_i=-C h_i e_i.\n\nThis is a formal algebraic GD relation; no interpolating virtual function\nis asserted. Define the virtual residual\n\n Qhat_ij=F_i-F_j+<e_i,X_j-X_i>+||e_i-e_j||^2/2.\n\nExpanding (4) cancels every extra norm and endpoint inner-product term:\n\n C Qhat_ij=Q^H_(p_i,p_j)+<e_i,Dinf_j-Dinf_i>.                     (5)\n\nLet V_i denote the ORIGINAL primitive energy V_q of block i, relative to\nits own actual endpoint. The following identity is exact on every formal\ngradient and function-value coordinate:\n\n V_H=sum_(i=0)^r V_i+C^2 V_h(F_0,...,F_r;e_0,...,e_r).       (6)\n\nHere the final energy uses the original steps h and the virtual data (4).\nTo check (6), subtract the child energies. The coefficient of f_i^e-f_r^e\nfor i<r is 2[(C-1)+beta_i]=2Ch_i. The coefficient of ||e_i||^2 is\n\n beta_i(1-beta_i)+C(C-1)\n =C^2h_i(1-h_i)+C(C-1)h_i.\n\nThe final coefficient is C(C-1)-(AC-1)AC. Since sum h_i=A-1, this equals\n\n -C(C-1)(A-1)-C^2 A(A-1),\n\nwhich is precisely the final coefficient obtained from C^2 V_h and (4).\nAll internal child coefficients agree without alteration. These checks\nalso apply at zero child steps.\n\n## 3. A full positive-flow candidate, and the exact missing payment\n\nLet E_i be the coordinate restriction from all physical gradients to\nthe s+1 gradients of block i. Let E extract the r+1 endpoint gradients.\nEmbed child arrays in their original coordinate sets and put\n\n Lambda^0=sum_i E_i^T Lambda^q E_i+C E^T Lambda^h E.           (7)\n\nAll off-diagonal entries are nonnegative; coinciding contributions add.\nThe flow is exactly the required physical flow. Internal child nodes\nhave flow -2q_a. At a nonterminal block endpoint, its flow is\n\n 2(C-1)-2C h_i=-2 beta_i.\n\nAt the final endpoint it is 2(C-1)+2C(A-1)=2(AC-1).\n\nLet G be the full physical gradient Gram matrix, and define B as the\nquadratic form\n\n B=C sum_(i!=j)Lambda^h_ij <e_i,Dinf_j-Dinf_i>.                    (8)\n\nCombining (1), (5), and (6), the full remainder of (7) is\n\n V_H+sum Lambda^0_ab Q^H_ab\n =sum_i <E_i^T M^q E_i,G>+C^2<E^T M^h E,G>-B.              (9)\n\nThe first two terms are full-coordinate PSD congruences. The last term\nhas no established sign. Dropping it is an error. Thus (7) is an explicit\npositive-flow lift, but NOT a proved PSD lift.\n\nIt actually fails for the M=0 inputs (13). In their full physical space\nR^4 choose scalar formal gradients (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(0,1,1,0).\nThen e_0=1, e_1=0, dinf_1=a, and (9) equals -2Ca<0. These are formal\ncoordinates used to disprove PSD of (7); they are NOT asserted to be\nan actual function trajectory. A different array certifies the physical\nword, as Section4 shows.\n\nHere is a precise additional sufficient collective payment condition.\nPut K=C^2 sum Lambda^h_ij Qhat_ij. Suppose there are real numbers\n0<=theta_i<=1, an actual ordered array Gamma_ab>=0 with zero diagonal,\nand a matrix P PSD on all physical gradients, such that the following\nPOLYNOMIAL identity holds after the original physical GD substitutions:\n\n K=sum_i theta_i V_i+sum_(a!=b)Gamma_ab Q^H_ab-<P,G>.         (10)\n\nThen a full certificate is explicitly\n\n Lambda^out=sum_i(1-theta_i)E_i^T Lambda^q E_i+Gamma,\n M^out=sum_i(1-theta_i)E_i^T M^q E_i+C^2 E^T M^h E+P.        (11)\n\nIndeed substitute (10) into (6) and use both input identities (1).\nEvery array coefficient in (11) is nonnegative and every matrix summand\nis PSD on the unchanged physical coordinates. Cancellation of all formal\nfunction values implies the exact original flow; the matrix equals the\noriginal D+sym(LT) by (1). This is an EXTRA sufficient matching condition,\nnot a consequence proved from the stated input primitive conditions.\n\nFor an exact formulation with no sufficient-cone narrowing, any array\nwith the physical flow equals Lambda^0+Xi, where Xi is a real zero-\ndiagonal array with zero divergence at every node. Full feasibility is\nequivalent to existence of such Xi with Lambda^0+Xi entrywise nonnegative\noff diagonal and the full quadratic form\n\n sum_i E_i^T M^q E_i+C^2E^T M^h E-B+sum Xi_ab Q^H_ab          (12)\n\nPSD. The last sum is purely quadratic because Xi has zero divergence.\nIn (12), the embedded matrices are evaluated against G; B and the last\nsum denote scalar quadratic forms on the same full gradient coordinates.\nThis is a useful exact residual interface, not a solution of that\nfeasibility problem. It does not discard any original array.\n\n## 4. The virtual-oracle shortcut is false even for two M=0 inputs\n\nPut a=sqrt(2), C=1+a, d=a-1=1/C, and take h=q=(a). Both words have\nthe ORIGINAL full certificate\n\n Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2C, Lambda_00=Lambda_11=0, M=0.       (13)\n\nFor completeness, the flow is (2-2C,2C-2)=(-2a,2a).\nWith T=[[1,0],[a,1]], the first diagonal of D+sym(LT) is\na(2-a)+2(1-a)=2-a^2=0; the second is\n-a(a+2)+2C=0; and twice the off-diagonal is -2+2C(a-1)=0.\nThus (13) verifies the entire two-gradient matrix and both ordered signs.\n\nThe physical output is H=(a,2,a), with length3 and capacity C^2.\nUse the single static convex1-smooth function f(x)=x^2/2, minimizer0,\nand initial point x_0=-C. Its whole actual run is\n\n x_0=-C,  x_1=1,  x_2=-1,  x_3=d,\n g_t=x_t,  f_t=x_t^2/2.\n\nBlock0 ends at e_0=y_0=1. Block1 starts at -1, ends at e_1=y_1=d,\nand has P_1=-a, dinf_1=-a-a d=-2. Consequently the virtual data (4) are\n\n (X_0,F_0,e_0)=(1,1/2,1),\n (X_1,F_1,e_1)=(-d,d^2/2,d).\n\nThey obey X_1=X_0-a e_0, but\n\n Qhat_01=-2d<0,      Qhat_10=2d^2>0.                       (14)\n\nFor the second equality, expansion gives\nQhat_10=(d^2-1)/2+a d+(d-1)^2/2=d(d+a-1)=2d^2.\nFor the first, Qhat_01=(1-d^2)/2-a+(1-d)^2/2=1-d-a=-2d.\n\nThus these virtual jets are NOT smooth-convex interpolable. Both input\ncertificates are M=0 and satisfy the balanced product relation. Neither\nof these additional restrictions rescues this virtual-oracle assertion.\n\nBoth actual child energies are zero: on the unit quadratic the one-step\nenergy is x_start^2-C^2 x_end^2, and C d=1. Every actual Q is zero.\nTherefore even allowing nonnegative payments by child energies cannot\nmake each virtual interpolation residual nonpositive separately: the\npositive Qhat_10 in (14) has zero child payment available. By contrast,\nthe template-weighted sum is\n\n 2 Qhat_01+2C Qhat_10=-4d+4C d^2=0.\n\nThis demonstrates why a potentially viable theorem must control the\nCOLLECTIVE template-weighted expression, not certify a fictitious\nsmooth-convex oracle pair by pair.\n\nThis example is explicitly NOT a counterexample to the physical lift.\nH=(a,2,a) is the accepted binary primitive join of q with itself: the\njoin surplus is1, since 1(2C+1)=C^2. Its full lifted certificate exists\nby accepted04219554e41d9750 Section11. Its actual V_H is zero on this run.\n\n## 5. A sufficient closure in the proved, proper template subclass\n\nCall a template BASIC here if it is obtained by primitive--primitive\nbinary joins starting from empty primitive leaves, with surplus d_v>0\nat every node satisfying d_v(A_left+A_right+d_v)=A_left A_right.\nThis is precisely the primitive tree construction of the accepted fact,\nnot a definition imposed on arbitrary primitive inputs.\n\nFor every such basic template h and every arbitrary full-certificate\nnonnegative child q, the word (2) DOES have a full certificate. Replace\neach of the r+1 empty leaves of its tree by q. At each original node,\nits two child capacities and its surplus are all multiplied by C:\n\n (A_left,A_right,d_v) -> (C A_left,C A_right,C d_v).\n\nThe defining surplus equation and its positive root are preserved.\nThe bridge becomes exactly 1+C(h_i-1), in its original inorder position.\nThe resulting inorder word is (2), and its length and capacity are (3).\n\nHere is the full array transformation, including the sign proof. At\neach transformed node set kappa=((A_left+A_right+d_v)/A_right)^2,\nwhich is unchanged under the common scaling. Embed the complete left\narray with factor1 and right array with factor kappa. Write p for the\nbridge query, z for the node's final query, and b_j for each physical\nright-child step at its nonterminal node j. Add the actual ordered edges\n\n (j,p): (kappa-1)b_j,    (z,p): kappa-1,\n (p,z): (kappa-1)C d_v,  (j,z): (kappa-1)b_j.                (15)\n\nThere are no omitted finite coordinates. All coefficients are\nnonnegative since kappa>1, d_v>0, and the physical b_j>=0. The accepted\nidentity gives exactly\n\n M_parent=E_left^T M_left E_left\n                 +kappa E_right^T M_right E_right,         (16)\n\nwith the original flow and D+sym(LT). Induction proves the full matrix\nPSD and every original ordered sign. This uses one same function on\none actual GD word throughout; there is no oracle reset.\n\nThe template subclass is proper EVEN INSIDE the full M=0 class. For\nh=(3/2,3/2), a full M=0 array is\n\n Lambda=[[0,1,1/2],[7/2,0,17/2],[1,14,0]].                 (17)\n\nIts row-minus-column flow is (-3,-3,6). For\nT=[[1,0,0],[3/2,1,0],[3/2,3/2,1]] and D=diag(3/4,3/4,-15),\nthe diagonal entries of LT are (-3/4,-3/4,15), and the paired\noff-diagonal sums are\n\n (-1-3/4)+(7/4)=0,\n (-1/2)+(1/2)=0,\n (-17/2)+(3/2+7)=0.\n\nThus D+sym(LT)=0 on all three formal gradients; all six ordered entries\nare positive. But every length2 basic primitive tree contains a length1\nempty-leaf join with step sqrt(2), whereas (17)'s word has neither step\nequal to sqrt(2). Hence basic-template closure says nothing by itself\nabout using (17), or every other M=0 word, as an arbitrary template.\n\n## 6. Repetition and the distinction between seed and template\n\nFor a FIXED basic template h of length r>=1 and capacity A, start with\nany fixed nonnegative full primitive child q^(0) of length s and capacity\nC. Recursively use (2) with q=q^(k). Equations(15)--(16) give actual full\ncertificates at every depth; all physical steps remain predetermined\nand nonnegative. The exact counts and capacities are\n\n length(q^(k))+1=(r+1)^k(s+1),\n capacity(q^(k))=A^k C.                                    (18)\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 Section11 makes a primitive word dominant with\nweights (w,sum w+1). Its full objective certificate and the accepted Huber\nwitness therefore give, at every depth and in the original W=2R units,\n\n W_(length(q^(k)))(q^(k))=1/(2A^k C-1).                     (19)\n\nThe upper bound is over every finite dimension and every admissible\nsmooth convex objective. It has no multiplicative loss depending on k.\nRescaling f by L restores arbitrary L>0 and leaves W unchanged.\n\nThe exponent of this family depends on A and r, NOT on the arbitrary\nleaf capacity C. Thus an exponent lower bound applied to (18)--(19)\ncan restrict A as a TEMPLATE capacity in a recursively closed class.\nIt cannot restrict C as an arbitrary fixed SEED capacity. To put C in\nthe exponent one would have to prove the missing theorem allowing that\nseed itself to serve as the template, with all the requisite hypotheses.\n\nMoreover the accepted basic recurrence already gives\nA<=C_r<=(r+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt(2)), for basic h. No external exponent\nbound is needed for that existing basic-template restriction. In\nparticular, (18)--(19) place no new ceiling on an arbitrary length5/6\nprimitive seed or on the full M=0 subclass.\n\n## 7. Source-dependent exponent check, after the sufficient basic closure\n\nThis section assumes an EXTERNAL premise; it is not a Danus fact import.\nSource: Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for\nGradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2 (2026), Theorem1.1 and definition(1),\nhttps://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2 . Its applicable statement is:\nthere are absolute K_YL>0 and n_YL such that every integer n>=n_YL\nand every predetermined h in[0,infinity)^n satisfy\n\n W_n(h)>=n^(-rho-K_YL sqrt(log log n/log n)).                 (20)\n\nIts R_n divides the objective gap by (L/2)||x_0-x_star||^2,\nso R_n there is EXACTLY this project's W=2R. The supremum includes\nall finite dimensions and all minimizers. At L=1 its GD convention\ncoincides with ours. Zero and arbitrarily large nonnegative steps are\nallowed; the theorem is uniform over horizon-dependent words. This is\nthe non-anytime theorem, not the infinitely-many-horizons anytime result.\nThe primary definition, statement, Section3.2 proof and its uniform\nconstant passage were read, including the finite-dimensional construction\nand its distance normalization in Section3.1. Exact HTML/PDF/text copies\nare in downloads/ye_liu_2609.09152v2.*. No formal acceptance is asserted.\n\nHere is the deduction, applied only when actual recursive feasibility\nand (19) have been proved. Put m=r+1>1 and n_k=m^k(s+1)-1. The lower\nbound (20) and (19) would imply\n\n log(2CA^k-1)/log(n_k)\n <=rho+K_YL sqrt(log log(n_k)/log(n_k)).                      (21)\n\nFor fixed finite s,C and A>1, the left side tends to log(A)/log(m),\nwhile the additional term on the right tends to zero. Therefore such\na recursively certified template necessarily obeys\n\n A<=m^rho.                                                  (22)\n\nThis implication assumes (20). It uses every depth of the ACTUAL\nfinite-horizon family, not an anytime embedding or a formal scalar\ncapacity recursion. Fixed prefactors, including C and s+1, disappear\nonly after division by log(n_k); no depth-dependent loss is discarded.\nFor the basic templates proved here, (22) is already contained in the\naccepted scalar bound, so it is not a new restriction. Applying (22)\nto an arbitrary length5/6 seed, or to all M=0 inputs such as (17), would\nassume the very template-closure theorem this pilot has not established.\n\nThe primary background source on composition was also downloaded and\nread: Grimmer, Shu, Wang, *Composing Optimized Stepsize Schedules for\nGradient Descent*, arXiv:2410.16249v2, definitions in Section2.1 and\nProposition6 with its proof. Its positive s-composable schedules include\nan additional product equality. Those definitions are not silently\nidentified with the general full primitive input (1), and no result\nfrom that source is a new proof premise here.\n\n## 8. A second nonnegative-flow formula fails even with M=0 inputs\n\nThe first candidate (7) is not the only tempting generalization of the\nbinary join. The following chronological fan formula has exact physical\nflow and nonnegative entries, and agrees with the accepted lift for the\none-step saturated template. For a general input array Lambda^h put\n\n b_i^back=sum_(j<i)Lambda^h_ij,    mu_i=1+b_i^back.\n\nScale the full child array in block i by mu_i. For each i<j add\nC Lambda^h_ij on the endpoint pair (p_i,p_j). For each i>j add\nLambda^h_ij times each of the two fans\n\n sum_(a<s)q_a Q^H_((i,a),p_j)+Q^H_(p_i,p_j),\n sum_(a<s)q_a Q^H_((i,a),p_i).                              (23)\n\nAll coefficients are nonnegative. The nonterminal child flow is\n-2mu_i q_a+b_i^back q_a+b_i^back q_a=-2q_a. The total flow of\nblock i is C omega_i^h, so the endpoint flow is precisely that of (7).\nThis verifies every required flow equation and sign, without a PSD claim.\n\nTake the M=0 template (17) and the M=0 child q=(sqrt(2)) from (13).\nTheir physical word is\n\n H=(a,(3+a)/2,a,(3+a)/2,a),  a=sqrt(2).\n\nSet all scalar formal endpoint gradients e_0,e_1,e_2 to zero, and let\nthe child starting gradients be u_0,u_1,u_2. Set every formal value to\nzero. Thus dinf_i=a u_i and V_i=a(1-a)u_i^2. Because bridge gradients\nvanish, z_1=y_0 and z_2=y_1=y_0-a u_1.\n\nFor the backward edge1->0, the sum of the two fans in (23) equals V_1:\nthe residual from the block1 start to y_0 is u_1^2/2, and that to its\nown end is (1/2-a)u_1^2, both multiplied by a. The same calculation\ngives V_2 for edge2->1. For edge2->0, the earlier endpoint is\ny_0=z_2+a u_1, so the fan sum is instead\n\n V_2+a^2 u_1u_2=V_2+dinf_1dinf_2.\n\nAll forward endpoint residuals vanish on this formal assignment. The\nchild M matrices and the template M matrix are zero. Identity(6) gives\nV_H=sum V_i. Therefore the complete remainder of (23), after the\nchild scalings cancel, is exactly\n\n Lambda^h_20 dinf_1dinf_2=dinf_1dinf_2.\n\nChoosing (u_0,u_1,u_2)=(0,1/a,-1/a) makes this remainder -1. This proves the baseline margin directly.\n\nTo fill every missing multiplier, let K0=6I-ee^T and\nF_l=sum_(i in S_l)(K0z)_i, w_l=F_l/(z_u-z_w), gamma_l=1-w_l.\nSet H=K0-sum_l w_l(e_u-e_w)(e_u-e_w)^T. Add epsilon on both directions\nof every non-tree pair and epsilon gamma_l on both directions of each\ntree pair. This changes the Laplacian by epsilon H, preserving both\nflow and Me=0. The full changed remainder is M=M0+epsilon sym(HT).\n\nFor an explicit construction let Zsum=2+C0+3A_*-2Y. The five w_l are\n(Zsum-6)/X, [2Zsum-6(2+X)]/Y,\n[3Zsum-6(2+X+C0)]/(C0+d),\n[6(2+X+C0+A_*)-4Zsum]/Y,\n[Zsum-6(C0+1+d)]/X.\nThese specify every entry of the dense array by the preceding additions.\nEach cut has at most9 crossing pairs, each potential difference has\nabsolute value<s<9, and each tree difference has magnitude>1. Thus\n|w_l|<81, |gamma_l|<82. Every original tree coefficient is at least2;\nafter filling, all30 coefficients are >=epsilon. The direct bounds\nc<5/2,k<6,tau<5delta,u<10,v0<16 give all final coefficients<97.\nMoreover ||H||<816 and ||T||<=||T||_F<sqrt141<12, so\n||sym(HT)||<10000. Since epsilon=delta/100000,\nM>(2delta/9-delta/10)P>(delta/9)P=(1/900)P on e-perp.\nThe upper bound ||M0||<342delta follows from four edge forms bounded\nby252delta and the bridge form bounded by90delta; adding the perturbation\ngives M<(7/2)P. For g=(y,0), ||Pg||^2>=||y||^2/6, so B>=I/5400.\nNo numerical evaluation has been used in this derivation.\n\nThe chart's starting coordinates are only\nN_32=N_35=2cX+epsilon, N_42=2cY+epsilon, all other17 equal epsilon.\nThe baseline cut alpha values are -2X,-2S,2c(d-1),2kS-2cY,2kX;\nthe beta values are0,0,2cC0S,2c(C0S+A_*X),2cX(C0+A_*).\nFilling changes beta by -epsilon Delta_l gamma_l and leaves alpha\nunchanged. Substitution into the cut formulas reproduces all ten tree\nentries of the explicit dense array. This proves exact chart readback.\nThe initial numerical job must additionally compare these independently\nconstructed arrays and check the actual original matrix and margins.\n\nIts capacity A_*=a+(99/100)[(1+a)(a+b)+1] is below48763/5000<49/5<Gamma5.\nIt is the required strict start, not a usable improving seed.\n\n## 4. Frozen numerical specification\n\nImplementation: run.wl, executed ONCE by the existing Wolfram evaluator.\nAll machine mathematics, including model construction, initial arrays,\nfactorization, optimization, original readbacks, and encoding, occurs in\nthat single call. No code is evaluated for calibration beforehand.\n\nVariables: five independent h; all20 off-tree ordered coefficients in\nlexicographic order; all15 entries of a lower triangular5-by-5 factor C.\nThe ten dependent Lambda entries use the exact cuts above. No reflection,\nsupport, repeated row, zero remainder, fixed rank, or one-parameter path\nis imposed. The relation h=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y) holds ONLY at the initial point.\nEquality constraints are the15 lower triangular entries of B-CC^T=0.\nAll30 multiplier inequalities remain imposed, including tree entries.\n\nSearch box:1/4<=each h_i<=6;0<=each original Lambda_ij<=400 (i!=j);\n-100<=each lower factor entry<=100, diagonal>=0; additionally every entry\nof the FULL6-by-5 factor JC is in[-100,100]. These are search restrictions,\nnot proved global bounds. The registered objective is maximize1+sum h_i.\n\nMethod: one FindMinimum call on its negative, Method->InteriorPoint,\nWorkingPrecision->MachinePrecision, AccuracyGoal->8, PrecisionGoal->8,\nMaxIterations->600. Initial point is exactly the algebraic specification\nabove evaluated at machine precision; its factor is obtained from the\nactual positive definite leading block. No jitter, alternate start, SDP,\nrepair, grid, precision rerun or second optimization is permitted.\n\nInitial abort tests: finite real arrays of the correct dimensions; full\nflow, Me, direct-array/chart comparison and full factor residual <=1e-9;\nall30 coefficients>=epsilon(1-1e-6); full minimum eigenvalue>=-1e-9;\ncomplement minimum>=1/900-1e-9; leading minimum>=1/5400-1e-9; all search\nboxes and factor diagonal signs. Failure aborts without optimization.\n\nNumerical candidate tests: actual original coefficient signs >=0;\nfull flow and Me infinity norms<=1e-7; full original/factor residual<=1e-7;\nfull minimum eigenvalue>=-1e-8; original/model matrix discrepancy<=1e-8;\nfactor diagonals>=0 and all search boxes. Stop early once such a point has\nA>Gamma5+1e-6. All negative entries and eigenvalues remain visible. This\nis only numerical credibility, never exact PSD or a certificate proof.\nNo exact reconstruction is planned in this job: complete arrays are saved\nfor a separately selected next stage if the candidate test succeeds.\n\nCounters: EvaluationMonitor increments an exact callback counter and\nthrows at2000. StepMonitor increments an exact step counter and performs\none full ORIGINAL-model readback at every accepted step. It throws by598\nsteps, reserving two of the600 total readbacks for initial and final checks.\nThe solver's internal iterations also have MaxIterations600. The initial\nand returned/final point are checked separately; the best checked point\nis retained with its already computed readback, avoiding a redundant call.\n\nTime: one30s charge made before evaluation, no refund. Model build and\noptimizer phase has a23s kernel guard with a22s callback wall guard; full\nreadback and compressed export have an overall26.5s kernel guard and the\nevaluator receives timeConstraint27. Reserve3s for local decoding and\npersistence, enforced by a local SIGALRM. The core deadline is absolute\nfrom call start, not renewed per iteration. Accounted cap is30 regardless\nof early exit; actual kernel, transport wall, and local processing times\nare recorded separately. Unknown-time timeout charges the full30 and\ncloses the ledger. The prior870s conservative reservation becomes900s;\nneither number is represented as measured runtime.\n\nOutput: final full readback FIRST, then best feasible and initial arrays;\nh,Lambda,M,C,JC,J and a fixed orthonormal complement basis; all original\nflows, Me, factor and model residuals, signs and full/complement/leading\neigenvalues; counters, solver output, version, status and every received\nwarning. Objective history is explicitly SAMPLED: step1,every10th step,\nand the final readback. Exact total and omitted counts are supplied.\nThere is no per-callback full-vector dump. The complete last callback\nvector is retained as a trial point, without an unperformed readback.\nLossless gzip/base64 chunks preserve the complete bounded payload; raw\nreceived response is saved before parsing. No clipping, truncation,\nrounding-away of negative values, or reconstructed missing output.\n\n## 5. Retrieval and authority limits\n\nThe shared COMPLETE draft841a70f2ad83841d was read in full and its SHA256\nmatched4e1f0afaa542bb54c0c4e047e72f9a50df6e60011718bd2a1305b8ffa6f4c6b6.\nThe derivation above independently reproduces the needed formulas from\naccepted originals; the shared draft is not promoted to a fact.\nThe known exact local BnB-PEP paper2203.07305v5 was read at Lemma3 and\nAlgorithm1 Stage2 for the factor/interior-point method analogy. No external\ntheorem is imported. Matlas hits on generalized eigenvalues/nonnegative\nrank were unrelated. Official Wolfram FindMinimum and TimeConstrained\ndocumentation was checked. No branch-and-bound or global optimum claim\nis made. All new algebra remains unverified because the current task\nexpressly forbids fact_submit. Failure of this run cannot prove full-cone\nnonexistence or settle the original problem.\n\n\n## Retained file: run.wl\n\nBlock[{$MessageList = {}}, Module[\n {started = AbsoluteTime[], h, nm, nv, la, cv, cm, vv, path, tree,\n  off, pairs, cuts, ee, tt, zz, om, lf, dd, mm, bb, jj, qq, ff,\n  cut, outside, aa, bt, den, uedge, vedge, eq, cons, starts,\n  a0, b0, t0, delta0, x0, y0, s0, cap0, d0, cc0, kk0, tau0,\n  eta0, u0, v0, eps0, h0, n0, direct, z0, sumz0, w0, gam0,\n  initial = {}, initialRead = Null, finalRead = Null, bestRead = Null,\n  finalVector = {}, bestA = -Infinity, lastStep = {}, lastEval = {},\n  lastRead = Null, iter = 0, evals = 0, reads = 0, calls = 0,\n  status = \"building\", stopReason = \"not_started\", initialPassed = False,\n  initialChecks = <||>, directError = Null, sol = Null, ruleSol = {},\n  history = {}, maxTrialCapacity = Null, phaseResult, result, payload,\n  chunks, encoded, read, onEval, onStep, row, realVectorQ,\n  tol = 10^-7, psdtol = 10^-8, gamma = 7977607/806000,\n  tag = \"max3equalityguard\", kernelElapsed, preExportElapsed,\n  modelBuildElapsed = Null, initialElapsed = Null, optimizationElapsed = Null,\n  optimizationStarted = Null, exportStarted, elapsedAtRead,\n  exactAttempted = False},\n TimeConstrained[\n  phaseResult = Catch[TimeConstrained[\n   ee = ConstantArray[1, 6];\n   path = {1, 2, 3, 6, 5, 4};\n   tree = Partition[path, 2, 1];\n   cuts = Table[Take[path, k], {k, 5}];\n   pairs = Select[Tuples[Range[6], 2], #[[1]] != #[[2]] &];\n   off = Select[pairs, ! MemberQ[Sort /@ tree, Sort[#]] &];\n   h = Table[Unique[\"step\"], {5}];\n   nv = Table[Unique[\"offtree\"], {20}];\n   nm = ConstantArray[0, {6, 6}];\n   Do[nm[[off[[k, 1]], off[[k, 2]]]] = nv[[k]], {k, 20}];\n   cm = Table[If[j <= i, Unique[\"factor\"], 0], {i, 5}, {j, 5}];\n   cv = Flatten[Table[cm[[i, j]], {i, 5}, {j, i}]];\n   vv = Join[h, nv, cv];\n   jj = Join[IdentityMatrix[5], {-ConstantArray[1, 5]}];\n   qq = N[Table[Which[i <= j, 1/Sqrt[j (j + 1)],\n           i == j + 1, -j/Sqrt[j (j + 1)], True, 0], {i, 6}, {j, 5}]];\n   tt = Table[Which[i == j, 1, j < i, h[[j]], True, 0], {i, 6}, {j, 6}];\n   zz = tt.ee; om = Join[-2 h, {2 Total[h]}]; la = nm;\n   Do[\n    {uedge, vedge} = tree[[k]]; cut = cuts[[k]];\n    outside = Complement[Range[6], cut];\n    aa = Total[om[[cut]]] - Total[Flatten[Table[nm[[i, j]] - nm[[j, i]],\n                                                {i, cut}, {j, outside}]]];\n    bt = -Total[Flatten[Table[nm[[i, j]] zz[[j]] - nm[[j, i]] zz[[i]],\n                                                {i, cut}, {j, outside}]]];\n    den = zz[[uedge]] - zz[[vedge]];\n    la[[uedge, vedge]] = (zz[[uedge]] aa - bt)/den;\n    la[[vedge, uedge]] = (zz[[vedge]] aa - bt)/den,\n    {k, 5}];\n   lf = DiagonalMatrix[la.ee] - la;\n   dd = DiagonalMatrix[Join[h (2 - h), {-Total[h] (Total[h] + 2)}]];\n   mm = dd + (lf.tt + Transpose[tt].Transpose[lf])/2;\n   bb = mm[[1 ;; 5, 1 ;; 5]]; ff = jj.cm;\n   eq = Flatten[Table[(bb - cm.Transpose[cm])[[i, j]], {i, 5}, {j, i}]];\n   cons = DeleteCases[Join[\n     Thread[eq == 0], Thread[h >= 1/4], Thread[h <= 6],\n     Thread[Extract[la, pairs] >= 0], Thread[Extract[la, pairs] <= 400],\n     Thread[cv >= -100], Thread[cv <= 100], Thread[Diagonal[cm] >= 0],\n     Thread[Flatten[ff] >= -100], Thread[Flatten[ff] <= 100]], True];\n   modelBuildElapsed = AbsoluteTime[] - started;\n\n   realVectorQ[v_List] := VectorQ[v, NumericQ] &&\n      FreeQ[v, Indeterminate | _DirectedInfinity | _Complex];\n   read[values_List, initialFactor_: False] := Module[\n    {rr, vUsed = values, hv, lm, cf, tm, fl, omega, dm, mat, sym,\n     basisFactor, flow, null, fr, mr, pe, ce, ev, eqr, lv, box,\n     diagok, finite, fdiag, feas, coreChecks, me, fe, ne, mre},\n    reads++;\n    If[reads > 600, status = \"readback_cap\"; {"id": "aa3c5e1d91baf405", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T02:12:15.979558+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max53 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED actual tree-projection pilot. On every original H_m,m>=128, the explicit carrier simplex with kappa^2=w0/3 and leaf start has genuine first-H2 face changes but ALL later projections stay below its finite cap; its true-radius score is <=[w0/(3+w0)] beta^(2n)c^(-2m), below COMPLETE K/ray/FULL-complex and with original B_n ratio tending0. Removing the carrier gives a different centered simplex with unique minimum0, exact real projection supports {0,1,2} then {0,1,2,3}, positive normal heights and retained deep state. Its useful terminal payoff remains BLOCKED; full state, full-word identity and a conditional core screen are included.", "evidence": "# Two actual growing tree-simplex projection bodies\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, round53.\nAssignment TASK.md and master_guidance8e797e97b78dcebd authorize ONE\nanalytical round after completed52/attempted53, ceilings53/54. No formal\nverification or fact_submit is authorized or performed. No new fact_id.\n\nTwo concrete bodies are selected, of the permitted maximum three. The\nfirst has a rigorous scoped full-word obstruction. The second has actual\nface changes, an explicit all-query state rule and a priced full-word\ninterface, but no proved useful terminal payoff. A representation or\nfirst-H2 calculation is not claimed to solve that interface. All new\ningredients are proved jointly here; earlier unverified findings are\nawareness only. No mathematical evaluator, numerical run, solver, DP\nexecution, machine algebra, replay, installation, consult, or subagent\nwas used.\n\n## 1. Original words and complete reference scopes\n\nThroughout fix an integer m>=128 and set\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i.\n\nEvery child retains these SAME enclosing-m parameters. In particular\nh_0=1+a, h_1=1+beta, h_2=1+a. Counting each level gives\n\n 1+S=N+(beta/c)(c^m-N)/(c-2)\n     =(1-zeta)N+zeta*c^m<c^m,\n zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).                         (1.1)\n\nIndeed c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1, whereas beta<1.\nFor i>=0 let s2(i) be the number of ones in its binary expansion, with\ns2(0)=0, and define the signed original prefix product\n\n Pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)). (1.2)\n\nTo prove the identity, the original recursive word has\nh_t-1=beta*c^(v2(t+1)-1), where v2(k) is the exponent of2 in k.\nCounting multiples of powers of2 shows\nsum_(k=1)^i v2(k)=sum_(j>=1)floor(i/2^j)=i-s2(i): for a binary\ndigit at position j its contribution to the floor sum is2^j-1.\nMultiplication gives (1.2), including i=0. Consequently\n\n |Pi_i|<=a for 1<=i<=n,\n Qunit:=Pi_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m).                    (1.3)\n\nFor a body K used below, the actual project function is f=L F_K and\nthe actual iterates obey x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F_K(x_i). Define\n\n V_K=2F_K(x_n)/R_K^2,\n R_K=dist(x_0,argmin F_K).                           (1.4)\n\nThis is the fixed W=2R normalization. The sharp global smoothness of\neach selected F_K is1, so no smaller local constant is substituted.\n\nThe accepted reference facts used are the following.\n\n* b98bb807d514e80d: the COMPLETE single-cut functional\n\n K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S), max_(0<=j<n)\n  (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]}.\n\n  It also specifies the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_t=max_(0<=k<t)Omega(C_k,C_(t-k-1)),\n B_t=max_(0<=k<t)G(C_k,B_(t-k-1)),\n Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n U_t^ZJ=1/B_t.\n\n* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: F_ray_n(h) is its maximum over ALL feasible\n  chronological checkpoint subsets, with the original retained offsets,\n  value areas, same-ray monotonicity conditions and terminal q optimization.\n  Its complete class contains K_n(h). No restricted checkpoint list is\n  substituted here. The only comparison needed below is\n\n Huber(h):=1/(1+2S)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h).              (1.5)\n\n* dfde2fa326c908bf: Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),\n  xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z), together with the zero optimum.\n  The FULL legal domain D_h consists of all z=alpha+i beta_complex\n  such that (alpha-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|beta_complex Area_C| for EVERY\n  directed simple cycle of these original query indices and optimum;\n  Energy_C=sum|xi_i-xi_j|^2 and\n  Area_C=sum Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) on the oriented edges j->i.\n  If ell_i is the maximal path weight from i to the optimum for weights\n  <gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2 on j->i, its exact maximal\n  gap is Gap_h(z)=-2ell_n. Write\n\n Cfull(h)=sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z).\n\n  At z=1 all cycle conditions hold, and the upper and lower gap bounds\n  of that accepted fact coincide. Hence\n\n Cfull(h)>=Gap_h(1)=Qunit.                           (1.6)\n\n  This comparison uses a point in the FULL domain, not a claim that a\n  lens or real slice exhausts it.\n\n* 04219554e41d9750, proof(9.3), gives for the same original recurrence\n  B_t<=4(t+1)^(log_2 lambda), in particular B_n<=4lambda^m. Its full\n  statement and the proof's indices were read directly from the fact\n  file. No new recurrence computation or finite-H6 transfer is used.\n\nThus Qunit<c^(-2m)<1/(2c^m)<Huber(h), by (1.1) and c^m>2.\n\n## 2. Projection function, smoothness, minimum, and all original rows\n\nLet K be any nonempty compact convex subset of a Euclidean space with\n0 in K. Define the SAME finite function at every query by\n\n F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nCompleting the square shows that its unique maximizing vector is the\nEuclidean projection g=P_K(x). Existence follows from compactness and\nuniqueness from strict convexity of squared distance. The exact normal\ncondition is\n\n <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in K.                     (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance on the segment\nfrom g to v. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2-||x-g||^2.\nAdding (2.1) for two inputs proves\n<x-x',g-g'>=||g-g'||^2, hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||.\nThe maximizing formulas at x and x+d give\n\n 0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<g,d><=<P_K(x+d)-g,d><=||d||^2.\n\nTherefore F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and grad F_K=P_K is\nglobally1-Lipschitz. Also\n\n 2F_K(x)=||x||^2-||x-P_K(x)||^2.                    (2.2)\n\nIts minimum is0, and its WHOLE minimizer set is\n\n K^polar={x:<x,v><=0 for all v in K}.                (2.3)\n\nOne direction follows directly from the maximizing formula. In the other\ndirection, if <x,v>>0 for some v in K, a sufficiently small positive\nmultiple epsilon*v is in K and has positive objective. This proves\nthat x is not a minimizer. It also proves (2.3) without identifying the\nminimum with an arbitrarily selected point.\n\nWrite y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=F_K(x_i), with g_i=P_K(x_i). The original\ninterpolation convention in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nSubstitution of f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives the exact identity\n\n Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                            (2.4)\n\nThe inequality is (2.1). It holds for ALL directed pairs, however\ndistant, and with any actual minimizer sample g_*=0,f_*=0. No sampled\nvalues, remote rows, or terminal rows are fitted separately.\n\nBoth bodies below have nonempty Euclidean interior in their stated\nambient space. On their interiors P_K(x)=x, so their global smoothness\nconstant1 is sharp.\n\n## 3. The explicit growing tree and exact symmetry reduction\n\nSet\n\n r_tree=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)r_tree^j,\n w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom, 0<=j<m.                        (3.1)\n\nUse one orthonormal coordinate e_v for every binary word v of length\nless than m. For a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m) define\n\n a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))e_(s_1...s_j).\n                                                                    (3.2)\n\nThere are N leaves and N-1 coordinates. Each ||a_s||=1 and sum_s a_s=0.\nDistinct coordinate rows of the feature matrix are orthogonal: disjoint\nsupports give zero, and every proper descendant row cancels within an\nancestor's constant-sign region. Every row has positive squared norm.\nThus the matrix has rank N-1, its kernel is exactly the constant leaf\nvector, and the N features are affinely independent. In particular0 is\nin the interior of their simplex. If two leaves first diverge at depth k,\n\n <a_s,a_t>=sum_(j<k)w_j-w_k.                       (3.3)\n\nLet s_left=0^m, write a_left=a_(s_left), and let e_root=e_empty be the\nroot coordinate. The selected initial points have coordinates only on its\nspine, and possibly one common carrier. The group of tree automorphisms\nwithin every off-spine subtree acts orthogonally, fixes these points,\nand permutes the leaf features. Projection onto either selected body\ncommutes with this group by uniqueness. Every non-spine coordinate of a\nfixed vector is zero: swapping the children of its node reverses that\ncoordinate. The GD update therefore preserves this subspace at EVERY\noriginal physical query, not just at word checkpoints.\n\nAverage the features in each first-right-turn group k, 0<=k<m, and\nretain the all-left leaf as group m. Their spine vectors are\n\n b_k=sum_(j<k)sqrt(w_j)e_(0^j)-sqrt(w_k)e_(0^k), k<m,\n b_m=sum_(j<m)sqrt(w_j)e_(0^j).                     (3.4)\n\nAn off-spine group average has exactly these coordinates, since every\nlater coordinate cancels. Averaging any convex representation over the\ngroup shows that the centered simplex's fixed section is EXACTLY\nconv{b_0,...,b_m}. With a carrier, its fixed section is the convex hull\nof0 and the corresponding carrier plus each b_k.\n\nHere is an explicit constructive active-set rule, valid for every state\nin this section, including every future state below. For a finite listed\nsimplex with vertices v_k, consider a nonempty subset I of its vertices.\nChoose k0 in I, form the edge columns E_I=(v_k-v_k0)_(k in I\\{k0}),\nand set\n\n t_I=(E_I^T E_I)^(-1)E_I^T(x-v_k0),\n g_I=v_k0+E_I t_I.                                (3.5)\n\nFor a singleton take g_I=v_k0. The edge Gram matrix is positive definite\nby affine independence. Every entry is specified by (3.1),(3.4) and the\nchosen carrier; no unknown feature or Gram fitting is present. Retain a\nsubset exactly when its barycentric coefficients t_I and1-sum t_I are\nnonnegative and\n\n <x-g_I,v_l-g_I><=0 for every listed vertex l.       (3.6)\n\nAt least one subset qualifies: use the positive barycentric support of\nthe true projection. Its within-face first derivatives vanish, giving\n(3.5); its remaining inequalities are (2.1). Conversely any qualifying\nsubset gives the unique projection by (2.1). Degenerate supports can\nrepeat the same answer but cannot give different answers. Equations\n(3.5),(3.6) are a finite symbolic characterization; no enumeration or\nnumerical evaluation was run. They specify all subsequent physical GD\nqueries by x_next=x-h*g and their values by the SAME F_K. The quantitative\nconclusions below require additional analysis beyond this rule.\n\n## 4. Candidate one: a common carrier stays in a screened cone regime\n\nAdd an orthogonal unit coordinate e_car and put\n\n kappa=sqrt(w_0/3), v_s=kappa e_car+a_s,\n K_car=conv({0} union {v_s}),\n C_car={sum_s t_s v_s:t_s>=0},\n v_left=v_(s_left), x_0=v_left.                     (4.1)\n\nThe v_s are linearly independent: a zero combination first has coefficient\nsum0 by its carrier coordinate, then all coefficients zero by affine\nindependence of the a_s. Hence K_car has full dimension N and C_car is\na closed convex cone (an invertible linear image of the nonnegative\northant). A vector in C_car has a unique total mass\n\n mass(g)=g_car/kappa=sum_s t_s.\n\nIt belongs to K_car exactly when mass(g)<=1. By (2.3), the whole minimum\nset is C_car^polar. Because x_0 is in C_car, for any z in this polar\n<x_0,z><=0, and ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2. Thus0 is the actual closest\nminimum and the EXACT paid radius is\n\n R_car^2=1+kappa^2.                                 (4.2)\n\nThe first gradient is g_0=x_0, so x_1=-a x_0. Two direct continuation attempts and their limits\n\nThe first attempt was to propagate a fixed exposed face, or treat a\nvisit to the quadratic core as permanent. Both shortcuts fail on these\nvery data. Equations(6.3),(6.5) give different positive supports at\nqueries1 and2. No remote interpolation defect occurs: (2.4)\nholds, and the true projection supplies the required repair automatically.\n\nThe second attempt was to simplify the full simplex projection by\nthresholding its unconstrained leaf masses with the ordinary Euclidean\nprobability-simplex formula. The inverse feature map changes the Euclidean metric; ordinary\nthresholding is not invariant under that change of coordinates.\n\nThese are concrete failures of two attempted reductions, not a proof\nthat a competitive centered-tree trajectory is impossible. The sharp global smoothness of\neach selected F_K is1, so no smaller local constant is substituted.\n\nThe accepted reference facts used are the following.\n\n* b98bb807d514e80d: the COMPLETE single-cut functional\n\n K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S), max_(0<=j<n)\n  (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]}.\n\n  It also specifies the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_t=max_(0<=k<t)Omega(C_k,C_(t-k-1)),\n B_t=max_(0<=k<t)G(C_k,B_(t-k-1)),\n Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n U_t^ZJ=1/B_t.\n\n* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: F_ray_n(h) is its maximum over ALL feasible\n  chronological checkpoint subsets, with the original retained offsets,\n  value areas, same-ray monotonicity conditions and terminal q optimization.\n  Its complete class contains K_n(h). Hence\n\n Cfull(h)>=Gap_h(1)=Qunit.
