Its full\n  statement and the proof's indices were read directly from the fact\n  file. No new recurrence computation or finite-H6 transfer is used.\n\nThus Qunit<c^(-2m)<1/(2c^m)<Huber(h), by (1.1) and c^m>2.\n\n## 2. Projection function, smoothness, minimum, and all original rows\n\nLet K be any nonempty compact convex subset of a Euclidean space with\n0 in K. Define the SAME finite function at every query by\n\n F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nCompleting the square shows that its unique maximizing vector is the\nEuclidean projection g=P_K(x). Existence follows from compactness and\nuniqueness from strict convexity of squared distance. The exact normal\ncondition is\n\n <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in K.                     (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance on the segment\nfrom g to v. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2-||x-g||^2.\nAdding (2.1) for two inputs proves\n<x-x',g-g'>=||g-g'||^2, hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||.\nThe maximizing formulas at x and x+d give\n\n 0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<g,d><=<P_K(x+d)-g,d><=||d||^2.\n\nTherefore F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and grad F_K=P_K is\nglobally1-Lipschitz. Also\n\n 2F_K(x)=||x||^2-||x-P_K(x)||^2.                    (2.2)\n\nIts minimum is0, and its WHOLE minimizer set is\n\n K^polar={x:<x,v><=0 for all v in K}.                (2.3)\n\nOne direction follows directly from the maximizing formula. In the other\ndirection, if <x,v>>0 for some v in K, a sufficiently small positive\nmultiple epsilon*v is in K and has positive objective. This proves\nthat x is not a minimizer. It also proves (2.3) without identifying the\nminimum with an arbitrarily selected point.\n\nWrite y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=F_K(x_i), with g_i=P_K(x_i). The original\ninterpolation convention in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nSubstitution of f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives the exact identity\n\n Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                            (2.4)\n\nThe inequality is (2.1). It holds for ALL directed pairs, however\ndistant, and with any actual minimizer sample g_*=0,f_*=0. No sampled\nvalues, remote rows, or terminal rows are fitted separately.\n\nBoth bodies below have nonempty Euclidean interior in their stated\nambient space. On their interiors P_K(x)=x, so their global smoothness\nconstant1 is sharp.\n\n## 3. The explicit growing tree and exact symmetry reduction\n\nSet\n\n r_tree=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)r_tree^j,\n w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom, 0<=j<m.                        (3.1)\n\nUse one orthonormal coordinate e_v for every binary word v of length\nless than m. For a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m) define\n\n a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))e_(s_1...s_j).\n                                                                    (3.2)\n\nThere are N leaves and N-1 coordinates. Each ||a_s||=1 and sum_s a_s=0.\nDistinct coordinate rows of the feature matrix are orthogonal: disjoint\nsupports give zero, and every proper descendant row cancels within an\nancestor's constant-sign region. Every row has positive squared norm.\nThus the matrix has rank N-1, its kernel is exactly the constant leaf\nvector, and the N features are affinely independent. In particular0 is\nin the interior of their simplex. If two leaves first diverge at depth k,\n\n <a_s,a_t>=sum_(j<k)w_j-w_k.                       (3.3)\n\nLet s_left=0^m, write a_left=a_(s_left), and let e_root=e_empty be the\nroot coordinate. The selected initial points have coordinates only on its\nspine, and possibly one common carrier. The group of tree automorphisms\nwithin every off-spine subtree acts orthogonally, fixes these points,\nand permutes the leaf features. Projection onto either selected body\ncommutes with this group by uniqueness. Every non-spine coordinate of a\nfixed vector is zero: swapping the children of its node reverses that\ncoordinate. The GD update therefore preserves this subspace at EVERY\noriginal physical query, not just at word checkpoints.\n\nAverage the features in each first-right-turn group k, 0<=k<m, and\nretain the all-left leaf as group m. Their spine vectors are\n\n b_k=sum_(j<k)sqrt(w_j)e_(0^j)-sqrt(w_k)e_(0^k), k<m,\n b_m=sum_(j<m)sqrt(w_j)e_(0^j).                     (3.4)\n\nAn off-spine group average has exactly these coordinates, since every\nlater coordinate cancels. Averaging any convex representation over the\ngroup shows that the centered simplex's fixed section is EXACTLY\nconv{b_0,...,b_m}. With a carrier, its fixed section is the convex hull\nof0 and the corresponding carrier plus each b_k.\n\nHere is an explicit constructive active-set rule, valid for every state\nin this section, including every future state below. For a finite listed\nsimplex with vertices v_k, consider a nonempty subset I of its vertices.\nChoose k0 in I, form the edge columns E_I=(v_k-v_k0)_(k in I\\{k0}),\nand set\n\n t_I=(E_I^T E_I)^(-1)E_I^T(x-v_k0),\n g_I=v_k0+E_I t_I.                                (3.5)\n\nFor a singleton take g_I=v_k0. The edge Gram matrix is positive definite\nby affine independence. Every entry is specified by (3.1),(3.4) and the\nchosen carrier; no unknown feature or Gram fitting is present. Retain a\nsubset exactly when its barycentric coefficients t_I and1-sum t_I are\nnonnegative and\n\n <x-g_I,v_l-g_I><=0 for every listed vertex l.       (3.6)\n\nAt least one subset qualifies: use the positive barycentric support of\nthe true projection. Its within-face first derivatives vanish, giving\n(3.5); its remaining inequalities are (2.1). Conversely any qualifying\nsubset gives the unique projection by (2.1). Degenerate supports can\nrepeat the same answer but cannot give different answers. Equations\n(3.5),(3.6) are a finite symbolic characterization; no enumeration or\nnumerical evaluation was run. They specify all subsequent physical GD\nqueries by x_next=x-h*g and their values by the SAME F_K. The quantitative\nconclusions below require additional analysis beyond this rule.\n\n## 4. Candidate one: a common carrier stays in a screened cone regime\n\nAdd an orthogonal unit coordinate e_car and put\n\n kappa=sqrt(w_0/3), v_s=kappa e_car+a_s,\n K_car=conv({0} union {v_s}),\n C_car={sum_s t_s v_s:t_s>=0},\n v_left=v_(s_left), x_0=v_left.                     (4.1)\n\nThe v_s are linearly independent: a zero combination first has coefficient\nsum0 by its carrier coordinate, then all coefficients zero by affine\nindependence of the a_s. Hence K_car has full dimension N and C_car is\na closed convex cone (an invertible linear image of the nonnegative\northant). A vector in C_car has a unique total mass\n\n mass(g)=g_car/kappa=sum_s t_s.\n\nIt belongs to K_car exactly when mass(g)<=1. By (2.3), the whole minimum\nset is C_car^polar. Because x_0 is in C_car, for any z in this polar\n<x_0,z><=0, and ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2. Thus0 is the actual closest\nminimum and the EXACT paid radius is\n\n R_car^2=1+kappa^2.                                 (4.2)\n\nThe first gradient is g_0=x_0, so x_1=-a x_0. Define the uniform\nright-root subtree average\n\n u_R=kappa e_car-sqrt(w_0)e_root.\n\nThe actual next gradient is\n\n g_1=(a/2)u_R,      ||g_1||=a*kappa.                 (4.3)\n\nHere is a full normal check. Set y_1=x_1-g_1. For every leaf whose\nfirst bit is1, <y_1,v_s>=0, because\n<v_left,v_s>=-2w_0/3 and <u_R,v_s>=4w_0/3. For a different leaf\nfirst diverging from s_left at depth k>=1,\n\n <y_1,v_s>=-a(sum_(j<k)w_j-w_k)<0,\n\nsince w_k<=w_1<w_0. For s=s_left the inner product is -a<0.\nThus y_1 is in C_car^polar and <y_1,g_1>=0. For any v in C_car,\n<y_1,v-g_1><=0, proving P_C_car(x_1)=g_1. Moreover its mass is\na/2<1, so (4.3) is also P_K_car(x_1). The mixture is origin plus\nALL right-root leaves, uniformly within that subtree. It is derived,\nnot prescribed. The noncore state y_1 is nonzero; for example every\ndeeper left-spine coordinate is -a sqrt(w_j).\n\nWe reproduce the precise cone mapping needed for the full word, rather\nthan using the earlier unverified C37 finding as a premise. For any\nclosed convex cone C and u=P_C(x), put z=x-u. Applying (2.1) to0 and2u\ngives <z,u>=0 and z in C^polar. Conversely\n<u,w-z><=0 for all w in C^polar, so z=P_(C^polar)(x). Therefore\n\n ||P_C(x)||=dist(x,C^polar),\n F_C(x)=||P_C(x)||^2/2,\n dist(x-hP_C(x),C^polar)<=|1-h|dist(x,C^polar).      (4.4)\n\nThe last bound uses the same old point z in the next distance minimization,\nsince x-hu=z+(1-h)u. The cone maximum defining F_C is finite by completing\nthe square, even though C itself is unbounded.\n\nRun the cone dynamics from the actual x_1 and denote its projection norms\nby d_i. By (4.3),(4.4),(1.2), for every i>=1,\n\n d_i<=a*kappa*product_(1<=t<i)|1-h_t|\n     =kappa |Pi_i|<=a*kappa<kappa.                  (4.5)\n\nEach cone projection has mass at most d_i/kappa<1. It is therefore in\nK_car and is ALSO its projection. Induction identifies the entire cone\ntrajectory with the entire actual bounded-body trajectory. In particular\nevery original physical query and the terminal value come from the SAME\nF_K_car. No changed function is substituted after query1. Formula (3.5)\nwith the carrier vertices, or the proved cone normal condition, selects\nall faces without any cancellation/extinction requirement.\n\nThis trajectory has another genuine face change within the first H2.\nAt x_2=x_1-(1+beta)g_1, every right-root leaf has inner product\n-2a beta w_0/3<0, whereas a leaf first diverging at depth1 has\n\n <x_2,v_s>=a w_0(r_tree-1+beta/3)>0.               (4.6)\n\nFor the last strict inequality it suffices to prove\n6m^2>lambda(2m+1)(m+1). Using lambda<5/2 reduces this to\nm^2>(15/2)m+5/2, which holds at m=8 and increases thereafter.\nSome small positive multiple of this left vertex has positive objective,\nso g_2 is nonzero. A mixture supported solely on the right-root leaves\nand origin has nonpositive linear term and cannot attain this positive\nobjective. Thus g_2 has positive left-root mass. No assertion is made\nthat the right-root mass vanishes or that a fixed next face persists.\n\nDespite these changing mixtures, (4.5) yields a COMPLETE full-word screen:\n\n 0<=V_car<=kappa^2/(1+kappa^2)*Qunit\n          =w_0/(3+w_0)*beta^(2n)c^(-2m).            (4.7)\n\nSince 0<w_0/(3+w_0)<1, equations(1.5),(1.6) give the strict SAME-word\ncomparisons\n\n V_car<Qunit<Huber(h)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h),\n V_car<Cfull(h),\n B_n V_car<=4*w_0/(3+w_0)*(lambda/c^2)^m\n                      *beta^(2n) ->0. At query1 the\nfinite boundary really matters, as demonstrated below, so the cone\nmapping from Section4 cannot be carried over.\n\nWrite the actual spine coordinates as x_(i,j)=sqrt(w_j) z_(i,j).\nIf p_(i,k) are the unique barycentric masses of g_i in the vertices b_k,\ndefine q_(i,j)=sum_(k>=j)p_(i,k). Then q_(i,0)=1 and\n1=q_(i,0)>=q_(i,1)>=...>=q_(i,m)>=0, and\n\n d_(i,j)=2q_(i,j+1)-q_(i,j),\n g_(i,j)=sqrt(w_j)d_(i,j),\n z_(i+1,j)=z_(i,j)-h_i d_(i,j), z_(0,j)=1.          (5.3)\n\nAt every query the masses are exactly selected by (3.5),(3.6) with b_k.\nEquivalently they uniquely minimize\nsum_(j<m)w_j[z_(i,j)-(2q_(j+1)-q_j)]^2 on the displayed monotone\nq simplex. The constructive rule, not this optimization notation alone,\nspecifies the answer. The exact values and normalized terminal score are\n\n F_K_ctr(x_i)=sum_(j<m)w_j[z_(i,j)d_(i,j)-d_(i,j)^2/2],\n V_ctr=sum_(j<m)w_j[2z_(n,j)d_(n,j)-d_(n,j)^2].      (5.4)\n\nThese are a persistent m-dimensional state and every original physical\nstep. There is no smaller reset state or new child clock.\n\n## 6. Actual first-H2 projections for the centered body\n\nDefine for positive integers J\n\n Dsp_J=sum_(j=0)^(J-1)(2c)^j,\n sigma_3=(7a-1)/Dsp_3,\n sigma_4=(15a-2-beta)/Dsp_4.                        (6.1)\n\nFor m>=128 the elementary bounds\n\n 2/5<a<5/12, 12/5<c<5/2, 127/128<=beta<1,\n 1/20<sigma_3<1/15, 1/52<sigma_4<1/40              (6.2)\n\nhold. The bounds on a,c follow from 12/5<lambda<29/12 and m>=128.\nFor sigma_3 its numerator is between9/5 and23/12, and its denominator\nis between721/25 and31; in particular\n9/155>1/20 and (23/12)/(721/25)=575/8652<1/15.\nFor sigma_4 the numerator is greater than3 and less than10/3; its\ndenominator is less than156 and greater than17429/125>400/3.\n\nInitially g_0=x_0 and z_(1,j)=-a for EVERY depth. The exact projection\nat query1 is\n\n d_(1,j)=-a+sigma_3 c^j, j=0,1,2,\n d_(1,j)=0, j>=3.                                  (6.3)\n\nIts positive masses are exactly exit groups0,1,2. Moreover\n\n 7/24<q_(1,1)<1/3, 0<q_(1,2)<1/20.\n\nFor the second bounds its numerator over Dsp_3 is\na(1+2c)+(1-3a)c^2. Because r_tree*c=2, its inner product with b_0,b_1,b_2\nis the common value w_0 sigma_3. Its inner product with b_3 is\nw_0[-7sigma_3+a r_tree^3]. This is strictly smaller, because\nsigma_3>a/c^3: the left side exceeds1/20 and a/c^3<1/30.\nFor subsequent first-exit vertices the residual inner products strictly\ndecrease: an increment inside the constant negative tail is\n-a w_k(2-r_tree)<0. The final all-left vertex also has a smaller value\nthan its immediately preceding exit vertex, by -2a w_(m-1)<0.\nThus every normal condition(3.6) holds and the displayed projection is\nthe actual unique one.\n\nThe next ACTUAL state, after the original h_1=1+beta, is\n\n z_(2,j)=a beta-(1+beta)sigma_3 c^j, j=0,1,2,\n z_(2,j)=-a, j>=3. Define earlier q by\nq_(2,0)=1 and q_(2,j+1)=(q_(2,j)+d_(2,j))/2.\nAll four barycentric masses are strictly positive, as follows.\nThe bounds(6.2) give\n\n 1/4<d_(2,0)<1/2, 0<d_(2,1)<1/4,\n d_(2,2)=-a+(1+beta)q_(1,2)+sigma_4 c^2<-23/160<0,\n d_(2,3)=-a+sigma_4 c^3<-3/320<0.\n\nFor completeness, the lower bounds on the first two entries follow\nfrom 127/320-2/15>1/4 and127/320-1/3>0. Its values on the four active vertices are all\nw_0 sigma_4. The first inactive normal inequality is exactly\nsigma_4>=a/c^4, which is strict because sigma_4>1/52 and\na/c^4<1/75. The negative tail then makes all further normal values\nstrictly decrease, with the same last-leaf check. This proves(6.5).\n\nThe common active normal values also give the exact actual conjugate\nexcesses cex_1=<y_1,g_1>=w_0 sigma_3>0 and\ncex_2=<y_2,g_2>=w_0 sigma_4>0. Two direct continuation attempts and their limits\n\nThe first attempt was to propagate a fixed exposed face, or treat a\nvisit to the quadratic core as permanent. Both shortcuts fail on these\nvery data. Equations(6.3),(6.5) give different positive supports at\nqueries1 and2. Also x_0 lies in K_ctr, but x_1=-a a_left does NOT.\nIts unique full-leaf barycentric coefficients are\n\n p_s=(1+a)/N for s!=s_left,\n p_(s_left)=(1+a)/N-a<0.                            (7.1)\n\nThe strict inequality follows from a>2/5 and N>=4. Consequently\nP_K_ctr(x_1)!=x_1. An initial core visit already fails to persist through\nthe next original step. No remote interpolation defect occurs: (2.4)\nholds, and the true projection supplies the required repair automatically.\n\nThe second attempt was to simplify the full simplex projection by\nthresholding its unconstrained leaf masses with the ordinary Euclidean\nprobability-simplex formula. This is false for the selected Haar metric.\nApplying that threshold formula to (7.1) would give mass1/(N-1) on all\nnon-left leaves, mass0 on the left leaf, and hence\n\n g_wrong=-a_left/(N-1).\n\nAll those non-left leaves would have positive weights, so their normal\ninner products with y_wrong=x_1-g_wrong must be identical at a genuine\nprojection. This directly violates the within-face stationarity\ncondition. The inverse feature map changes the Euclidean metric; ordinary\nthresholding is not invariant under that change of coordinates.\n\nThese are concrete failures of two attempted reductions, not a proof\nthat a competitive centered-tree trajectory is impossible. In particular\nthere is no proof that the support remains a prefix of exit groups after\nquery2, and no failure of such a later-only prefix conjecture is asserted.\nThe full state rule retains arbitrary later supports.\n\n## 8.
